The MNEE Hackathon: Programmable Money for Agents, Commerce, and Automated Finance invites developers, builders, and innovators to shape the next era of digital commerce and autonomous coordination.

Money is becoming programmable — not just for humans, but for AI agents, online communities, businesses, and decentralized networks. This hackathon challenges teams to build real applications where value moves automatically, transparently, and reliably.

Using MNEE, a USD-backed stablecoin live on Ethereum, participants will create systems that enable:

AI agents to transact and pay for services autonomously

Creators and businesses to accept stablecoin payments

Smart contracts to automate financial workflows and settlements

The goal: Design the future of commerce infrastructure — stable, programmable, and accessible to everyone.

Whether you’re an AI engineer, web3 developer, fintech builder, or designer shaping user experiences, this hackathon is your platform to build tools that power the next generation of economic interactions.

Learn more at https://mnee.io

Requirements
To qualify, your project must use MNEE, the USD-backed stablecoin on Ethereum, and demonstrate how programmable money can power new forms of digital commerce, AI automation, or financial coordination.

 

🛠️ What to Build
Create a working prototype or demo in one of the core tracks built on the following contract: 0x8ccedbAe4916b79da7F3F612EfB2EB93A2bFD6cF:

AI & Agent Payments – agents or automated systems paying for services or data

Commerce & Creator Tools – checkout, paywalls, or payout systems using MNEE stablecoin

Financial Automation – programmable invoicing, escrow, or treasury management

 

😎 Functionality
The Project must be capable of being successfully installed and running consistently on the platform for which it is intended and must function as depicted in the video and/or expressed in the text description.

 

📦 What to Submit on Devpost
Each submission must include:

Project Description – clear explanation of features, purpose, and use of MNEE.

Demo Video – show your project running and explain how it works in up to 5 minutes.

Working Demo or Live URL if applicable – web app, mobile app, or test environment.

Public Code Repository – include source code, setup instructions, and an open-source license.

All submissions must be in English and publicly accessible for judging and testing.

Judging Criteria
Technological Implementation
How well does the solution work? Quality of engineering, smart contract logic, security, stability, and use of MNEE in a meaningful way.
Design & User Experience
Is the product intuitive and well-designed? Clear UX, usability, user flow, and overall polish of the experience.
Impact Potential
Could this project make a real difference? Practical utility, potential users, scalability, ability to be adopted in the real world.
Originality & Quality of Idea
Is the concept fresh and compelling? Creativity, problem-solving, and whether the idea stands out from existing solutions.
Solves Real Coordination Problems
Does it meaningfully improve collective decision-making or economic coordination? Examples: treasury transparency, shared budgeting, automated governance, agent-to-agent commerce.https://mnee-eth.devpost.com/resources

MNEE Hackathon: Programmable Money for Agents, Creators and Automated Finance (“Official Rules”)
NO PURCHASE OR PAYMENT NECESSARY TO ENTER OR WIN. A PURCHASE OR PAYMENT WILL NOT INCREASE YOUR CHANCES OF WINNING. 

SUBMISSION OF ANY ENTRY CONSTITUTES AGREEMENT TO THESE OFFICIAL RULES AS A CONTRACT BETWEEN ENTRANT (AND EACH INDIVIDUAL MEMBER OF ENTRANT), THE HACKATHON SPONSOR, AND DEVPOST.

 

1. Dates and Timing
Registration Period: November 17, 2025 (8:00  am Eastern Time) – January 12, 2026 (5:00 pm Eastern Time) (“Registration Period”).

Submission Period: November 17, 2025 (8:00 am Eastern Time) – January 12, 2026 (5:00 pm Eastern Time) (“Submission Period”).

Judging Period: January 12, 2026 (8:00 am Eastern Time) – January 29, 2026 (5:00 pm Eastern Time) (“Judging Period”).

Winners Announced: On or around February 17, 2026 (2:00 pm Eastern Time).

 

2. Sponsor and Administrator
Sponsor: MNEE, LLC, 3411 Silverside Rd, DE Unit 249, Wilmington, Delaware, PENN 19810, United States

Administrator: Devpost, Inc. (“Devpost”), 222 Broadway, Floor 19, New York, NY 10038

 

3. Eligibility
The Hackathon IS open to: 

Individuals who are at least the age of majority where they reside as of the time of entry (“Eligible Individuals”);

Teams of Eligible Individuals (“Teams”); and

Organizations (including corporations, not-for-profit corporations and other nonprofit organizations, limited liability companies, partnerships, and other legal entities) that exist and have been organized or incorporated at the time of entry.

(the above are collectively, “Entrants”)

An Eligible Individual may join more than one Team or Organization and an Eligible Individual who is part of a Team or Organization may also enter the Hackathon on an individual basis. If a Team or Organization is entering the Hackathon, they must appoint and authorize one individual (the “Representative”) to represent, act, and enter a Submission, on their behalf. By entering a Submission on behalf of a Team or Organization you represent and warrant that you are the Representative authorized to act on behalf of your Team or Organization.

The Hackathon IS NOT open to: 

Individuals who are residents of, or Organizations domiciled in, a country, state, province or territory where the laws of the United States or local law prohibits participating or receiving a prize in the Hackathon (including, but not limited to, Brazil, Quebec, Russia, Crimea, Cuba, Iran, North Korea, Syria and any other country designated by the United States Treasury's Office of Foreign Assets Control) 

Organizations involved with the design, production, paid promotion, execution, or distribution of the Hackathon, including the Sponsor and Administrator (“Promotion Entities”).

Employees, representatives and agents** of such Promotion Entities, and all members of their immediate family or household*

Any other individual involved with the design, production, promotion, execution, or distribution of the Hackathon, and each member of their immediate family or household*

Any Judge (defined below), or company or individual that employs a Judge

Any parent company, subsidiary, or other affiliate*** of any organization described above

Any other individual or organization whose participation in the Hackathon would create, in the sole discretion of the Sponsor and/or Administrator, a real or apparent conflict of interest 

*The members of an individual’s immediate family include the individual’s spouse, children and stepchildren, parents and stepparents, and siblings and stepsiblings. The members of an individual’s household include any other person that shares the same residence as the individual for at least three (3) months out of the year. 

**Agents include individuals or organizations that in creating a Submission to the Hackathon, are acting on behalf of, and at the direction of, a Promotion Entity through a contractual or similar relationship.

***An affiliate is: (a) an organization that is under common control, sharing a common majority or controlling owner, or common management; or (b) an organization that has a substantial ownership in, or is substantially owned by the other organization.

 

4. How To Enter 
Entrants may enter by visiting mnee-eth.devpost.com (“Hackathon Website”) and follow the below steps:

Register for the Hackathon on the Hackathon Website by clicking the “Join Hackathon” button. To complete registration, sign up to create a free Devpost account, or log in with an existing Devpost account. This will enable you to receive important updates and to create your Submission.

Entry in the Hackathon constitutes consent for the Sponsor and Devpost to collect and maintain an Entrant’s personal information for the purpose of operating and publicizing the Hackathon.

Complete and enter all of the required fields on the “Enter a Submission” page of the Hackathon Website (each a “Submission”) during the Submission Period and follow the requirements below.

Project Requirements
What to Create: Entrants must build a working prototype or demo that uses MNEE on the following contract (0x8ccedbAe4916b79da7F3F612EfB2EB93A2bFD6cF) in one of the following categories: 

AI & Agent Payments – agents or automated systems paying for services or data

Commerce & Creator Tools – checkout, paywalls, or payout systems using stablecoins

Financial Automation – programmable invoicing, escrow, or treasury management (each a “Project”). 

Functionality: The Project must be capable of being successfully installed and running consistently on the platform for which it is intended and must function as depicted in the video and/or expressed in the text description.

Platforms: A submitted Project must run on the platform for which it is intended and which is specified in the Submission Requirements. 

New & Existing Projects Only: Projects must be either newly created by the Entrant during the Hackathon Submission Period or, if it existed prior to the Hackathon Submission Period, only meaningful new functionality to be added during the hackathon (e.g., MNEE integration)

Third-Party Integrations: If a Project integrates any third-party SDK, APIs and/or data, Entrant must be authorized to use them in accordance with any terms and conditions or licensing requirements of the tool.

Submission Requirements 
Submissions to the Hackathon must meet the following requirements:

Include a Project built with the required developer tools and meets the above Project Requirements.

Indicate the best (select one) track that fits your project: 

AI & Agent Payments – agents or automated systems paying for services or data

Commerce & Creator Tools – checkout, paywalls, or payout systems using stablecoins

Financial Automation – programmable invoicing, escrow, or treasury management

Include a text description that should explain the features, purpose and use of MNEEin your Project.

Include a demonstration video of your Project. The video portion of the Submission:

should be up to (5) minutes. Judges are not required to watch beyond five minutes 

should include footage that shows the Project functioning on the device for which it was built

must be uploaded to and made publicly visible on YouTube, Vimeo, or and a link to the video must be provided on the Submission form on the Hackathon Website; and

must not include third party trademarks, or copyrighted music or other material unless the Entrant has permission to use such material.

Include a URL to a functional demo app and (or provide apk file if on Android)

Include a URL to a public and open source code repository. The repository must contain a README, and all necessary source code, assets, and instructions required for the project to be functional. The repository must be public and open source by including an open source license file. This license should be detectable and visible at the top of the repository page (in the About section).  

Indicate any third-party APIs or SDKs used. Note: you must have permission/license to use them.

Multiple Submissions 

An Entrant may submit more than one Submission, however, each Submission must be unique and substantially different from each of the Entrant’s other Submissions, as determined by the Sponsor and Devpost in their sole discretion.

Submission ownership 

Be the original work of the Entrant, be solely owned by the Entrant, and not violate the IP rights of any other person or entity.

Testing 

Access must be provided to an Entrant’s working Project for judging and testing by providing a link to a website, functioning demo, or a test build. If Entrant’s website is private, Entrant must include login credentials in its testing instructions. The Entrant must make the Project available free of charge and without any restriction, for testing, evaluation and use by the Sponsor, Administrator and Judges until the Judging Period ends. Judges are not required to test the Project and may choose to judge based solely on the text description, images, and video provided in the Submission.

If the Project includes software that runs on proprietary or third party hardware that is not widely available to the public, including software running on devices or wearable technology other than smartphones, tablets, or desktop computers, the Sponsor and/or Administrator reserve the right, at their sole discretion, to require the Entrant to provide physical access to the Project hardware upon request.  

Language Requirements

All Submission materials must be in English or, if not in English, the Entrant must provide an English translation of the demonstration video, text description, and testing instructions as well as all other materials submitted. 

Team Representation

If a team or organization is entering the Hackathon, they must appoint and authorize one individual (the “Representative”) to represent, act, and enter a Submission, on their behalf. The Representative must meet the eligibility requirements above. By entering a Submission on the Hackathon Website on behalf of a team or organization you represent and warrant that you are the Representative authorized to act on behalf of your team or organization.

Intellectual Property 

Your Submission must: (a) be your (or your Team, or Organization’s) original work product; (b) be solely owned by you, your Team, your Organization with no other person or entity having any right or interest in it; and (c) not violate the intellectual property rights or other rights including but not limited to copyright, trademark, patent, contract, and/or privacy rights, of any other person or entity. An Entrant may contract with a third party for technical assistance to create the Submission provided the Submission components are solely the Entrant’s work product and the result of the Entrant’s ideas and creativity, and the Entrant owns all rights to them. An Entrant may submit a Submission that includes the use of open source software or hardware, provided the Entrant complies with applicable open source licenses and, as part of the Submission, creates software that enhances and builds upon the features and functionality included in the underlying open source product. By entering the Hackathon, you represent, warrant, and agree that your Submission meets these requirements.

Financial or Preferential Support 

A Project must not have been developed, or derived from a Project developed, with financial or preferential support from the Sponsor or Administrator. Such Projects include, but are not limited to, those that received funding or investment for their development, were developed under contract, or received a commercial license, from the Sponsor or Administrator any time prior to the end of Hackathon Submission Period. The Sponsor, at their sole discretion, may disqualify a Project, if awarding a prize to the Project would create a real or apparent conflict of interest.

 

5. Submission Modifications
Draft Submissions 

Prior to the end of the Submission Period, you may save draft versions of your Submission on Devpost to your portfolio before submitting the submission materials to the Hackathon for evaluation. Once the Submission Period has ended, you may not make any changes or alterations to your Submission, but you may continue to update the Project in your Devpost portfolio.

Modifications 

After the Submission Period. The Sponsor and Devpost may permit you to modify part of your Submission after the Submission Period for the purpose of adding, removing or replacing material that potentially infringes a third party mark or right, discloses personally identifiable information, or is otherwise inappropriate. The modified Submission must remain substantively the same as the original Submission with the only modification being what the Sponsor and Devpost permits. 

 

6. Judges & Criteria
Eligible Submissions will be evaluated by a panel of judges selected by the Sponsor (the “Judges”). Judges may be employees of the Sponsor or third parties, may or may not be listed individually on the Hackathon Website, and may change before or during the Judging Period. Judging may take place in one or more rounds with one or more panels of Judges, at the discretion of the Sponsor. 

Stage One) The first stage will determine via pass/fail whether the ideas meet a baseline level of viability, in that the Project reasonably fits the theme and reasonably applies the required APIs/SDKs featured in the Hackathon.

Stage Two) All Submissions that pass Stage One will be evaluated in Stage Two based on the following equally weighted criteria (the “Judging Criteria”):

Entries will be judged on the following equally weighted criteria, and according to the sole and absolute discretion of the judges:

Technological Implementation

How well does the solution work? Quality of engineering, smart contract logic, security, stability, and use of MNEE in a meaningful way.

Design & User Experience

Is the product intuitive and well-designed? Clear UX, usability, user flow, and overall polish of the experience.

Impact Potential

Could this project make a real difference? Practical utility, potential users, scalability, ability to be adopted in the real world.

Originality & Quality of the Idea

Is the concept fresh and compelling? Creativity, problem-solving, and whether the idea stands out from existing solutions

Solves Real Coordination Problems
Does it meaningfully improve collective decision-making or economic coordination? Examples: treasury transparency, shared budgeting, automated governance, agent-to-agent commerce.

The scores from the Judges will determine the potential winners of the applicable prizes. The Entrant(s) that are eligible for a Prize, and whose Submissions earn the highest overall scores based on the applicable Judging Criteria, will become potential winners of that Prize.

Tie Breaking 

For each Prize listed below, if two or more Submissions are tied, the tied Submission with the highest score in the first applicable criterion listed above will be considered the higher scoring Submission. In the event any ties remain, this process will be repeated, as needed, by comparing the tied Submissions’ scores on the next applicable criterion. If two or more Submissions are tied on all applicable criteria, the panel of Judges will vote on the tied Submissions.

 

7. Intellectual Property Rights
All Submissions remain the intellectual property of the individuals or organizations that developed them. By submitting an entry, entrants agree that the Sponsor will have a fully paid, non-exclusive license to use such entry for judging the entry. Entrants agree that the Sponsor and Devpost shall have the right to promote the Submission and use the name, likeness, voice and image of all individuals contributing to a Submission, in any materials promoting or publicizing the Hackathon and its results, during the Hackathon Period and for three years thereafter.  Some Submission components may be displayed to the public. Other Submission materials may be viewed by the sponsor, Devpost, and judges for screening and evaluation. By submitting an entry or accepting any prize, entrants represent and warrant that (a) submitted content is not copyrighted, protected by trade secret or otherwise subject to third party intellectual property rights or other proprietary rights, including privacy and publicity rights, unless entrant is the owner of such rights or has permission from their rightful owner to post the content; and (b) the content submitted does not contain any viruses, Trojan horses, worms, spyware or other disabling devices or harmful or malicious code.

Participants should inform themselves as to the legal requirements applicable to them in respect of the acquisition, holding and disposition of cryptocurrency and upon delivery, and as to the income and other tax consequences to them of such acquisition, holding and disposition.

Substitutions & Changes: Prizes are non-transferable by the winner. Sponsor in its sole discretion has the right to make a prize substitution of equivalent or greater value. Sponsor will not award a prize if there are no eligible Submissions entered in the Hackathon, or if there are no eligible Entrants or Submissions for a specific prize.

Verification Requirement: THE AWARD OF A PRIZE TO A POTENTIAL WINNER IS SUBJECT TO VERIFICATION OF THE IDENTITY, QUALIFICATIONS AND ROLE OF THE POTENTIAL WINNER IN THE CREATION OF THE SUBMISSION. No Submission or Entrant shall be deemed a winning Submission or winner until their post-competition prize affidavits have been completed and verified, even if prospective winners have been announced verbally or on the competition website. The final decision to designate a winner shall be made by the Sponsor and/or Administrator. Deadline for Returning Required Forms: ten (10) business days after the Required Forms are sent. Failure to return the required forms may result in disqualification. Failure of the winner to return the paperwork in more than three months will result in forfeiture of the prize.

Prize Delivery: Prizes will be payable to the Entrant, if an individual; to the Entrant’s Representative, if a Team; or to the Organization, if the Entrant is an Organization. It will be the responsibility of the winning Entrant’s Representative to allocate the Prize among their Team or Organization’s participating members, as the Representative deems appropriate. A monetary Prize will be mailed to the winning Entrant’s address (if an individual) or the Representative’s address (if a Team or Organization), or sent electronically to the Entrant, Entrant’s Representative, or Organization’s bank account, only after receipt of the completed winner affidavit and other required forms (collectively the “Required Forms”), if applicable. The deadline for returning the Required Forms to the Administrator is ten (10) business days after the Required Forms are sent. Failure to provide correct information on the Required Forms, or other correct information required for the delivery of a Prize, may result in delayed Prize delivery, disqualification of the Entrant, or forfeiture of a Prize. Prizes will be delivered within 60 days of the Sponsor or Devpost’s receipt of the completed Required Forms.

Fees & Taxes: Winners (and in the case of Team or Organization, all participating members) are responsible for any fees associated with receiving or using a prize, including but not limited to, wiring fees or currency exchange fees. Winners (and in the case of Team or Organization, all participating members) are responsible for reporting and paying all applicable taxes in their jurisdiction of residence (federal, state/provincial/territorial and local). Winners may be required to provide certain information to facilitate receipt of the award, including completing and submitting any tax or other forms necessary for compliance with applicable withholding and reporting requirements. United States residents may be required to provide a completed form W-9 and residents of other countries may be required to provide a completed W-8BEN form. Winners are also responsible for complying with foreign exchange and banking regulations in their respective jurisdictions and reporting the receipt of the Prize to relevant government departments/agencies, if necessary. The Sponsor, Devpost, and/or Prize provider reserves the right to withhold a portion of the prize amount to comply with the tax laws of the United States or other Sponsor jurisdiction, or those of a winner’s jurisdiction.

 

9. Entry Conditions and Release
By entering the Hackathon, you (and, if you are entering on behalf of a Team, Organization each participating members) agree(s) to the following:

The relationship between you, the Entrant, and the Sponsor and Administrator, is not a confidential, fiduciary, or other special relationship.

You will be bound by and comply with these Official Rules and the decisions of the Sponsor, Administrator, and/or the Hackathon Judges which are binding and final in all matters relating to the Hackathon.

You release, indemnify, defend and hold harmless the Promotion Entities, and their respective parent, subsidiary, and affiliated companies, the Prize suppliers and any other organizations responsible for sponsoring, fulfilling, administering, advertising or promoting the Hackathon, and all of their respective past and present officers, directors, employees, agents and representatives (hereafter the “Released Parties”) from and against any and all claims, expenses, and liabilities (including reasonable attorneys’ fees), including but not limited to negligence and damages of any kind to persons and property, defamation, slander, libel, violation of right of publicity, infringement of trademark, copyright or other intellectual property rights, property damage, or death or personal injury arising out of or relating to an Entrant's entry, creation of Submission or entry of a Submission, participation in the Hackathon, acceptance or use or misuse of the Prize (including any travel or activity related thereto) and/or the broadcast, transmission, performance, exploitation or use of the Submission as authorized or licensed by these Official Rules. 

Without limiting the foregoing, the Released Parties shall have no liability in connection with: 

Any incorrect or inaccurate information, whether caused by the Sponsor or Administrator’s electronic or printing error, or by any of the equipment or programming associated with or utilized in the Hackathon; 

Technical failures of any kind, including, but not limited to malfunctions, interruptions, or disconnections in phone lines, internet connectivity or electronic transmission errors, or network hardware or software or failure of the Hackathon Website;

Unauthorized human intervention in any part of the entry process or the Hackathon;

Technical or human error which may occur in the administration of the Hackathon or the processing of Submissions; or 

Any injury or damage to persons or property which may be caused, directly or indirectly, in whole or in part, from the Entrant’s participation in the Hackathon or receipt or use or misuse of any Prize.

The Released Parties are not responsible for incomplete, late, misdirected, damaged, lost, illegible, or incomprehensible Submissions or for address or email address changes of the Entrants. Proof of sending or submitting the aforementioned will not be deemed to be proof of receipt by the Sponsor or Administrator. If for any reason any Entrant’s Submission is determined to have not been received or been erroneously deleted, lost, or otherwise destroyed or corrupted, the Entrant’s sole remedy is to request the opportunity to resubmit its Submission. Such a request must be made promptly after the Entrant knows or should have known there was a problem and will be determined at the sole discretion of the Sponsor.

 

10. Publicity
By participating in the Hackathon, Entrant consents to the promotion and display of the Entrant’s Submission, and to the use of personal information about themselves for promotional purposes, by the Sponsor, Administrator, and third parties acting on their behalf. Such personal information includes, but is not limited to, your name, likeness, photograph, voice, opinions, comments and hometown and country of residence. It may be used in any existing or newly created media, worldwide without further payment or consideration or right of review, unless prohibited by law. 11. General Conditions

  - Treasury rules ≠ execution engine  
  - UX ≠ financial guarantees

- **Failure-aware design**
  - Partial failure handling
  - Timeout settlement
  - Refund / escrow unwind paths

## 🔑 Direct Answer First — *Why not all 3 in one product?*
**You absolutely CAN put all 3 tracks into ONE enterprise product.**  
There is **no rule violation**, and in fact **an enterprise system naturally spans all three**.

The earlier separation was **for judge legibility**, not technical necessity.

Now let’s do this properly and **lock both options**:

1. Why judges *sometimes* penalize “everything-in-one”  
2. How to design **ONE enterprise product that cleanly satisfies ALL 3 tracks**  
3. Exact **resources & tooling** used for:
   - the 4 individual products
   - the **new unified product (ALL 3 tracks)**

No shrinking. No demo framing.

---

# 1️⃣ Judge Reality (Why people warn against “all-in-one”)

Judges do **not** penalize scope.  
They penalize **ambiguity**.

**Failure mode they see often:**
> “This does everything”  
> → but **no single flow is deeply proven**

So the risk is **communication**, not architecture.

**Enterprise truth:**  
Banks, payment processors, ERP systems **always** cover:
- payments
- commerce
- automation  
in one platform.

👉 **So the correct move is NOT to split products — it is to structure the product with clear subsystems.**

---

# 2️⃣ NEW LOCKED PRODUCT — ALL 3 TRACKS IN ONE

## 🏛️ PRODUCT 5 — **Autonomous Commerce & Treasury Fabric (ACTF)**  
(**Enterprise-grade, single product, multi-track compliant**)

### What it is
A **programmable money operating system** where:

- AI agents  
- human creators  
- automated workflows  

**all transact, escrow, invoice, and settle using MNEE**, under policy and governance.

This is **not a bundle**.  
This is **one coherent financial fabric**.

### ✅ Track 2: **Commerce & Creator Tools** (FULL)

**Inside ACTF:**
- Creator checkout in MNEE
- Agent or human payer
- Automatic creator payouts

- Subscription / paywall logic

### ✅ Track 3: **Financial Automation** (FULL)

## 1) Enterprise pain points (why ATG exists)

### 1.1 The real problem
Enterprises are starting to run **automated systems / agents** that:
- buy data
- pay for APIs / compute
- pay vendors / freelancers
- settle internal chargebacks

But finance teams **cannot** allow “an agent wallet” to spend freely because it breaks:
- spend controls
- auditability
- approvals
- fraud monitoring
- reconciliation

### 1.2 Why it exists (root causes)
1) **Non-human actors** are not natively supported by finance governance  
2) Traditional controls assume:
   - a human approver
   - a purchase order process
   - a bank payment batch
3) On-chain payments are **final**, so control must happen **before** settlement  
4) ERC-20 transfers alone do not encode:
   - purpose
   - policy compliance
   - approval decisions
   - rollback/escrow conditions

### 1.3 Who suffers (departments + individuals)
**Finance & Treasury**
- Manual reviews of agent spend requests
- Policy exceptions and audit evidence creation
- Month-end reconciliation nightmare

**Security / Risk / Fraud**
- No deterministic guardrails → must rely on after-the-fact monitoring  
- High incident cost (investigation + containment)

**Engineering / Platform**
- Rebuild custom payment logic per agent/product team  
- Fragile integrations, inconsistent standards

**Procurement & Vendor Management**
- No consistent “who can pay whom under what terms”

### 1.4 Loss types (time / energy / money)
- **Time:** manual approvals, spreadsheet controls, reconciliation
- **Money:** overspend, duplicate payments, vendor disputes, fraud leakage
- **Energy:** operational overhead + repeated escalations + audit stress
- **Opportunity cost:** teams slow down adoption of automation/agents

### 1.5 Customer / user definition
**Customer (buyer):** Enterprise (CFO org / treasury owner / platform owner)  
**Primary users:**
- Treasury admin
- Finance ops analyst
- Risk admin
- Engineering platform admin
- Agent operator (the team running agents)

**Secondary users:**
- Vendors / data providers / creators (payment recipients)
- Auditors (internal/external)

---

## 2) What ATG is (one sentence, enterprise grade)
**ATG is a policy-enforced, auditable, deterministic settlement layer that lets agents and automated systems spend MNEE safely via approvals, escrow, limits, and traceable intent.**

Hackathon Track fit:
- **AI & Agent Payments** (primary): agents pay for services/data
- **Financial Automation** (secondary): treasury management + escrow citeturn1view0

---

## 3) Use cases (UC) and scenarios (S)

### UC-01: Agent pays for a data/API service (autonomous within limits)
**Actors:** Agent, Treasury Governor, Service Provider  
**Goal:** Pay MNEE automatically if policy allows.

**Scenario S-01 (happy path)**
1. Agent requests “Pay provider X 15 MNEE for dataset Y”
2. ATG checks:
   - agent registered
   - provider allowlisted
   - spend limit not exceeded
3. ATG executes transfer (or escrow-release) and emits audit event
4. Provider receives MNEE, receipts generated

**Scenario S-02 (policy deny)**
- Provider not allowlisted → request rejected with reason code + event log

### UC-02: Human-approved payment for high-risk spend
**Actors:** Agent, Approver, Treasury  
**Scenario S-03**
1. Agent creates payment request
2. Request enters “PendingApproval”
3. Approver approves on UI
4. Treasury executes settlement
5. Full audit: request→approval→payment hash

### UC-03: Escrow + conditional release (milestone-based)
**Actors:** Agent, Provider, Treasury  
**Scenario S-04**
1. Agent opens escrow with MNEE amount
2. Provider delivers service
3. Agent (or oracle) marks success
4. Escrow releases; if timeout/dispute → refund path

### UC-04: Budgeting & chargeback (cost center attribution)
**Actors:** Finance ops  
**Scenario S-05**
- Each payment tagged with:
  - cost center
  - agent id
  - purpose hash
  - request id
- Finance exports events for reconciliation

---

## 4) Requirements (R) — Functional + Non-functional

### 4.1 Functional requirements
**R-01 Agent Registry:** register/disable agents; map agent → policy profile  
**R-02 Allowlist / Denylist:** approved payees, optional token restrictions  
**R-03 Limits:** daily/weekly/monthly spend, per-transaction cap  
**R-04 Payment Requests:** create, approve/deny, execute, cancel  
**R-05 Escrow:** lock funds, release, refund, timeout  
**R-06 Audit Events:** every decision emits structured on-chain events  
**R-07 Idempotency:** prevent duplicate execution for same request id  
**R-08 Emergency Controls:** pause outbound payments (circuit breaker)

### 4.2 Non-functional requirements (enterprise)
**NFR-01 Security:** principle of least privilege; role-based access control  
**NFR-02 Observability:** events + off-chain indexing compatibility  
**NFR-03 Determinism:** no hidden off-chain state required for correctness  
**NFR-04 Upgradability posture:** (optional) proxy-ready design, or explicit “no-upgrade” with migration plan  
**NFR-05 Testability:** comprehensive automated tests + reproducible manual test guide

---

## 5) Technology choices (market standard, enterprise)
### 5.1 Smart contracts
- **Solidity** (EVM standard for Ethereum)
- **OpenZeppelin Contracts** for AccessControl, SafeERC20, Pausable patterns (battle-tested) citeturn0search17turn0search2

### 5.2 Dev & test framework
- **Hardhat** for compilation, local network, deployment scripts, automated tests citeturn0search1turn0search5

### 5.3 On-chain interaction
- **ethers.js** (widely used JS/TS Ethereum library) citeturn1view0

### 5.4 Frontend / wallet integration
- **wagmi + viem** (modern TS-first stack; wagmi built on viem) citeturn0search3turn0search11turn0search15

### 5.5 Why these, and why not others (brief, enterprise reasons)
- **Why Hardhat:** mature, widely adopted, strong plugin ecosystem, standard in hackathons and teams citeturn0search1turn0search5  
- **Why OpenZeppelin:** de facto standard security library for access control & ERC-20 safety citeturn0search17turn0search2  
- **Why wagmi/viem over older web3.js:** stronger TS ergonomics + modern approach; wagmi is explicitly built on viem citeturn0search3turn0search11  
- **Why MetaMask/Coinbase/Rainbow:** maximum judge accessibility (they’re common consumer wallets) and align with the hackathon’s own wallet suggestions citeturn1view0

## 6) Architecture (components)

### 6.1 On-chain contracts
1) **AgentRegistry.sol**  
   - registers agent wallets
   - associates policy profile id
2) **PolicyEngine.sol**  
   - allowlist checks
   - spending limits (rolling windows)
3) **TreasuryGovernor.sol**  
   - create payment request
   - approve/deny
   - execute transfer (MNEE)
4) **EscrowVault.sol**  
   - hold MNEE
   - conditional release/refund

### 6.2 Off-chain services (enterprise support)
- **Indexer** (reads contract events to DB)
- **Admin Console** (React UI)
- **Policy Admin API** (optional; but policy enforcement stays on-chain)

### 6.3 AI usage (optional, allowed but not required)
AI is **not** used to “decide payments” (that’s risky).  
AI is used for:
- anomaly detection suggestions (off-chain)
- policy tuning recommendations
- natural-language request drafting → converted into deterministic request payload

### 8.1 Solidity: core interfaces (MNEE ERC-20)

### 8.2 contracts/AgentRegistry.sol
- Purpose: enterprise identity layer for non-human spenders  
- Why: you cannot allow arbitrary wallets to claim “I am an agent”

```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "@openzeppelin/contracts/access/AccessControl.sol";

contract AgentRegistry is AccessControl {
    bytes32 public constant REGISTRY_ADMIN_ROLE = keccak256("REGISTRY_ADMIN_ROLE");

    struct AgentInfo {
        bool enabled;
        uint256 policyId;
        string metadataURI; // optional: off-chain details / DID / etc.
    }

    mapping(address => AgentInfo) private agents;

    event AgentRegistered(address indexed agent, uint256 policyId, string metadataURI);
    event AgentStatusChanged(address indexed agent, bool enabled);
    event AgentPolicyChanged(address indexed agent, uint256 policyId);

    constructor(address superAdmin) {
        _grantRole(DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE, superAdmin);
        _grantRole(REGISTRY_ADMIN_ROLE, superAdmin);
    }

    function registerAgent(address agent, uint256 policyId, string calldata metadataURI)
        external
        onlyRole(REGISTRY_ADMIN_ROLE)
    {
        agents[agent] = AgentInfo({ enabled: true, policyId: policyId, metadataURI: metadataURI });
        emit AgentRegistered(agent, policyId, metadataURI);
    }

    function setAgentEnabled(address agent, bool enabled)
        external
        onlyRole(REGISTRY_ADMIN_ROLE)
    {
        agents[agent].enabled = enabled;
        emit AgentStatusChanged(agent, enabled);
    }

    function setAgentPolicy(address agent, uint256 policyId)
        external
        onlyRole(REGISTRY_ADMIN_ROLE)
    {
        agents[agent].policyId = policyId;
        emit AgentPolicyChanged(agent, policyId);
    }

    function getAgent(address agent) external view returns (AgentInfo memory) {
        return agents[agent];
    }

    function isAgentEnabled(address agent) external view returns (bool) {
        return agents[agent].enabled;
    }

    function policyIdOf(address agent) external view returns (uint256) {
        return agents[agent].policyId;
    }
}
```

**Why OpenZeppelin AccessControl:** standard, explicit role governance; reduces security mistakes. citeturn0search17

---

### 8.3 contracts/PolicyEngine.sol
- Purpose: enforce allowlists + limits deterministically
- Why: policy must be enforceable *before* the token transfer

```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "@openzeppelin/contracts/access/AccessControl.sol";

contract PolicyEngine is AccessControl {
    bytes32 public constant POLICY_ADMIN_ROLE = keccak256("POLICY_ADMIN_ROLE");

    struct Policy {
        bool exists;
        uint256 perTxLimit;     // max per payment
        uint256 dailyLimit;     // max per 24h window (simplified)
        bool requireApproval;   // force human approval
    }

    // policyId => Policy
    mapping(uint256 => Policy) public policies;

    // policyId => payee => allowed
    mapping(uint256 => mapping(address => bool)) public allowlist;

    // policyId => dayIndex => spent
    mapping(uint256 => mapping(uint256 => uint256)) public dailySpent;

    event PolicyUpserted(uint256 indexed policyId, uint256 perTxLimit, uint256 dailyLimit, bool requireApproval);
    event AllowlistSet(uint256 indexed policyId, address indexed payee, bool allowed);

    constructor(address superAdmin) {
        _grantRole(DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE, superAdmin);
        _grantRole(POLICY_ADMIN_ROLE, superAdmin);
    }

    function upsertPolicy(uint256 policyId, uint256 perTxLimit, uint256 dailyLimit, bool requireApproval)
        external
        onlyRole(POLICY_ADMIN_ROLE)
    {
        policies[policyId] = Policy(true, perTxLimit, dailyLimit, requireApproval);
        emit PolicyUpserted(policyId, perTxLimit, dailyLimit, requireApproval);
    }

    function setAllowlist(uint256 policyId, address payee, bool allowed)
        external
        onlyRole(POLICY_ADMIN_ROLE)
    {
        allowlist[policyId][payee] = allowed;
        emit AllowlistSet(policyId, payee, allowed);
    }

    function checkAndConsume(uint256 policyId, address payee, uint256 amount, uint256 timestamp)
        external
        returns (bool ok, string memory reason, bool requireApproval)
    {
        Policy memory p = policies[policyId];
        if (!p.exists) return (false, "POLICY_NOT_FOUND", false);
        if (!allowlist[policyId][payee]) return (false, "PAYEE_NOT_ALLOWED", p.requireApproval);
        if (amount > p.perTxLimit) return (false, "PER_TX_LIMIT", p.requireApproval);

        uint256 dayIndex = timestamp / 1 days;
        uint256 spent = dailySpent[policyId][dayIndex];

        if (spent + amount > p.dailyLimit) return (false, "DAILY_LIMIT", p.requireApproval);

        dailySpent[policyId][dayIndex] = spent + amount;
        return (true, "OK", p.requireApproval);
    }
}
```

**Note:** enterprise version can extend to rolling windows, per-agent buckets, cost centers, etc. This is the deterministic core.

---

### 8.4 contracts/TreasuryGovernor.sol (the heart)
- Purpose: payment request lifecycle + settlement
- Why: enterprise traceability needs request IDs, approvals, idempotency

```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "@openzeppelin/contracts/token/ERC20/utils/SafeERC20.sol";
import "@openzeppelin/contracts/security/Pausable.sol";
import "@openzeppelin/contracts/access/AccessControl.sol";

interface IAgentRegistry {
    function isAgentEnabled(address agent) external view returns (bool);
    function policyIdOf(address agent) external view returns (uint256);
}

interface IPolicyEngine {
    function checkAndConsume(uint256 policyId, address payee, uint256 amount, uint256 timestamp)
        external
        returns (bool ok, string memory reason, bool requireApproval);
}

contract TreasuryGovernor is AccessControl, Pausable {
    using SafeERC20 for IERC20;

    bytes32 public constant TREASURY_ADMIN_ROLE = keccak256("TREASURY_ADMIN_ROLE");
    bytes32 public constant APPROVER_ROLE = keccak256("APPROVER_ROLE");

    IERC20 public immutable mnee;
    IAgentRegistry public immutable agentRegistry;
    IPolicyEngine public immutable policyEngine;

    enum Status { None, PendingApproval, Approved, Executed, Denied, Cancelled }

    struct PaymentRequest {
        address agent;
        address payee;
        uint256 amount;
        uint256 createdAt;
        bytes32 purposeHash;   // deterministic “why” (hash of invoice/data request)
        Status status;
        string lastReason;
    }

    mapping(bytes32 => PaymentRequest) public requests;

    event PaymentRequested(bytes32 indexed requestId, address indexed agent, address indexed payee, uint256 amount, bytes32 purposeHash);
    event PaymentApproved(bytes32 indexed requestId, address indexed approver);
    event PaymentDenied(bytes32 indexed requestId, address indexed approver, string reason);
    event PaymentExecuted(bytes32 indexed requestId, address indexed executor, bytes32 txRef);
    event PaymentCancelled(bytes32 indexed requestId, address indexed agent);

    constructor(
        address superAdmin,
        address mneeToken,
        address registry,
        address policy
    ) {
        _grantRole(DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE, superAdmin);
        _grantRole(TREASURY_ADMIN_ROLE, superAdmin);
        _grantRole(APPROVER_ROLE, superAdmin);

        mnee = IERC20(mneeToken);
        agentRegistry = IAgentRegistry(registry);
        policyEngine = IPolicyEngine(policy);
    }

    function pause() external onlyRole(TREASURY_ADMIN_ROLE) { _pause(); }
    function unpause() external onlyRole(TREASURY_ADMIN_ROLE) { _unpause(); }

    function createPaymentRequest(bytes32 requestId, address payee, uint256 amount, bytes32 purposeHash)
        external
        whenNotPaused
    {
        require(requests[requestId].status == Status.None, "REQUEST_ALREADY_EXISTS");
        require(agentRegistry.isAgentEnabled(msg.sender), "AGENT_DISABLED");

        uint256 policyId = agentRegistry.policyIdOf(msg.sender);

        (bool ok, string memory reason, bool requireApproval) =
            policyEngine.checkAndConsume(policyId, payee, amount, block.timestamp);

        if (!ok) {
            // Policy decision is still auditable on-chain via revert? We prefer storing.
            requests[requestId] = PaymentRequest(msg.sender, payee, amount, block.timestamp, purposeHash, Status.Denied, reason);
            emit PaymentRequested(requestId, msg.sender, payee, amount, purposeHash);
            emit PaymentDenied(requestId, address(0), reason);
            return;
        }

        Status st = requireApproval ? Status.PendingApproval : Status.Approved;
        requests[requestId] = PaymentRequest(msg.sender, payee, amount, block.timestamp, purposeHash, st, "OK");
        emit PaymentRequested(requestId, msg.sender, payee, amount, purposeHash);
    }

    function approve(bytes32 requestId) external onlyRole(APPROVER_ROLE) whenNotPaused {
        PaymentRequest storage r = requests[requestId];
        require(r.status == Status.PendingApproval, "NOT_PENDING");
        r.status = Status.Approved;
        emit PaymentApproved(requestId, msg.sender);
    }

    function deny(bytes32 requestId, string calldata reason) external onlyRole(APPROVER_ROLE) whenNotPaused {
        PaymentRequest storage r = requests[requestId];
        require(r.status == Status.PendingApproval, "NOT_PENDING");
        r.status = Status.Denied;
        r.lastReason = reason;
        emit PaymentDenied(requestId, msg.sender, reason);
    }

    function cancel(bytes32 requestId) external whenNotPaused {
        PaymentRequest storage r = requests[requestId];
        require(r.agent == msg.sender, "NOT_OWNER");
        require(r.status == Status.PendingApproval || r.status == Status.Approved, "NOT_CANCELABLE");
        r.status = Status.Cancelled;
        emit PaymentCancelled(requestId, msg.sender);
    }

    function execute(bytes32 requestId) external onlyRole(TREASURY_ADMIN_ROLE) whenNotPaused {
        PaymentRequest storage r = requests[requestId];
        require(r.status == Status.Approved, "NOT_APPROVED");

        r.status = Status.Executed;

        // TreasuryGovernor must hold MNEE already (funded by treasury ops).
        mnee.safeTransfer(r.payee, r.amount);

        // txRef is an audit correlation id; here we emit requestId again.
        emit PaymentExecuted(requestId, msg.sender, requestId);
    }
}
```

**Why SafeERC20 + AccessControl + Pausable:** standard secure patterns for ERC-20 interactions and emergency shutdown. citeturn0search2turn0search17

---

### 8.5 contracts/EscrowVault.sol (enterprise escrow)
For ATG, escrow is a first-class feature (milestones, disputes, timeout).

```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "@openzeppelin/contracts/token/ERC20/utils/SafeERC20.sol";
import "@openzeppelin/contracts/access/AccessControl.sol";

contract EscrowVault is AccessControl {
    using SafeERC20 for IERC20;

    bytes32 public constant ESCROW_ADMIN_ROLE = keccak256("ESCROW_ADMIN_ROLE");

    IERC20 public immutable mnee;

    enum EscrowStatus { None, Funded, Released, Refunded }

    struct Escrow {
        address payer;     // treasury/agent
        address payee;     // provider
        uint256 amount;
        uint256 expiresAt;
        EscrowStatus status;
    }

    mapping(bytes32 => Escrow) public escrows;

    event EscrowFunded(bytes32 indexed escrowId, address indexed payer, address indexed payee, uint256 amount, uint256 expiresAt);
    event EscrowReleased(bytes32 indexed escrowId, address indexed by);
    event EscrowRefunded(bytes32 indexed escrowId, address indexed by);

    constructor(address superAdmin, address mneeToken) {
        _grantRole(DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE, superAdmin);
        _grantRole(ESCROW_ADMIN_ROLE, superAdmin);
        mnee = IERC20(mneeToken);
    }

    function fund(bytes32 escrowId, address payee, uint256 amount, uint256 expiresAt)
        external
        onlyRole(ESCROW_ADMIN_ROLE)
    {
        require(escrows[escrowId].status == EscrowStatus.None, "ESCROW_EXISTS");
        escrows[escrowId] = Escrow(msg.sender, payee, amount, expiresAt, EscrowStatus.Funded);
        emit EscrowFunded(escrowId, msg.sender, payee, amount, expiresAt);
    }

    function release(bytes32 escrowId) external onlyRole(ESCROW_ADMIN_ROLE) {
        Escrow storage e = escrows[escrowId];
        require(e.status == EscrowStatus.Funded, "NOT_FUNDED");
        e.status = EscrowStatus.Released;
        mnee.safeTransfer(e.payee, e.amount);
        emit EscrowReleased(escrowId, msg.sender);
    }

    function refund(bytes32 escrowId) external onlyRole(ESCROW_ADMIN_ROLE) {
        Escrow storage e = escrows[escrowId];
        require(e.status == EscrowStatus.Funded, "NOT_FUNDED");
        require(block.timestamp >= e.expiresAt, "NOT_EXPIRED");
        e.status = EscrowStatus.Refunded;
        // funds remain in treasury; if vault itself holds funds, transfer back here
        emit EscrowRefunded(escrowId, msg.sender);
    }
}
```

# 11) 100% Manual Testing (step-by-step)

## Manual Setup (local enterprise reproducibility)
1) Install Node LTS + git  
2) `git clone <repo>`  
3) `npm i`  
4) Start local chain:  
   - `npx hardhat node`
5) In a new terminal deploy:  
   - `npx hardhat run scripts/deploy.ts --network localhost`
6) Fund TreasuryGovernor with test MNEE  
   - For local tests you deploy a **MockMNEE ERC-20** that mimics 18 decimals and then set ATG token address to it.  
   - For live/sepolia you point to the official contract address. (Contract address required by hackathon is on Devpost resources.) citeturn1view0

## Manual Test M-04 (Happy path: agent pays provider)
1) Open Admin UI (`apps/web`) and connect MetaMask (local Hardhat account imported)
2) Register agent wallet
3) Create policy:
   - perTxLimit = 50 MNEE
   - dailyLimit = 200 MNEE
   - requireApproval = false
4) Allowlist provider address
5) From agent console:
   - create `requestId` (random bytes32)
   - set payee/provider
   - amount = 15 MNEE
   - purposeHash = keccak256(invoice JSON)
6) Confirm request is “Approved”
7) As treasury admin, click “Execute”
8) Verify:
   - provider balance increased by 15 MNEE
   - PaymentExecuted event emitted with requestId

## Manual Test M-03 (limit exceed)
1) Set perTxLimit = 10 MNEE
2) Try payment 15 MNEE
3) Expected:
   - request stored as Denied with reason “PER_TX_LIMIT”
   - PaymentDenied event emitted

## Manual Test M-08 (pause)
1) Treasury admin clicks Pause
2) Attempt create/approve/execute
3) Expected: blocked until Unpause

*(Repeat similarly for escrow fund/release/refund.)*

```ts
import { expect } from "chai";
import { ethers } from "hardhat";

describe("ATG - unit", function () {
  it("T-A01: registers agent and toggles enabled", async () => {
    const [admin, agent] = await ethers.getSigners();

    const AgentRegistry = await ethers.getContractFactory("AgentRegistry");
    const reg = await AgentRegistry.deploy(admin.address);

    await reg.registerAgent(agent.address, 1, "ipfs://agent");
    expect((await reg.getAgent(agent.address)).enabled).to.eq(true);

    await reg.setAgentEnabled(agent.address, false);
    expect(await reg.isAgentEnabled(agent.address)).to.eq(false);
  });

  it("T-A07: rejects duplicate requestId", async () => {
    const [admin, agent, payee] = await ethers.getSigners();

    // Deploy mock token
    const Mock = await ethers.getContractFactory("MockMNEE");
    const mnee = await Mock.deploy();

    const AgentRegistry = await ethers.getContractFactory("AgentRegistry");
    const reg = await AgentRegistry.deploy(admin.address);

    const PolicyEngine = await ethers.getContractFactory("PolicyEngine");
    const pol = await PolicyEngine.deploy(admin.address);
    await pol.upsertPolicy(1, ethers.parseUnits("100", 18), ethers.parseUnits("1000", 18), false);
    await pol.setAllowlist(1, payee.address, true);

    await reg.registerAgent(agent.address, 1, "ipfs://agent");

    const TG = await ethers.getContractFactory("TreasuryGovernor");
    const tg = await TG.deploy(admin.address, await mnee.getAddress(), await reg.getAddress(), await pol.getAddress());

    const reqId = ethers.keccak256(ethers.toUtf8Bytes("REQ-1"));
    await tg.connect(agent).createPaymentRequest(reqId, payee.address, ethers.parseUnits("10", 18), reqId);

    await expect(
      tg.connect(agent).createPaymentRequest(reqId, payee.address, ethers.parseUnits("10", 18), reqId)
    ).to.be.revertedWith("REQUEST_ALREADY_EXISTS");
  });
});
```

# 16) Enterprise extensions (what we add and why)

## 16.1 New enterprise pain points this extension solves
### P-EX1: “Approvals must be multi-party” (SoD)
**Why exists:** Enterprises require **segregation of duties** (SoD): the person who requests cannot be the one who approves and executes.  
**Who suffers:** Finance controls, audit, compliance.  
**Loss:** audit findings, policy violations, fraud exposure.

### P-EX2: “Intent integrity” (request payload tampering)
**Why exists:** If the request payload is only UI-driven, it can be altered by:
- compromised client
- backend bug
- malicious operator  
**Loss:** unauthorized payments, disputes.

### P-EX3: “Cost center attribution + budget governance”
**Why exists:** Payments need accounting structure:
- cost center
- project
- GL code
- vendor category  
**Loss:** month-end reconciliation, inability to charge back.

### P-EX4: “Replay / double-execution resilience”
**Why exists:** In distributed systems, retries happen.  
**Loss:** duplicates, overpay.

### P-EX5: “Enterprise audit evidence (complete)”
**Why exists:** Auditors want:
- who approved
- what policy applied
- why payment was made  
**Loss:** time + audit failures.

# 17) Extended use cases + scenarios (enterprise)

## UC-EX01: Multi-approver high-risk payment (2-of-3)
**Actors:** Agent, Approver A, Approver B, Treasury Executor  
**Scenario S-EX01**
1. Agent creates request (amount above threshold)
2. System requires **2 approvals**
3. Approver A approves → request stays pending
4. Approver B approves → request becomes Approved
5. Executor executes settlement  
**Audit:** on-chain shows both approvals

## UC-EX02: EIP-712 signed intent from agent runtime
**Actors:** Agent runtime, ATG  
**Scenario S-EX02**
1. Agent runtime produces typed data (EIP-712)
2. Signs with agent key
3. UI/backend submits to chain
4. Contract verifies signature and rejects tampering

## UC-EX03: Cost center budget enforcement
**Scenario S-EX03**
- Payment request includes cost center id  
- Budget controller enforces cost center daily/monthly spend caps  
- Finance exports events for ERP entry

**R-EX11 Upgrade posture:** explicit upgrade strategy (proxy or migration)

# 21) Code extensions (step-by-step)

## 21.1 Add EIP-712 intent verification
**Why:** prevents payload tampering and proves the agent authorized the request.

### contracts/IntentVerifier.sol
```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "@openzeppelin/contracts/utils/cryptography/ECDSA.sol";

abstract contract IntentVerifier {
    using ECDSA for bytes32;

    // EIP-712 domain separator components
    bytes32 private immutable _DOMAIN_SEPARATOR;
    uint256 private immutable _CACHED_CHAIN_ID;

    // Typed struct hash for payment intent
    // PaymentIntent(address agent,address payee,uint256 amount,bytes32 purposeHash,bytes32 costCenterId,uint256 nonce,bytes32 requestId)
    bytes32 public constant PAYMENT_INTENT_TYPEHASH =
        keccak256("PaymentIntent(address agent,address payee,uint256 amount,bytes32 purposeHash,bytes32 costCenterId,uint256 nonce,bytes32 requestId)");

    constructor(string memory name, string memory version) {
        _CACHED_CHAIN_ID = block.chainid;
        _DOMAIN_SEPARATOR = _buildDomainSeparator(name, version);
    }

    function domainSeparator() public view returns (bytes32) {
        if (block.chainid == _CACHED_CHAIN_ID) return _DOMAIN_SEPARATOR;
        // If chain id changes (fork), rebuild
        return _buildDomainSeparator("ATG", "1");
    }

    function _buildDomainSeparator(string memory name, string memory version) internal view returns (bytes32) {
        return keccak256(
            abi.encode(
                keccak256("EIP712Domain(string name,string version,uint256 chainId,address verifyingContract)"),
                keccak256(bytes(name)),
                keccak256(bytes(version)),
                block.chainid,
                address(this)
            )
        );
    }

    function _hashIntent(
        address agent,
        address payee,
        uint256 amount,
        bytes32 purposeHash,
        bytes32 costCenterId,
        uint256 nonce,
        bytes32 requestId
    ) internal view returns (bytes32) {
        bytes32 structHash = keccak256(
            abi.encode(
                PAYMENT_INTENT_TYPEHASH,
                agent,
                payee,
                amount,
                purposeHash,
                costCenterId,
                nonce,
                requestId
            )
        );
        return keccak256(abi.encodePacked("\x19\x01", domainSeparator(), structHash));
    }

    function _verifyIntentSig(bytes32 digest, address signer, bytes calldata signature) internal pure returns (bool) {
        return digest.recover(signature) == signer;
    }
}
```

**Why OpenZeppelin ECDSA:** standard and reduces cryptographic mistakes. citeturn0search2

---

## 21.2 Add multi-approver policy module

### contracts/ApprovalPolicy.sol
```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "@openzeppelin/contracts/access/AccessControl.sol";

contract ApprovalPolicy is AccessControl {
    bytes32 public constant APPROVAL_ADMIN_ROLE = keccak256("APPROVAL_ADMIN_ROLE");

    struct PolicyApproval {
        bool exists;
        uint8 threshold;               // M approvals required
        address[] approvers;           // N approvers
        mapping(address => bool) isApprover;
    }

    mapping(uint256 => PolicyApproval) private approvals; // policyId -> approval config

    event ApprovalPolicySet(uint256 indexed policyId, uint8 threshold, address[] approvers);

    constructor(address superAdmin) {
        _grantRole(DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE, superAdmin);
        _grantRole(APPROVAL_ADMIN_ROLE, superAdmin);
    }

    function setApprovalPolicy(uint256 policyId, uint8 threshold, address[] calldata approvers)
        external
        onlyRole(APPROVAL_ADMIN_ROLE)
    {
        require(threshold > 0, "THRESHOLD_ZERO");
        require(approvers.length >= threshold, "N_LT_THRESHOLD");

        PolicyApproval storage p = approvals[policyId];
        // reset mapping safely by reinitializing: set exists false and recreate not possible in-place,
        // but for hackathon scope we assume policyId set once, or you deploy new contract for changes.
        // Enterprise: store versioned policies.

        p.exists = true;
        p.threshold = threshold;
        p.approvers = approvers;

        for (uint256 i = 0; i < approvers.length; i++) {
            p.isApprover[approvers[i]] = true;
        }

        emit ApprovalPolicySet(policyId, threshold, approvers);
    }

    function getThreshold(uint256 policyId) external view returns (uint8) {
        return approvals[policyId].threshold;
    }

    function isApprover(uint256 policyId, address who) external view returns (bool) {
        return approvals[policyId].isApprover[who];
    }

    function getApprovers(uint256 policyId) external view returns (address[] memory) {
        return approvals[policyId].approvers;
    }
}
```

## 21.3 Add budget controller (cost center governance)

### contracts/BudgetController.sol
```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "@openzeppelin/contracts/access/AccessControl.sol";

contract BudgetController is AccessControl {
    bytes32 public constant BUDGET_ADMIN_ROLE = keccak256("BUDGET_ADMIN_ROLE");

    struct Budget {
        bool exists;
        uint256 dailyLimit;
        uint256 monthlyLimit;
    }

    mapping(bytes32 => Budget) public budgets; // costCenterId -> budget

    // costCenterId -> dayIndex -> spent
    mapping(bytes32 => mapping(uint256 => uint256)) public dailySpent;
    // costCenterId -> monthIndex -> spent
    mapping(bytes32 => mapping(uint256 => uint256)) public monthlySpent;

    event BudgetSet(bytes32 indexed costCenterId, uint256 dailyLimit, uint256 monthlyLimit);

    constructor(address superAdmin) {
        _grantRole(DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE, superAdmin);
        _grantRole(BUDGET_ADMIN_ROLE, superAdmin);
    }

    function setBudget(bytes32 costCenterId, uint256 dailyLimit, uint256 monthlyLimit)
        external
        onlyRole(BUDGET_ADMIN_ROLE)
    {
        budgets[costCenterId] = Budget(true, dailyLimit, monthlyLimit);
        emit BudgetSet(costCenterId, dailyLimit, monthlyLimit);
    }

    function checkAndConsume(bytes32 costCenterId, uint256 amount, uint256 timestamp)
        external
        returns (bool ok, string memory reason)
    {
        Budget memory b = budgets[costCenterId];
        if (!b.exists) return (false, "BUDGET_NOT_FOUND");

        uint256 dayIndex = timestamp / 1 days;
        uint256 monthIndex = timestamp / 30 days;

        uint256 d = dailySpent[costCenterId][dayIndex];
        uint256 m = monthlySpent[costCenterId][monthIndex];

        if (d + amount > b.dailyLimit) return (false, "COSTCENTER_DAILY_LIMIT");
        if (m + amount > b.monthlyLimit) return (false, "COSTCENTER_MONTHLY_LIMIT");

        dailySpent[costCenterId][dayIndex] = d + amount;
        monthlySpent[costCenterId][monthIndex] = m + amount;

        return (true, "OK");
    }
}
```

---

## 21.4 Upgrade TreasuryGovernor to enterprise (multi-approver + signed intent + budgets)

### Key enterprise changes
- Add `agentNonce` mapping: prevents replay
- Verify EIP-712 signature from agent
- Enforce SoD: agent cannot approve
- Multi-approver threshold check per policy
- Enforce cost center budget consumption
- Better audit events (policyId, costCenterId, reasonCode)

### contracts/TreasuryGovernorEnterprise.sol
```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "@openzeppelin/contracts/token/ERC20/utils/SafeERC20.sol";
import "@openzeppelin/contracts/security/Pausable.sol";
import "@openzeppelin/contracts/access/AccessControl.sol";
import "./IntentVerifier.sol";

interface IAgentRegistryE {
    function isAgentEnabled(address agent) external view returns (bool);
    function policyIdOf(address agent) external view returns (uint256);
}
interface IPolicyEngineE {
    function checkAndConsume(uint256 policyId, address payee, uint256 amount, uint256 timestamp)
        external
        returns (bool ok, string memory reason, bool requireApproval);
}
interface IApprovalPolicyE {
    function getThreshold(uint256 policyId) external view returns (uint8);
    function isApprover(uint256 policyId, address who) external view returns (bool);
}
interface IBudgetControllerE {
    function checkAndConsume(bytes32 costCenterId, uint256 amount, uint256 timestamp)
        external
        returns (bool ok, string memory reason);
}

contract TreasuryGovernorEnterprise is AccessControl, Pausable, IntentVerifier {
    using SafeERC20 for IERC20;

    bytes32 public constant TREASURY_ADMIN_ROLE = keccak256("TREASURY_ADMIN_ROLE");

    IERC20 public immutable mnee;
    IAgentRegistryE public immutable agentRegistry;
    IPolicyEngineE public immutable policyEngine;
    IApprovalPolicyE public immutable approvalPolicy;
    IBudgetControllerE public immutable budgetController;

    enum Status { None, PendingApproval, Approved, Executed, Denied, Cancelled }

    struct PaymentRequest {
        address agent;
        address payee;
        uint256 amount;
        uint256 createdAt;
        uint256 policyId;
        bytes32 purposeHash;
        bytes32 costCenterId;
        uint256 nonce;
        Status status;
        uint8 approvals;        // count approvals
        string reasonCode;      // enterprise reason
    }

    mapping(bytes32 => PaymentRequest) public requests;
    mapping(bytes32 => mapping(address => bool)) public approvedBy; // requestId -> approver -> bool

    mapping(address => uint256) public agentNonces; // monotonic nonce per agent

    event PaymentRequested(
        bytes32 indexed requestId,
        address indexed agent,
        address indexed payee,
        uint256 amount,
        uint256 policyId,
        bytes32 purposeHash,
        bytes32 costCenterId,
        uint256 nonce,
        Status status,
        string reasonCode
    );

    event PaymentApprovalRecorded(bytes32 indexed requestId, address indexed approver, uint8 approvals, uint8 threshold);
    event PaymentExecuted(bytes32 indexed requestId, address indexed executor);
    event PaymentDenied(bytes32 indexed requestId, string reasonCode);
    event PaymentCancelled(bytes32 indexed requestId);

    constructor(
        address superAdmin,
        address mneeToken,
        address registry,
        address policy,
        address approval,
        address budget
    )
        IntentVerifier("ATG", "1")
    {
        _grantRole(DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE, superAdmin);
        _grantRole(TREASURY_ADMIN_ROLE, superAdmin);

        mnee = IERC20(mneeToken);
        agentRegistry = IAgentRegistryE(registry);
        policyEngine = IPolicyEngineE(policy);
        approvalPolicy = IApprovalPolicyE(approval);
        budgetController = IBudgetControllerE(budget);
    }

    function pause() external onlyRole(TREASURY_ADMIN_ROLE) { _pause(); }
    function unpause() external onlyRole(TREASURY_ADMIN_ROLE) { _unpause(); }

    /**
     * Agent creates a request by submitting an EIP-712 signed intent.
     * Anyone can relay it (UI/backend), but signature must match agent.
     */
    function createSignedRequest(
        bytes32 requestId,
        address agent,
        address payee,
        uint256 amount,
        bytes32 purposeHash,
        bytes32 costCenterId,
        bytes calldata signature
    )
        external
        whenNotPaused
    {
        require(requests[requestId].status == Status.None, "REQUEST_ALREADY_EXISTS");
        require(agentRegistry.isAgentEnabled(agent), "AGENT_DISABLED");

        uint256 policyId = agentRegistry.policyIdOf(agent);

        // nonce
        uint256 nonce = agentNonces[agent];
        bytes32 digest = _hashIntent(agent, payee, amount, purposeHash, costCenterId, nonce, requestId);
        require(_verifyIntentSig(digest, agent, signature), "BAD_SIGNATURE");

        // consume nonce immediately (prevents replay even if later denied)
        agentNonces[agent] = nonce + 1;

        // enforce cost center budgets
        (bool bok, string memory breason) = budgetController.checkAndConsume(costCenterId, amount, block.timestamp);
        if (!bok) {
            requests[requestId] = PaymentRequest(agent, payee, amount, block.timestamp, policyId, purposeHash, costCenterId, nonce, Status.Denied, 0, breason);
            emit PaymentRequested(requestId, agent, payee, amount, policyId, purposeHash, costCenterId, nonce, Status.Denied, breason);
            emit PaymentDenied(requestId, breason);
            return;
        }

        // policy checks
        (bool ok, string memory reason, bool requireApproval) =
            policyEngine.checkAndConsume(policyId, payee, amount, block.timestamp);

        if (!ok) {
            requests[requestId] = PaymentRequest(agent, payee, amount, block.timestamp, policyId, purposeHash, costCenterId, nonce, Status.Denied, 0, reason);
            emit PaymentRequested(requestId, agent, payee, amount, policyId, purposeHash, costCenterId, nonce, Status.Denied, reason);
            emit PaymentDenied(requestId, reason);
            return;
        }

        Status st = requireApproval ? Status.PendingApproval : Status.Approved;
        requests[requestId] = PaymentRequest(agent, payee, amount, block.timestamp, policyId, purposeHash, costCenterId, nonce, st, 0, "OK");
        emit PaymentRequested(requestId, agent, payee, amount, policyId, purposeHash, costCenterId, nonce, st, "OK");
    }

    function approve(bytes32 requestId) external whenNotPaused {
        PaymentRequest storage r = requests[requestId];
        require(r.status == Status.PendingApproval, "NOT_PENDING");

        // SoD: requester cannot approve
        require(msg.sender != r.agent, "SOD_VIOLATION");

        // policy-based approver set
        require(approvalPolicy.isApprover(r.policyId, msg.sender), "NOT_AUTH_APPROVER");
        require(!approvedBy[requestId][msg.sender], "ALREADY_APPROVED");

        approvedBy[requestId][msg.sender] = true;
        r.approvals += 1;

        uint8 threshold = approvalPolicy.getThreshold(r.policyId);
        emit PaymentApprovalRecorded(requestId, msg.sender, r.approvals, threshold);

        if (r.approvals >= threshold) {
            r.status = Status.Approved;
        }
    }

    function cancel(bytes32 requestId) external whenNotPaused {
        PaymentRequest storage r = requests[requestId];
        require(r.agent == msg.sender, "NOT_OWNER");
        require(r.status == Status.PendingApproval || r.status == Status.Approved, "NOT_CANCELABLE");
        r.status = Status.Cancelled;
        emit PaymentCancelled(requestId);
    }

    function execute(bytes32 requestId) external onlyRole(TREASURY_ADMIN_ROLE) whenNotPaused {
        PaymentRequest storage r = requests[requestId];
        require(r.status == Status.Approved, "NOT_APPROVED");

        r.status = Status.Executed;
        mnee.safeTransfer(r.payee, r.amount);

        emit PaymentExecuted(requestId, msg.sender);
    }
}
```

**Enterprise effect:**  
- Signed intent ensures integrity  
- Nonce makes replay hard  
- Budget controller ensures cost center governance  
- Approval policy enables M-of-N approvals  
- SoD enforced

```ts
import { expect } from "chai";
import { ethers } from "hardhat";

function cc(name: string) {
  return ethers.keccak256(ethers.toUtf8Bytes(name));
}

describe("ATG - enterprise", function () {
  it("T-EX03: accepts valid EIP-712 signed intent", async () => {
    const [admin, agent, payee, approverA, approverB] = await ethers.getSigners();

    const Mock = await ethers.getContractFactory("MockMNEE");
    const mnee = await Mock.deploy();

    const AgentRegistry = await ethers.getContractFactory("AgentRegistry");
    const reg = await AgentRegistry.deploy(admin.address);
    await reg.registerAgent(agent.address, 1, "ipfs://agent");

    const PolicyEngine = await ethers.getContractFactory("PolicyEngine");
    const pol = await PolicyEngine.deploy(admin.address);
    await pol.upsertPolicy(1, ethers.parseUnits("100", 18), ethers.parseUnits("1000", 18), true);
    await pol.setAllowlist(1, payee.address, true);

    const Approval = await ethers.getContractFactory("ApprovalPolicy");
    const ap = await Approval.deploy(admin.address);
    await ap.setApprovalPolicy(1, 2, [approverA.address, approverB.address]);

    const Budget = await ethers.getContractFactory("BudgetController");
    const bc = await Budget.deploy(admin.address);
    await bc.setBudget(cc("CC-IT"), ethers.parseUnits("1000", 18), ethers.parseUnits("5000", 18));

    const TG = await ethers.getContractFactory("TreasuryGovernorEnterprise");
    const tg = await TG.deploy(
      admin.address,
      await mnee.getAddress(),
      await reg.getAddress(),
      await pol.getAddress(),
      await ap.getAddress(),
      await bc.getAddress()
    );

    // fund treasury contract
    await mnee.transfer(await tg.getAddress(), ethers.parseUnits("1000", 18));

    const requestId = ethers.keccak256(ethers.toUtf8Bytes("REQ-ENT-1"));
    const purposeHash = ethers.keccak256(ethers.toUtf8Bytes("invoice#123"));
    const costCenterId = cc("CC-IT");
    const amount = ethers.parseUnits("10", 18);

    // nonce=0 first
    const domain = { name: "ATG", version: "1", chainId: (await ethers.provider.getNetwork()).chainId, verifyingContract: await tg.getAddress() };
    const types = {
      PaymentIntent: [
        { name: "agent", type: "address" },
        { name: "payee", type: "address" },
        { name: "amount", type: "uint256" },
        { name: "purposeHash", type: "bytes32" },
        { name: "costCenterId", type: "bytes32" },
        { name: "nonce", type: "uint256" },
        { name: "requestId", type: "bytes32" },
      ],
    };
    const value = { agent: agent.address, payee: payee.address, amount, purposeHash, costCenterId, nonce: 0, requestId };

    const sig = await agent.signTypedData(domain as any, types as any, value as any);

    await tg.createSignedRequest(requestId, agent.address, payee.address, amount, purposeHash, costCenterId, sig);

    const r = await tg.requests(requestId);
    expect(r.status).to.eq(1); // PendingApproval
  });

  it("T-EX05: blocks replayed signature (nonce)", async () => {
    // Same test idea: submit same signed payload twice → second fails with REQUEST_ALREADY_EXISTS
    // and even if requestId differed, nonce increments, so old signature becomes invalid (BAD_SIGNATURE).
  });

  it("T-EX01: requires 2 approvals then becomes Approved", async () => {
    // ApproverA approves -> still pending; ApproverB approves -> approved
  });
});
```

# 24) Manual tests (enterprise extensions) — 100% step-by-step

## M-EX01: 2-of-3 approvals flow
1) Register policy with threshold=2 and approvers A,B,C
2) Agent submits signed request above threshold requiring approval
3) Approver A approves → UI shows approvals=1/2, still pending
4) Approver B approves → UI shows Approved
5) Treasury executes → payee receives MNEE
6) Verify on-chain events:
   - PaymentRequested
   - PaymentApprovalRecorded x2
   - PaymentExecuted

## M-EX02: signature integrity
1) Generate signed intent
2) Change amount in UI without re-signing
3) Submit
4) Expect: rejected with `BAD_SIGNATURE`

## M-EX03: cost center budget block
1) Set CC-IT dailyLimit=5 MNEE
2) Attempt signed request for 10 MNEE
3) Expect: Denied with `COSTCENTER_DAILY_LIMIT`

# 28) Hardening objectives (what changes now)
We harden ATG across five enterprise dimensions:

2) **Rolling-window limits** (real spend controls, not day buckets)
