# 29) Policy versioning (enterprise correctness)

## 29.1 Why versioning is mandatory
In enterprise finance, policies change:
- approver rotation
- vendor allowlist updates
- risk thresholds
- budgets

Without versioning:
- an old request could be evaluated under a new policy unknowingly
- audit trails become disputable (“which policy was applied?”)

## 29.2 Requirement additions
**R-H01 Policy versions:** policyId + version, immutable once created  
**R-H02 Request binds to policy version:** request stores (policyId, policyVersion)  
**R-H03 Upgrade requires new version:** changing approvers/limits creates v+1

## 29.3 Implementation approach
- `PolicyEngineV.sol` stores multiple versions per policyId
- `ApprovalPolicyV.sol` same approach
- `TreasuryGovernorEnterprise` stores the version it evaluated against

---

## 29.4 Code: PolicyEngineV (versioned + allowlist)
```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "@openzeppelin/contracts/access/AccessControl.sol";

library Reason {
    bytes32 internal constant OK = keccak256("OK");
    bytes32 internal constant POLICY_NOT_FOUND = keccak256("POLICY_NOT_FOUND");
    bytes32 internal constant PAYEE_NOT_ALLOWED = keccak256("PAYEE_NOT_ALLOWED");
    bytes32 internal constant PER_TX_LIMIT = keccak256("PER_TX_LIMIT");
    bytes32 internal constant ROLLING_LIMIT = keccak256("ROLLING_LIMIT");
}

contract PolicyEngineV is AccessControl {
    bytes32 public constant POLICY_ADMIN_ROLE = keccak256("POLICY_ADMIN_ROLE");

    struct PolicyV {
        bool exists;
        uint256 perTxLimit;
        uint256 rollingLimit;      // max spend per rollingWindow
        uint32  rollingWindowSec;  // e.g. 86400 for 24h rolling
        bool requireApproval;
    }

    // policyId => latestVersion
    mapping(uint256 => uint256) public latestVersion;

    // policyId => version => PolicyV
    mapping(uint256 => mapping(uint256 => PolicyV)) public versions;

    // policyId => version => payee => allowed
    mapping(uint256 => mapping(uint256 => mapping(address => bool))) public allowlist;

    // policyId => version => spend ring buffer is handled by SpendWindow module (next section)
    event PolicyVersionCreated(uint256 indexed policyId, uint256 indexed version, PolicyV policy);
    event AllowlistSet(uint256 indexed policyId, uint256 indexed version, address indexed payee, bool allowed);

    constructor(address superAdmin) {
        _grantRole(DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE, superAdmin);
        _grantRole(POLICY_ADMIN_ROLE, superAdmin);
    }

    function createNewVersion(
        uint256 policyId,
        uint256 perTxLimit,
        uint256 rollingLimit,
        uint32 rollingWindowSec,
        bool requireApproval
    )
        external
        onlyRole(POLICY_ADMIN_ROLE)
        returns (uint256 version)
    {
        require(rollingWindowSec > 0, "WINDOW_ZERO");
        version = latestVersion[policyId] + 1;
        latestVersion[policyId] = version;

        versions[policyId][version] = PolicyV(true, perTxLimit, rollingLimit, rollingWindowSec, requireApproval);
        emit PolicyVersionCreated(policyId, version, versions[policyId][version]);
    }

    function setAllowlist(uint256 policyId, uint256 version, address payee, bool allowed)
        external
        onlyRole(POLICY_ADMIN_ROLE)
    {
        require(versions[policyId][version].exists, "VERSION_NOT_FOUND");
        allowlist[policyId][version][payee] = allowed;
        emit AllowlistSet(policyId, version, payee, allowed);
    }

    function getPolicy(uint256 policyId, uint256 version) external view returns (PolicyV memory) {
        return versions[policyId][version];
    }

    function isAllowed(uint256 policyId, uint256 version, address payee) external view returns (bool) {
        return allowlist[policyId][version][payee];
    }
}
```

---

# 30) Rolling-window spend limits (real enterprise control)

## 30.1 Why day buckets are not enterprise-grade
Daily buckets allow exploitation:
- spend near day boundary
- overspend within 24h by splitting across days

Rolling windows are closer to:
- bank card controls
- enterprise procurement thresholds
- fraud guardrails

## 30.2 Requirement additions
**R-H04 Rolling-window spend tracking:** enforce rollingLimit over rollingWindowSec  
**R-H05 Deterministic on-chain implementation:** no off-chain dependency

## 30.3 Implementation approach: SpendWindow module (ring buffer)
We implement a **time-sliced ring buffer**:
- define `sliceSec` (e.g., 15 minutes = 900 sec)
- keep `N = rollingWindowSec / sliceSec` buckets
- each bucket tracks spend in that slice
- maintain total for the rolling window

This is deterministic and gas-bounded.

### contracts/SpendWindow.sol
```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

library Reason {
    bytes32 internal constant OK = keccak256("OK");
    bytes32 internal constant ROLLING_LIMIT = keccak256("ROLLING_LIMIT");
}

contract SpendWindow {
    struct Window {
        uint32 sliceSec;       // e.g. 900
        uint32 nSlices;        // rollingWindowSec / sliceSec
        uint64 lastSliceIndex; // last observed slice index
        uint256 rollingTotal;  // total spend in window
        mapping(uint64 => uint256) sliceSpend; // sliceIndex => spend
    }

    function _currentSlice(uint32 sliceSec) internal view returns (uint64) {
        return uint64(block.timestamp / sliceSec);
    }

    function checkAndConsume(
        Window storage w,
        uint256 rollingLimit,
        uint32 rollingWindowSec,
        uint256 amount
    )
        internal
        returns (bool ok, bytes32 reason)
    {
        // initialize on first use
        if (w.sliceSec == 0) {
            w.sliceSec = 900; // 15m default (enterprise: configurable)
            w.nSlices = uint32(rollingWindowSec / w.sliceSec);
            if (w.nSlices == 0) w.nSlices = 1;
            w.lastSliceIndex = _currentSlice(w.sliceSec);
        }

        uint64 curr = _currentSlice(w.sliceSec);
        uint64 last = w.lastSliceIndex;

        // advance slices: clear out expired slices
        if (curr > last) {
            uint64 steps = curr - last;
            // cap clearing to nSlices to prevent huge loops (worst-case)
            if (steps > w.nSlices) steps = w.nSlices;

            for (uint64 i = 1; i <= steps; i++) {
                uint64 idx = (last + i) % w.nSlices;
                // subtract expired slice spend
                w.rollingTotal -= w.sliceSpend[idx];
                w.sliceSpend[idx] = 0;
            }
            w.lastSliceIndex = curr;
        }

        // check limit
        if (w.rollingTotal + amount > rollingLimit) {
            return (false, Reason.ROLLING_LIMIT);
        }

        // consume in current bucket
        uint64 bucket = curr % w.nSlices;
        w.sliceSpend[bucket] += amount;
        w.rollingTotal += amount;

        return (true, Reason.OK);
    }
}
```

# 31) Bytes32 reason codes (enterprise analytics)

## 31.1 Why bytes32
Strings are:
- inconsistent
- expensive
- hard to aggregate reliably

bytes32 reason codes:
- easy to index
- easy to count
- stable over time

## 31.2 Requirement additions
**R-H06 Standard reason codes:** all deny states emit reasonCode bytes32

We already started this with `Reason` library; now we apply across contracts.

# 32) TreasuryGovernorEnterprise hardened (version + rolling window + bytes32 reasons)

### Key deltas from previous Enterprise contract
- store `policyVersion`
- consume spend from `SpendWindow` keyed by (policyId, version) and optionally by agent
- reasonCode is bytes32
- audit events include policyVersion and reasonCode

### contracts/TreasuryGovernorEnterpriseHardened.sol
```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "@openzeppelin/contracts/token/ERC20/utils/SafeERC20.sol";
import "@openzeppelin/contracts/security/Pausable.sol";
import "@openzeppelin/contracts/access/AccessControl.sol";
import "./IntentVerifier.sol";
import "./SpendWindow.sol";
import "./PolicyEngineV.sol";

library Reason {
    bytes32 internal constant OK = keccak256("OK");
    bytes32 internal constant VERSION_NOT_FOUND = keccak256("VERSION_NOT_FOUND");
    bytes32 internal constant PAYEE_NOT_ALLOWED = keccak256("PAYEE_NOT_ALLOWED");
    bytes32 internal constant PER_TX_LIMIT = keccak256("PER_TX_LIMIT");
    bytes32 internal constant ROLLING_LIMIT = keccak256("ROLLING_LIMIT");
    bytes32 internal constant BUDGET_NOT_FOUND = keccak256("BUDGET_NOT_FOUND");
    bytes32 internal constant BAD_SIGNATURE = keccak256("BAD_SIGNATURE");
    bytes32 internal constant SOD_VIOLATION = keccak256("SOD_VIOLATION");
    bytes32 internal constant NOT_AUTH_APPROVER = keccak256("NOT_AUTH_APPROVER");
}

interface IAgentRegistryH {
    function isAgentEnabled(address agent) external view returns (bool);
    function policyIdOf(address agent) external view returns (uint256);
}
interface IApprovalPolicyH {
    function getThreshold(uint256 policyId, uint256 version) external view returns (uint8);
    function isApprover(uint256 policyId, uint256 version, address who) external view returns (bool);
}
interface IBudgetControllerH {
    function checkAndConsume(bytes32 costCenterId, uint256 amount, uint256 timestamp)
        external
        returns (bool ok, bytes32 reason);
}

contract TreasuryGovernorEnterpriseHardened is AccessControl, Pausable, IntentVerifier {
    using SafeERC20 for IERC20;
    using SpendWindow for SpendWindow.Window;

    bytes32 public constant TREASURY_ADMIN_ROLE = keccak256("TREASURY_ADMIN_ROLE");

    IERC20 public immutable mnee;
    IAgentRegistryH public immutable agentRegistry;
    PolicyEngineV public immutable policyEngineV;
    IApprovalPolicyH public immutable approvalPolicyV;
    IBudgetControllerH public immutable budgetController;

    enum Status { None, PendingApproval, Approved, Executed, Denied, Cancelled }

    struct PaymentRequest {
        address agent;
        address payee;
        uint256 amount;
        uint256 createdAt;
        uint256 policyId;
        uint256 policyVersion;
        bytes32 purposeHash;
        bytes32 costCenterId;
        uint256 nonce;
        Status status;
        uint8 approvals;
        bytes32 reasonCode;
    }

    mapping(bytes32 => PaymentRequest) public requests;
    mapping(bytes32 => mapping(address => bool)) public approvedBy;

    mapping(address => uint256) public agentNonces;

    // rolling spend windows per policy-version (enterprise: optionally per agent too)
    mapping(uint256 => mapping(uint256 => SpendWindow.Window)) private policyWindows;

    event PaymentRequested(
        bytes32 indexed requestId,
        address indexed agent,
        address indexed payee,
        uint256 amount,
        uint256 policyId,
        uint256 policyVersion,
        bytes32 purposeHash,
        bytes32 costCenterId,
        uint256 nonce,
        Status status,
        bytes32 reasonCode
    );
    event PaymentApprovalRecorded(bytes32 indexed requestId, address indexed approver, uint8 approvals, uint8 threshold);
    event PaymentExecuted(bytes32 indexed requestId, address indexed executor);
    event PaymentDenied(bytes32 indexed requestId, bytes32 reasonCode);
    event PaymentCancelled(bytes32 indexed requestId);

    constructor(
        address superAdmin,
        address mneeToken,
        address registry,
        address policyV,
        address approvalV,
        address budget
    )
        IntentVerifier("ATG", "1")
    {
        _grantRole(DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE, superAdmin);
        _grantRole(TREASURY_ADMIN_ROLE, superAdmin);

        mnee = IERC20(mneeToken);
        agentRegistry = IAgentRegistryH(registry);
        policyEngineV = PolicyEngineV(policyV);
        approvalPolicyV = IApprovalPolicyH(approvalV);
        budgetController = IBudgetControllerH(budget);
    }

    function pause() external onlyRole(TREASURY_ADMIN_ROLE) { _pause(); }
    function unpause() external onlyRole(TREASURY_ADMIN_ROLE) { _unpause(); }

    function createSignedRequest(
        bytes32 requestId,
        address agent,
        address payee,
        uint256 amount,
        bytes32 purposeHash,
        bytes32 costCenterId,
        bytes calldata signature
    )
        external
        whenNotPaused
    {
        require(requests[requestId].status == Status.None, "REQUEST_ALREADY_EXISTS");
        require(agentRegistry.isAgentEnabled(agent), "AGENT_DISABLED");

        uint256 policyId = agentRegistry.policyIdOf(agent);
        uint256 version = policyEngineV.latestVersion(policyId);
        PolicyEngineV.PolicyV memory p = policyEngineV.versions(policyId, version);
        if (!p.exists) {
            _deny(requestId, agent, payee, amount, policyId, version, purposeHash, costCenterId, Reason.VERSION_NOT_FOUND);
            return;
        }

        // EIP-712 verify
        uint256 nonce = agentNonces[agent];
        bytes32 digest = _hashIntent(agent, payee, amount, purposeHash, costCenterId, nonce, requestId);
        if (!_verifyIntentSig(digest, agent, signature)) {
            _deny(requestId, agent, payee, amount, policyId, version, purposeHash, costCenterId, Reason.BAD_SIGNATURE);
            return;
        }
        agentNonces[agent] = nonce + 1;

        // cost center budget
        (bool bok, bytes32 breason) = budgetController.checkAndConsume(costCenterId, amount, block.timestamp);
        if (!bok) {
            _deny(requestId, agent, payee, amount, policyId, version, purposeHash, costCenterId, breason);
            return;
        }

        // allowlist
        if (!policyEngineV.allowlist(policyId, version, payee)) {
            _deny(requestId, agent, payee, amount, policyId, version, purposeHash, costCenterId, Reason.PAYEE_NOT_ALLOWED);
            return;
        }

        // per-tx
        if (amount > p.perTxLimit) {
            _deny(requestId, agent, payee, amount, policyId, version, purposeHash, costCenterId, Reason.PER_TX_LIMIT);
            return;
        }

        // rolling window
        (bool ok, bytes32 rreason) = policyWindows[policyId][version].checkAndConsume(p.rollingLimit, p.rollingWindowSec, amount);
        if (!ok) {
            _deny(requestId, agent, payee, amount, policyId, version, purposeHash, costCenterId, rreason);
            return;
        }

        Status st = p.requireApproval ? Status.PendingApproval : Status.Approved;

        requests[requestId] = PaymentRequest(
            agent, payee, amount, block.timestamp, policyId, version, purposeHash, costCenterId, nonce, st, 0, Reason.OK
        );

        emit PaymentRequested(requestId, agent, payee, amount, policyId, version, purposeHash, costCenterId, nonce, st, Reason.OK);
    }

    function approve(bytes32 requestId) external whenNotPaused {
        PaymentRequest storage r = requests[requestId];
        require(r.status == Status.PendingApproval, "NOT_PENDING");

        if (msg.sender == r.agent) revert("SOD_VIOLATION");
        if (!approvalPolicyV.isApprover(r.policyId, r.policyVersion, msg.sender)) revert("NOT_AUTH_APPROVER");
        require(!approvedBy[requestId][msg.sender], "ALREADY_APPROVED");

        approvedBy[requestId][msg.sender] = true;
        r.approvals += 1;

        uint8 threshold = approvalPolicyV.getThreshold(r.policyId, r.policyVersion);
        emit PaymentApprovalRecorded(requestId, msg.sender, r.approvals, threshold);

        if (r.approvals >= threshold) r.status = Status.Approved;
    }

    function cancel(bytes32 requestId) external whenNotPaused {
        PaymentRequest storage r = requests[requestId];
        require(r.agent == msg.sender, "NOT_OWNER");
        require(r.status == Status.PendingApproval || r.status == Status.Approved, "NOT_CANCELABLE");
        r.status = Status.Cancelled;
        emit PaymentCancelled(requestId);
    }

    function execute(bytes32 requestId) external onlyRole(TREASURY_ADMIN_ROLE) whenNotPaused {
        PaymentRequest storage r = requests[requestId];
        require(r.status == Status.Approved, "NOT_APPROVED");
        r.status = Status.Executed;
        mnee.safeTransfer(r.payee, r.amount);
        emit PaymentExecuted(requestId, msg.sender);
    }

    function _deny(
        bytes32 requestId,
        address agent,
        address payee,
        uint256 amount,
        uint256 policyId,
        uint256 version,
        bytes32 purposeHash,
        bytes32 costCenterId,
        bytes32 reasonCode
    ) internal {
        requests[requestId] = PaymentRequest(agent, payee, amount, block.timestamp, policyId, version, purposeHash, costCenterId, agentNonces[agent], Status.Denied, 0, reasonCode);
        emit PaymentRequested(requestId, agent, payee, amount, policyId, version, purposeHash, costCenterId, agentNonces[agent], Status.Denied, reasonCode);
        emit PaymentDenied(requestId, reasonCode);
    }
}
```

# 33) Approval policy versioning (hardening)
We mirror versioning in approver sets too.

### contracts/ApprovalPolicyV.sol
```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "@openzeppelin/contracts/access/AccessControl.sol";

contract ApprovalPolicyV is AccessControl {
    bytes32 public constant APPROVAL_ADMIN_ROLE = keccak256("APPROVAL_ADMIN_ROLE");

    struct ApprovalV {
        bool exists;
        uint8 threshold;
        address[] approvers;
        mapping(address => bool) isApprover;
    }

    mapping(uint256 => uint256) public latestVersion;
    mapping(uint256 => mapping(uint256 => ApprovalV)) private versions;

    event ApprovalVersionCreated(uint256 indexed policyId, uint256 indexed version, uint8 threshold, address[] approvers);

    constructor(address superAdmin) {
        _grantRole(DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE, superAdmin);
        _grantRole(APPROVAL_ADMIN_ROLE, superAdmin);
    }

    function createNewVersion(uint256 policyId, uint8 threshold, address[] calldata approvers)
        external
        onlyRole(APPROVAL_ADMIN_ROLE)
        returns (uint256 version)
    {
        require(threshold > 0, "THRESHOLD_ZERO");
        require(approvers.length >= threshold, "N_LT_THRESHOLD");

        version = latestVersion[policyId] + 1;
        latestVersion[policyId] = version;

        ApprovalV storage v = versions[policyId][version];
        v.exists = true;
        v.threshold = threshold;
        v.approvers = approvers;
        for (uint256 i = 0; i < approvers.length; i++) {
            v.isApprover[approvers[i]] = true;
        }

        emit ApprovalVersionCreated(policyId, version, threshold, approvers);
    }

    function getThreshold(uint256 policyId, uint256 version) external view returns (uint8) {
        return versions[policyId][version].threshold;
    }

    function isApprover(uint256 policyId, uint256 version, address who) external view returns (bool) {
        return versions[policyId][version].isApprover[who];
    }
}
```

# 34) Budget controller hardening: bytes32 reasons
Update BudgetController to return bytes32 reason codes (no strings).

### contracts/BudgetControllerH.sol
```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "@openzeppelin/contracts/access/AccessControl.sol";

library BudgetReason {
    bytes32 internal constant OK = keccak256("OK");
    bytes32 internal constant BUDGET_NOT_FOUND = keccak256("BUDGET_NOT_FOUND");
    bytes32 internal constant COSTCENTER_DAILY_LIMIT = keccak256("COSTCENTER_DAILY_LIMIT");
    bytes32 internal constant COSTCENTER_MONTHLY_LIMIT = keccak256("COSTCENTER_MONTHLY_LIMIT");
}

contract BudgetControllerH is AccessControl {
    bytes32 public constant BUDGET_ADMIN_ROLE = keccak256("BUDGET_ADMIN_ROLE");

    struct Budget {
        bool exists;
        uint256 dailyLimit;
        uint256 monthlyLimit;
    }

    mapping(bytes32 => Budget) public budgets;
    mapping(bytes32 => mapping(uint256 => uint256)) public dailySpent;
    mapping(bytes32 => mapping(uint256 => uint256)) public monthlySpent;

    event BudgetSet(bytes32 indexed costCenterId, uint256 dailyLimit, uint256 monthlyLimit);

    constructor(address superAdmin) {
        _grantRole(DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE, superAdmin);
        _grantRole(BUDGET_ADMIN_ROLE, superAdmin);
    }

    function setBudget(bytes32 costCenterId, uint256 dailyLimit, uint256 monthlyLimit)
        external
        onlyRole(BUDGET_ADMIN_ROLE)
    {
        budgets[costCenterId] = Budget(true, dailyLimit, monthlyLimit);
        emit BudgetSet(costCenterId, dailyLimit, monthlyLimit);
    }

    function checkAndConsume(bytes32 costCenterId, uint256 amount, uint256 timestamp)
        external
        returns (bool ok, bytes32 reason)
    {
        Budget memory b = budgets[costCenterId];
        if (!b.exists) return (false, BudgetReason.BUDGET_NOT_FOUND);

        uint256 dayIndex = timestamp / 1 days;
        uint256 monthIndex = timestamp / 30 days;

        uint256 d = dailySpent[costCenterId][dayIndex];
        uint256 m = monthlySpent[costCenterId][monthIndex];

        if (d + amount > b.dailyLimit) return (false, BudgetReason.COSTCENTER_DAILY_LIMIT);
        if (m + amount > b.monthlyLimit) return (false, BudgetReason.COSTCENTER_MONTHLY_LIMIT);

        dailySpent[costCenterId][dayIndex] = d + amount;
        monthlySpent[costCenterId][monthIndex] = m + amount;

        return (true, BudgetReason.OK);
    }
}
```

# 35) Upgrade / migration posture (explicit enterprise stance)

You have two enterprise-valid options. Pick one and document it:

## Option A — Immutable contracts + migration plan (recommended for audit clarity)
- Deploy contracts as immutable
- If changes needed:
  - create new policy versions (already supported)
  - for logic changes, deploy new contract instance
  - migrate treasury funds via admin-controlled transfer

**Pros:** simplest audit, lowest upgrade risk  
**Cons:** new address when code changes

## Option B — Proxy upgrade (UUPS/Transparent)
- Use OpenZeppelin proxy patterns
- Must include:
  - upgrade role separation
  - timelocks
  - upgrade event audits

**Pros:** stable address  
**Cons:** higher governance and security complexity

**Hackathon-safe enterprise posture:** Option A is often viewed as “safer” unless they explicitly expect upgradeability.

(OpenZeppelin has formal docs for upgrade patterns if you choose B.) citeturn0search17

# 42) Vendor & Payment Risk Tiers (Deterministic, On-Chain)

## 42.1 Why risk tiers exist
Not all payments are equal:
- $5 to a known SaaS ≠ $5,000 to a new data broker
- “trusted vendor” ≠ “unknown wallet”
- “internal cost” ≠ “external payout”

Without tiers:
- policies become bloated
- approvals are overused
- agents lose autonomy

---

## 42.2 Risk Tier Model (Enterprise Standard)

We define **explicit tiers**:

| Tier | Meaning | Typical Examples |
|----|-------|------------------|
| **R0 – Trusted** | Fully trusted | Internal services, long-term vendors |
| **R1 – Low** | Known, low-risk | Popular APIs, known creators |
| **R2 – Medium** | Some uncertainty | New vendors, new datasets |
| **R3 – High** | High risk | First-time vendors, large payouts |
| **R4 – Restricted** | Prohibited | Sanctioned, blocked, flagged |

Each tier maps to **policy behavior**, not discretion.

---

## 42.3 Requirements (Risk Tiers)

**R-RT01** Each payee has exactly one risk tier  
**R-RT02** Policy defines behavior per tier:
- perTxLimit multiplier
- rollingLimit multiplier
- approval threshold
- allow/deny  
**R-RT03** Tier is evaluated **before** spend window consumption  
**R-RT04** Tier is auditable on-chain

---

# 43) Risk Tier Registry (On-Chain)

### contracts/RiskTierRegistry.sol
```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "@openzeppelin/contracts/access/AccessControl.sol";

contract RiskTierRegistry is AccessControl {
    bytes32 public constant RISK_ADMIN_ROLE = keccak256("RISK_ADMIN_ROLE");

    enum Tier { R0, R1, R2, R3, R4 }

    mapping(address => Tier) public tierOf;

    event RiskTierSet(address indexed payee, Tier tier);

    constructor(address superAdmin) {
        _grantRole(DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE, superAdmin);
        _grantRole(RISK_ADMIN_ROLE, superAdmin);
    }

    function setRiskTier(address payee, Tier tier)
        external
        onlyRole(RISK_ADMIN_ROLE)
    {
        tierOf[payee] = tier;
        emit RiskTierSet(payee, tier);
    }
}
```

**Why explicit registry:**  
- No inference
- No AI guessing
- Deterministic and auditable

---

# 44) Policy × Risk Tier Matrix

Policies reference **risk rules**:

| Tier | Allowed? | perTx Multiplier | Rolling Multiplier | Approval |
|----|---------|-----------------|-------------------|----------|
| R0 | Yes | 1.0× | 1.0× | None |
| R1 | Yes | 0.8× | 0.8× | Optional |
| R2 | Yes | 0.5× | 0.5× | 1-of-N |
| R3 | Conditional | 0.2× | 0.2× | 2-of-N |
| R4 | No | 0 | 0 | Block |

This matrix is encoded **on-chain**, not in docs.

---

## 44.1 Policy extension for risk multipliers

Extend `PolicyEngineV` to include tier rules.

### contracts/PolicyRiskRules.sol
```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "@openzeppelin/contracts/access/AccessControl.sol";
import "./RiskTierRegistry.sol";

contract PolicyRiskRules is AccessControl {
    bytes32 public constant POLICY_ADMIN_ROLE = keccak256("POLICY_ADMIN_ROLE");

    struct TierRule {
        bool allowed;
        uint16 perTxBps;     // basis points multiplier (10000 = 1.0x)
        uint16 rollingBps;   // basis points multiplier
        uint8 minApprovals;  // override minimum approvals
    }

    // policyId => version => tier => rule
    mapping(uint256 => mapping(uint256 => mapping(RiskTierRegistry.Tier => TierRule))) public rules;

    event RiskRuleSet(uint256 indexed policyId, uint256 indexed version, RiskTierRegistry.Tier tier, TierRule rule);

    constructor(address superAdmin) {
        _grantRole(DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE, superAdmin);
        _grantRole(POLICY_ADMIN_ROLE, superAdmin);
    }

    function setTierRule(
        uint256 policyId,
        uint256 version,
        RiskTierRegistry.Tier tier,
        bool allowed,
        uint16 perTxBps,
        uint16 rollingBps,
        uint8 minApprovals
    )
        external
        onlyRole(POLICY_ADMIN_ROLE)
    {
        rules[policyId][version][tier] = TierRule(allowed, perTxBps, rollingBps, minApprovals);
        emit RiskRuleSet(policyId, version, tier, rules[policyId][version][tier]);
    }
}
```

# 45) TreasuryGovernor hardening — risk tier enforcement

## 45.1 Enforcement order (critical)
1) **Agent enabled**
2) **Signed intent**
3) **Risk tier check**
4) **Tier allows payment?**
5) **Adjusted perTx limit**
6) **Adjusted rolling window**
7) **Approval threshold override**
8) **Budget consume**
9) **State transition**

This order prevents:
- budget pollution
- spend window pollution
- ambiguous denial reasons

---

## 45.2 Pseudocode inside `createSignedRequest`
```solidity
tier = riskRegistry.tierOf(payee);
rule = policyRiskRules.rules(policyId, version, tier);

if (!rule.allowed) DENY(RISK_BLOCKED);

effectivePerTx = basePerTx * rule.perTxBps / 10000;
effectiveRolling = baseRolling * rule.rollingBps / 10000;

if (amount > effectivePerTx) DENY(PER_TX_LIMIT);
if (!rollingWindow.consume(effectiveRolling, amount)) DENY(ROLLING_LIMIT);

requiredApprovals = max(baseApprovals, rule.minApprovals);
```

Everything is deterministic and auditable.

# 46) Operational Runbook (Enterprise)

This is what **ops, finance, and security** follow.

---

## 46.1 Incident Types

| Incident | Example |
|-------|--------|
| **I-01** Agent key compromised |
| **I-02** Unexpected spend spike |
| **I-03** Wrong vendor paid |
| **I-04** Policy misconfiguration |
| **I-05** Suspected fraud |
| **I-06** Smart contract bug |

---

## 46.2 Runbook — Step by Step

### I-01 Agent key compromised
**Goal:** Stop damage immediately.

1) Treasury admin calls `pause()`  
2) Registry admin disables agent in `AgentRegistry`  
3) Rotate agent key (off-chain)  
4) Re-enable agent with new address  
5) Unpause treasury  
6) Audit:
   - list denied/executed requests
   - export events for timeline

**Evidence produced:**
- Pause event
- AgentStatusChanged
- PaymentDenied (if attempted)

---

### I-02 Unexpected spend spike
**Goal:** Contain, analyze, resume safely.

1) Pause treasury  
2) Inspect:
   - rolling window state
   - cost center budgets
   - risk tier involved  
3) If benign:
   - tighten tier multipliers
   - create new policy version
4) Unpause  
5) Monitor in real time

---

### I-03 Wrong vendor paid
**Goal:** Recover funds if possible; record loss if not.

1) Identify requestId + tx hash  
2) Check escrow status:
   - If escrowed → refund
   - If settled → no on-chain rollback  
3) Update vendor risk tier to R3 or R4  
4) File incident report

**Key note:**  
ATG does **not pretend blockchain reversibility exists** — this honesty is enterprise-grade.

---

### I-04 Policy misconfiguration
1) Pause treasury  
2) Create **new policy version** (never mutate old)  
3) Update agent to point to new policy version  
4) Unpause  
5) Verify new requests bind to new version

---

### I-05 Suspected fraud
1) Pause  
2) Raise risk tier to R4 (blocked)  
3) Disable agent(s)  
4) Export:
   - all PaymentRequested / Denied / Executed events
5) External investigation  
6) Resume with tightened controls

---

### I-06 Smart contract bug
**Option A (Immutable strategy):**
1) Pause  
2) Deploy fixed contract  
3) Migrate treasury funds  
4) Update admin UI  
5) Decommission old address

**Option B (Proxy strategy):**
1) Pause  
2) Upgrade via timelock  
3) Run post-upgrade checks  
4) Unpause

---

## 52) Accounting model (what finance needs)

### 52.1 Accounting principles applied
- **Event-sourced accounting**: on-chain events are the source of truth
- **Double-entry semantics**: every movement has debit/credit meaning
- **Immutable evidence**: blockchain hash anchors every entry
- **Period correctness**: postings align to fiscal periods

### 52.2 Accounting actors
- **Treasury** (custody of MNEE)
- **Expense / COGS** (agent spend)
- **Vendor / Creator Payable**
- **Escrow Holding** (temporary asset)
- **Chargeback / Adjustment** (off-chain corrective entries only)

## 54) Accounting mappings (GL logic)

### 54.1 Standard mappings

| Event | Debit | Credit |
|-----|------|--------|
| Payment Executed | Expense / COGS | Treasury |
| Escrow Funded | Escrow Asset | Treasury |
| Escrow Released | Expense / COGS | Escrow Asset |
| Escrow Refunded | Treasury | Escrow Asset |

### 54.2 Why escrow is an asset
Escrowed funds are:
- still owned
- not yet expensed  
Hence tracked as **temporary asset**, not expense.

## 56) Reconciliation process (step-by-step)

### Daily close (automated)
1) Indexer pulls `PaymentExecuted`, `Escrow*` events
2) Build JE set for the day
3) Sum MNEE deltas
4) Compare against:
   - Treasury wallet balance change
   - Sum(JEs)
5) If mismatch → **alert**

### Month-end close
- Lock period
- Export JE batch
- Finance posts to ERP
- Chain hash stored as audit reference

---

## 57) SIEM integration (security operations)

### 57.1 Why SIEM is mandatory
Money + agents = **attack surface**:
- compromised agent keys
- approval abuse
- unusual spend velocity
- vendor risk escalation

### 57.2 SIEM-ready event schema
Events are **structured**, **typed**, **low-noise**.

```json
{
  "event_time": "2026-01-09T23:25:14Z",
  "event_type": "ATG_PAYMENT_DENIED",
  "severity": "MEDIUM",
  "reason_code": "ROLLING_LIMIT",
  "agent": "0xAgent...",
  "payee": "0xVendor...",
  "risk_tier": "R3",
  "policy_id": 1,
  "policy_version": 3,
  "amount_mnee": "50",
  "request_id": "0xREQ..."
}
```

### 57.3 Severity mapping
| Reason | Severity |
|------|----------|
| OK | INFO |
| PER_TX_LIMIT | LOW |
| ROLLING_LIMIT | MEDIUM |
| RISK_BLOCKED | HIGH |
| BAD_SIGNATURE | CRITICAL |
| SOD_VIOLATION | CRITICAL |

---

## 58) SIEM pipelines (examples)

### 58.1 Targets
- entity["company","Splunk","siem platform"]
- entity["company","Elastic","search and analytics"]
- entity["company","Datadog","monitoring platform"]

### 58.2 Ingestion flow
1) Chain events → Indexer
2) Normalize → SIEM schema
3) Stream via HTTPS / Kafka / Log forwarder
4) Correlate with:
   - IAM logs
   - CI/CD logs
   - Cloud infra logs

---

## 59) Detection rules (examples)

### D-01 Compromised agent
```
IF BAD_SIGNATURE events > 3 in 10 minutes
THEN alert CRITICAL
```

### D-02 Approval abuse
```
IF same approver approves > N high-risk requests/hour
THEN alert HIGH
```

### D-03 Spend anomaly
```
IF rolling spend slope > baseline × 3
THEN alert MEDIUM
```

---

## 60) Incident → Accounting linkage
Every SIEM incident links back to:
- request_id
- tx_hash
- JE(s)
- cost center

This is **forensic completeness**.

---

## 61) Manual tests — Accounting + SIEM

### M-ACC-01 Reconciliation
1) Execute 3 payments (10, 20, 30 MNEE)
2) Export JEs
3) Sum = 60 MNEE
4) Compare treasury balance delta = −60 MNEE

### M-SIEM-01 Signature attack
1) Submit tampered signed intent
2) Observe `BAD_SIGNATURE`
3) Verify SIEM alert fired with CRITICAL severity

---

## 62) Automated tests — Accounting correctness

### T-ACC-01 JE consistency
```ts
expect(sum(journal.amounts)).to.eq(treasuryDelta);
```

### T-ACC-02 Escrow semantics
- Escrow fund → no expense JE
- Escrow release → expense JE created

## 1) Product statement (what Product 2 is)

**Agent-to-Agent Commerce Network (A2A-CN)** is:

> A **protocol + coordination layer** that allows autonomous AI agents to **discover services, negotiate terms, transact using MNEE, and settle automatically** — without human-in-the-loop per transaction, yet **within enterprise guardrails**.

This is **not**:
- a generic marketplace UI
- a wallet
- a DAO
- a chatbot payment trick

This is **machine-to-machine commerce infrastructure**.

---

## 2) Core pain point (why this must exist)

### P2-P1: Agents cannot reliably buy from other agents
Today:
- Agents can *reason* but **cannot transact safely**
- Payments are:
  - manual
  - hard-coded
  - insecure
  - non-auditable

**Result:**  
Agents remain **assistants**, not **economic actors**.

---

### P2-P2: No trust primitive for agent services
Problems:
- How does Agent A know Agent B will deliver?
- How does Agent B know it will get paid?
- How does an enterprise know *what was bought*?

Without a protocol:
- every integration is bespoke
- trust is off-chain
- disputes are human-handled

---

### P2-P3: Coordination cost explodes
Who suffers:
- Platform engineering teams
- Finance
- Security
- Customers (slow responses, outages)

Loss profile:
- time (manual approvals)
- money (overpay / failed retries)
- opportunity (agents can’t self-optimize)

---

## 3) Who the customers are (explicit)

### Primary customer
- **Enterprises running multiple AI agents**  
  (ops agents, data agents, infra agents, procurement agents)

### Secondary customers
- AI service providers (data, inference, tools)
- Platform operators
- Marketplaces

### End beneficiaries
- End users (faster, cheaper, autonomous services)

---

## 4) What exactly is being bought/sold (important)

**Agents trade services, not chat messages.**

Examples:
- API calls
- Datasets
- Inference jobs
- Monitoring checks
- Compute bursts
- Content generation
- Verification services

Each service has:
- price
- SLA
- delivery format
- dispute rules

---

## 5) Use cases (enterprise-real, not toy)

### UC2-01: Data-for-compute swap
- Agent A (Analytics) needs dataset
- Agent B (Data broker) sells dataset
- Payment in MNEE
- Delivery hash verified

### UC2-02: Inference-as-a-service
- Agent A needs fast inference
- Agent B offers GPU inference
- Pay-per-call, auto-settled

### UC2-03: Agent supply chain
- Agent A orchestrates:
  - Agent B (data)
  - Agent C (processing)
  - Agent D (QA)
- Each agent paid independently
- Entire chain auditable

---

## 6) Why this problem exists (root causes)

1) No **service identity** for agents  
2) No **standard negotiation format**  
3) No **atomic delivery-for-payment primitive**  
4) No **machine-verifiable proof of service**  
5) No **enterprise-safe settlement rail**

Humans solve these socially.  
Machines need **protocols**.

---

## 9) Trust & delivery model (critical)

- Hash of delivered data

- On-chain callback confirmation

Without **proof-of-service**:
- sellers cheat
- buyers refuse payment
- disputes explode

A2A-CN enforces:
> **No proof → no payment**

## 10) Governance model

- **Seller agent**

- Deterministic rules

- Explicit failure modes

## 8) Agreement lifecycle (critical)

2) Buyer selects offer
3) Buyer funds escrow (MNEE)
4) Seller delivers service
5) Buyer verifies delivery
6) Escrow releases payment
7) Dispute window expires

**No step is optional.**

## 9) Trust & delivery model

- Hash of delivered artifact
- Signed receipt by buyer agent
- Deterministic callback result

**Rule:**

| Risk | Mitigation |
|----|-----------|

## 13) ENTERPRISE CONTRACT DESIGN (YES, INCLUDED)

### 13.1 AgentServiceRegistry.sol
```solidity
struct Service {
  bytes32 serviceId;
  address agent;
  string schemaURI;
  bool active;
}
```

Purpose:
- Identifies **who can sell what**
- Revocable
- Auditable

---

### 13.2 ServiceOffer.sol
```solidity
struct Offer {
  bytes32 offerId;
  bytes32 serviceId;
  uint256 priceMNEE;
  uint32 slaSeconds;
  bytes32 termsHash;
  bool active;
}
```

Purpose:
- Immutable price + SLA
- No renegotiation mid-flight

---

### 13.3 ServiceAgreement.sol
```solidity
struct Agreement {
  bytes32 agreementId;
  address buyer;
  address seller;
  uint256 amount;
  uint32 expiry;
  Status status;
}
```

Purpose:
- Binds buyer & seller
- References offer
- Governs escrow lifecycle

---

### 13.4 ServiceEscrow.sol
```solidity
enum Status { Funded, Delivered, Released, Disputed, Refunded }

struct Escrow {
  bytes32 agreementId;
  uint256 amount;
  bytes32 deliveryProof;
  Status status;
}
```

Purpose:
- Locks MNEE
- Releases only on proof
- Deterministic state machine

---

### 13.5 DisputeResolver.sol
```solidity
struct Dispute {
  bytes32 agreementId;
  address raisedBy;
  uint256 deadline;
  Status outcome;
}
```

Purpose:
- Time-boxed
- Rule-based
- No human arbitration by default

---

- Deterministic contracts
- No AI judgement on money

# 1) PRODUCT 2 — Canonical Definition (LOCKED)

**Agent-to-Agent Commerce Network (A2A-CN)** is:

> A deterministic, escrow-based, verifiable **machine-to-machine commerce protocol** that allows autonomous agents to **discover services, negotiate immutable agreements, exchange value using MNEE, and settle only upon cryptographically provable delivery**, with full enterprise auditability.

This is **economic infrastructure**, not a marketplace UI.

# 2) Pain Point — Deep, Quantified, Enterprise-Real

## P2-P1: Agents cannot transact autonomously
- Agents can plan but **cannot buy services safely**
- Humans remain in the loop
- Autonomy collapses at payment boundary

**Loss**
- Time: minutes → hours per decision
- Cost: human approvals
- Reliability: retries, failures

---

## P2-P2: No atomic service↔payment primitive
Without atomicity:
- Sellers deliver but are unpaid
- Buyers pay but get nothing
- Disputes go human/legal

**Loss**
- Refund overhead
- Trust erosion
- Platform abandonment

---

## P2-P3: No auditable agent commerce trail
Finance asks:
- What did the agent buy?
- From whom?
- Under which SLA?
- Was it delivered?

Without on-chain evidence:
- No ERP entry
- No compliance
- No accountability

# 8) Smart Contract Design (FULL)

## 8.1 AgentServiceRegistry.sol
```solidity
struct Service {
    bytes32 serviceId;
    address agent;
    bytes32 schemaHash;
    bool active;
}

mapping(bytes32 => Service) public services;
```

Purpose:
- Defines **who can sell what**
- Revocable
- Auditable

---

## 8.2 ServiceOfferRegistry.sol
```solidity
struct Offer {
    bytes32 offerId;
    bytes32 serviceId;
    uint256 priceMNEE;
    uint32 slaSeconds;
    bytes32 termsHash;
    bool active;
}
```

Immutable once created.

---

## 8.3 ServiceAgreement.sol
```solidity
enum AgreementStatus { Created, Funded, Delivered, Released, Disputed, Refunded }

struct Agreement {
    bytes32 agreementId;
    address buyer;
    address seller;
    uint256 amount;
    uint32 expiry;
    AgreementStatus status;
}
```

Binds buyer + seller + offer.

---

## 8.4 ServiceEscrow.sol (MNEE-based)
```solidity
struct Escrow {
    bytes32 agreementId;
    uint256 amount;
    bytes32 deliveryProof;
    AgreementStatus status;
}
```

Rules:
- No proof → no release
- Deterministic state machine

---

## 8.5 DisputeResolver.sol
```solidity
struct Dispute {
    bytes32 agreementId;
    address raisedBy;
    uint256 deadline;
    bool resolved;
    bool buyerWins;
}
```

Time-boxed, rule-based.

---

# 9) Full Solidity Skeleton (Representative)

```solidity
function deliver(bytes32 agreementId, bytes32 proof) external {
    Agreement storage a = agreements[agreementId];
    require(msg.sender == a.seller);
    require(a.status == Funded);
    escrows[agreementId].deliveryProof = proof;
    a.status = Delivered;
}

function release(bytes32 agreementId) external {
    Agreement storage a = agreements[agreementId];
    require(a.status == Delivered);
    require(block.timestamp <= a.expiry);
    a.status = Released;
    mnee.transfer(a.seller, a.amount);
}
```

---

| Service Released | Expense | Escrow |
| Refund | Treasury | Escrow |

ERP-ready.

## 1) ENTERPRISE PAIN STATEMENT (WHY THIS EXISTS)

### P2-1: Autonomous agents cannot transact autonomously
Agents today:
- can reason
- can plan
- can optimize

But **cannot buy services from other agents safely**.

Why?
- No escrow primitive
- No proof-of-delivery standard
- No dispute resolution
- No accounting trail

**Result:** Agents are capped at “assistant” level.

---

### P2-2: Human coordination cost kills agent value
Without agent commerce:
- Humans approve API keys
- Humans reconcile invoices
- Humans resolve failures

**Loss per org (typical):**
- Time: hours/day
- Cost: finance + ops headcount
- Risk: fraud & mispayment
- Opportunity: no self-optimizing systems

---

### P2-3: No auditability of machine-initiated spend
Finance & audit questions that cannot be answered today:
- Which agent bought what?
- From whom?
- Under what SLA?
- Was it delivered?
- Was it disputed?

**Without A2A-CN → no compliance.**

10/01/2026 00:00

**PRODUCT 2:** **Agent-to-Agent Commerce Network (A2A-CN)** — **FULL ELABORATION (Enterprise-grade, like Product-1, with real detail + code + manual/automation tests)**

You’re right: headings alone are BS. Below is the **real** content—**explained, step-by-step**, with **enterprise logic**, **contracts**, **events**, **accounting + SIEM**, and **manual + automated tests**. No fluff.

---

# 1) Product definition
**A2A-CN** is a **service commerce protocol** for autonomous agents where:

- **Seller agents** publish machine-readable service offers (price, SLA, proof schema).
- **Buyer agents** accept offers and fund escrow in **MNEE**.
- **Settlement happens only after verifiable delivery** (proof-of-delivery).
- **Disputes are time-boxed and deterministic**.
- Every step emits **audit-grade events** that feed **ERP journals** and **SIEM alerts**.

This is not “pay agent B.”  
This is: **agent-to-agent procurement + escrow + delivery verification + dispute resolution + accounting evidence**.

---

# 2) Pain point (why it exists, who suffers, impact)
### 2.1 Why it exists
Enterprises already have:
- procurement processes,
- vendor onboarding,
- contracts/SLA,
- reconciliation.

Agents have none of that. They can *decide* but can’t *contract+settle* safely.

### 2.2 Who suffers
- **Platform Engineering:** bespoke integrations per vendor/agent, no standard interface.
- **Finance:** cannot reconcile “agent spend” to services received; no invoice/receipt equivalent.
- **Security/Risk:** no detection for unusual agent spend, fraud patterns, compromised agent keys.
- **Operations:** failures become manual escalations (“why didn’t it deliver?”).
- **End customers:** latency + reliability loss when automation stalls at payment boundary.

### 2.3 What losses look like
- **Time:** approvals + manual payment + manual check = minutes → hours per transaction chain.
- **Money:** retries, refunds, duplicate payments, overpay to “be safe”.
- **Risk:** paying unknown sellers, non-delivery, partial delivery, “poisoned data” delivery.
- **Scalability:** you can’t run 50–500 agents if each purchase requires humans.

---

# 3) Customer, user, and “customer of the customer”
### Primary customer (enterprise buyer)
Company running agents: e.g., NOC automation, data ops, AI ops, internal procurement.

### Users
- **Buyer Agent**: initiates purchase.
- **Seller Agent**: provides service.
- **Orchestrator Agent** (optional): chains multiple buys (data → compute → QA).

### “Customer of the customer”
End users consuming the outcome (reports, uptime, automated fixes). They care about:
- reliability,
- speed,
- correctness,
- cost.

---

# 4) What is a “service” (strict definition)
A service in A2A-CN is **not** “chat help”. It is a deliverable with verifiable proof:

Examples:
- dataset download link + hash
- inference output hash
- monitoring result hash
- proof-of-computation receipt
- signed attestation (seller signs a structured payload)

Each service defines:
- **Input schema** (what buyer provides)
- **Output schema** (what seller returns)
- **Proof schema** (how buyer verifies)
- **SLA** (deadline, retries)
- **Price** (MNEE)
- **Dispute window** (time-boxed)

---

# 5) Use cases (detailed)
## UC2-01: Dataset purchase (classic)
1) Seller publishes “Dataset X” offer with:
   - price = 25 MNEE,
   - SLA = 10 minutes,
   - proof schema: `keccak256(file_bytes)`.
2) Buyer accepts and funds escrow.
3) Seller delivers dataset URI + file hash.
4) Buyer verifies hash after download.
5) Buyer “accepts delivery” on-chain (or auto-accept if proof matches).
6) Escrow releases MNEE to seller.

## UC2-02: Inference-as-a-service
- Buyer sends job spec hash.
- Seller returns output hash.
- Buyer verifies deterministic output or re-run check.
- Escrow releases.

## UC2-03: Multi-agent chain (real enterprise coordination)
Orchestrator:
- buys dataset from Seller A,
- buys compute from Seller B,
- buys validation from Seller C.
If any stage fails, only that stage refunds; chain continues or aborts deterministically.

## UC2-04: Redundant sourcing (resilience)
Buyer funds two sellers for same service; chooses the best proof; releases one; refunds other at SLA expiry.

---

# 6) Requirements (Functional + Non-functional)
### Functional requirements
**R2-F01 Service identity:** sellers register services (revocable).  
**R2-F02 Offer immutability:** offers (price, SLA, proof schema) are immutable once published.  
**R2-F03 Agreement creation:** buyer accepts an offer creating an agreement with a deadline.  
**R2-F04 Escrow funding:** buyer funds escrow in MNEE; escrow locks funds.  
**R2-F05 Delivery proof:** seller submits delivery proof per schema.  
**R2-F06 Acceptance:** buyer accepts delivery (or auto-accept if proof matches).  
**R2-F07 Settlement:** escrow releases payment after acceptance; refund after expiry if not delivered/accepted.  
**R2-F08 Dispute:** buyer can dispute within window; deterministic resolution rules.  
**R2-F09 Audit events:** every state change emits structured events with reason codes.  
**R2-F10 Accounting export support:** events carry fields needed for journal entries.

### Non-functional requirements
**R2-N01 Determinism:** no AI discretion; same inputs → same outcome.  
**R2-N02 Least privilege:** no admin “force release”.  
**R2-N03 Bounded gas:** per-agreement ops O(1).  
**R2-N04 Compliance evidence:** on-chain trace reconstructs what was bought and delivered.  
**R2-N05 Security telemetry:** emit reason codes suitable for SIEM.

---

# 7) Architecture (enterprise)
## On-chain
1) **AgentServiceRegistry**: who can sell which service ID.  
2) **OfferRegistry**: offers referencing service IDs.  
3) **AgreementManager**: agreement lifecycle (buyer-seller binding).  
4) **Escrow**: MNEE lock, release, refund.  
5) **DisputeResolver**: time-boxed disputes.

## Off-chain
- **Indexer** (reads events)
- **Accounting Exporter** (creates journals)
- **SIEM Forwarder** (creates alerts)
- **Agent Runtimes** (buyer/seller software)

---

# 8) Core state machine (no ambiguity)
Agreement status:

- `CREATED` (buyer accepted offer, not funded yet)
- `FUNDED` (escrow funded, SLA timer active)
- `DELIVERED` (seller posted proof)
- `ACCEPTED` (buyer accepted; ready to release)
- `RELEASED` (payment sent)
- `REFUNDED` (refund to buyer)
- `DISPUTED` (dispute open)
- `RESOLVED` (dispute outcome applied)

**Invariant:** funds move exactly once: `RELEASED` XOR `REFUNDED`.

---

# 9) Reason codes (enterprise)
Use `bytes32` reason codes (analytics-friendly):
- `OK`
- `OFFER_INACTIVE`
- `AGREEMENT_EXPIRED`
- `NOT_BUYER`
- `NOT_SELLER`
- `BAD_STATUS`
- `PROOF_MISSING`
- `DISPUTE_WINDOW_CLOSED`

---

# 10) Solidity code (enterprise-grade baseline)
Below is a **coherent minimal enterprise implementation** (not just structs). It’s designed for Hardhat + OpenZeppelin.

## 10.1 `AgentServiceRegistry.sol`
```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "@openzeppelin/contracts/access/AccessControl.sol";

contract AgentServiceRegistry is AccessControl {
    bytes32 public constant REGISTRY_ADMIN_ROLE = keccak256("REGISTRY_ADMIN_ROLE");

    struct Service {
        bytes32 serviceId;
        address seller;
        bytes32 schemaHash; // hash of JSON schema / ABI / interface descriptor stored off-chain
        bool active;
    }

    mapping(bytes32 => Service) public services; // serviceId => Service

    event ServiceRegistered(bytes32 indexed serviceId, address indexed seller, bytes32 schemaHash);
    event ServiceStatusChanged(bytes32 indexed serviceId, bool active);

    constructor(address admin) {
        _grantRole(DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE, admin);
        _grantRole(REGISTRY_ADMIN_ROLE, admin);
    }

    function registerService(bytes32 serviceId, address seller, bytes32 schemaHash)
        external
        onlyRole(REGISTRY_ADMIN_ROLE)
    {
        services[serviceId] = Service(serviceId, seller, schemaHash, true);
        emit ServiceRegistered(serviceId, seller, schemaHash);
    }

    function setActive(bytes32 serviceId, bool active) external onlyRole(REGISTRY_ADMIN_ROLE) {
        services[serviceId].active = active;
        emit ServiceStatusChanged(serviceId, active);
    }

    function isActive(bytes32 serviceId) external view returns (bool) {
        return services[serviceId].active;
    }
}
```

## 10.2 `OfferRegistry.sol`
```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "@openzeppelin/contracts/access/AccessControl.sol";
import "./AgentServiceRegistry.sol";

contract OfferRegistry is AccessControl {
    bytes32 public constant OFFER_ADMIN_ROLE = keccak256("OFFER_ADMIN_ROLE");

    struct Offer {
        bytes32 offerId;
        bytes32 serviceId;
        address seller;
        uint256 price;       // in MNEE smallest units
        uint32  slaSeconds;  // delivery deadline from funding time
        uint32  disputeWindowSeconds; // after delivery
        bytes32 termsHash;   // hash of terms (off-chain)
        bytes32 proofSchemaHash; // hash describing proof format
        bool active;
    }

    AgentServiceRegistry public immutable registry;
    mapping(bytes32 => Offer) public offers;

    event OfferPublished(bytes32 indexed offerId, bytes32 indexed serviceId, address indexed seller, uint256 price);
    event OfferStatusChanged(bytes32 indexed offerId, bool active);

    constructor(address admin, address serviceRegistry) {
        _grantRole(DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE, admin);
        _grantRole(OFFER_ADMIN_ROLE, admin);
        registry = AgentServiceRegistry(serviceRegistry);
    }

    function publishOffer(
        bytes32 offerId,
        bytes32 serviceId,
        uint256 price,
        uint32 slaSeconds,
        uint32 disputeWindowSeconds,
        bytes32 termsHash,
        bytes32 proofSchemaHash
    ) external onlyRole(OFFER_ADMIN_ROLE) {
        require(registry.isActive(serviceId), "SERVICE_INACTIVE");
        address seller = registry.services(serviceId).seller;
        offers[offerId] = Offer(
            offerId, serviceId, seller, price, slaSeconds, disputeWindowSeconds, termsHash, proofSchemaHash, true
        );
        emit OfferPublished(offerId, serviceId, seller, price);
    }

    function setActive(bytes32 offerId, bool active) external onlyRole(OFFER_ADMIN_ROLE) {
        offers[offerId].active = active;
        emit OfferStatusChanged(offerId, active);
    }
}
```

## 10.3 `A2AEscrow.sol` (agreement + escrow + dispute)
This is the “heart”. It uses SafeERC20 for MNEE.

```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "@openzeppelin/contracts/token/ERC20/utils/SafeERC20.sol";
import "@openzeppelin/contracts/access/AccessControl.sol";
import "./OfferRegistry.sol";

library A2AReason {
    bytes32 internal constant OK = keccak256("OK");
    bytes32 internal constant OFFER_INACTIVE = keccak256("OFFER_INACTIVE");
    bytes32 internal constant BAD_STATUS = keccak256("BAD_STATUS");
    bytes32 internal constant NOT_BUYER = keccak256("NOT_BUYER");
    bytes32 internal constant NOT_SELLER = keccak256("NOT_SELLER");
    bytes32 internal constant AGREEMENT_EXPIRED = keccak256("AGREEMENT_EXPIRED");
    bytes32 internal constant DISPUTE_WINDOW_CLOSED = keccak256("DISPUTE_WINDOW_CLOSED");
    bytes32 internal constant PROOF_MISSING = keccak256("PROOF_MISSING");
}

contract A2AEscrow is AccessControl {
    using SafeERC20 for IERC20;

    bytes32 public constant ESCROW_ADMIN_ROLE = keccak256("ESCROW_ADMIN_ROLE");

    IERC20 public immutable mnee;
    OfferRegistry public immutable offers;

    enum Status { NONE, CREATED, FUNDED, DELIVERED, ACCEPTED, RELEASED, REFUNDED, DISPUTED, RESOLVED }

    struct Agreement {
        bytes32 agreementId;
        bytes32 offerId;
        bytes32 serviceId;
        address buyer;
        address seller;
        uint256 amount;
        uint256 fundedAt;
        uint256 deliverBy;        // fundedAt + SLA
        uint256 disputeBy;        // deliveryAt + disputeWindow
        uint256 deliveryAt;
        bytes32 deliveryProof;    // hash/pointer proof
        Status status;
        bytes32 lastReason;
        bytes32 costCenterId;     // for accounting / ERP tagging
    }

    mapping(bytes32 => Agreement) public agreements;

    event AgreementCreated(bytes32 indexed agreementId, bytes32 indexed offerId, address indexed buyer, address seller, uint256 amount, bytes32 costCenterId);
    event EscrowFunded(bytes32 indexed agreementId, uint256 fundedAt, uint256 deliverBy);
    event Delivered(bytes32 indexed agreementId, bytes32 deliveryProof, uint256 deliveryAt, uint256 disputeBy);
    event Accepted(bytes32 indexed agreementId);
    event Released(bytes32 indexed agreementId, uint256 amount);
    event Refunded(bytes32 indexed agreementId, uint256 amount);
    event Disputed(bytes32 indexed agreementId, address indexed raisedBy);
    event Resolved(bytes32 indexed agreementId, bool buyerWins);

    constructor(address admin, address mneeToken, address offerRegistry) {
        _grantRole(DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE, admin);
        _grantRole(ESCROW_ADMIN_ROLE, admin);
        mnee = IERC20(mneeToken);
        offers = OfferRegistry(offerRegistry);
    }

    function createAgreement(bytes32 agreementId, bytes32 offerId, bytes32 costCenterId) external {
        OfferRegistry.Offer memory o = offers.offers(offerId);
        require(o.active, "OFFER_INACTIVE");

        agreements[agreementId] = Agreement({
            agreementId: agreementId,
            offerId: offerId,
            serviceId: o.serviceId,
            buyer: msg.sender,
            seller: o.seller,
            amount: o.price,
            fundedAt: 0,
            deliverBy: 0,
            disputeBy: 0,
            deliveryAt: 0,
            deliveryProof: bytes32(0),
            status: Status.CREATED,
            lastReason: A2AReason.OK,
            costCenterId: costCenterId
        });

        emit AgreementCreated(agreementId, offerId, msg.sender, o.seller, o.price, costCenterId);
    }

    function fund(bytes32 agreementId) external {
        Agreement storage a = agreements[agreementId];
        require(a.status == Status.CREATED, "BAD_STATUS");
        require(msg.sender == a.buyer, "NOT_BUYER");

        OfferRegistry.Offer memory o = offers.offers(a.offerId);
        require(o.active, "OFFER_INACTIVE");

        a.fundedAt = block.timestamp;
        a.deliverBy = block.timestamp + o.slaSeconds;
        a.status = Status.FUNDED;

        mnee.safeTransferFrom(a.buyer, address(this), a.amount);

        emit EscrowFunded(agreementId, a.fundedAt, a.deliverBy);
    }

    function deliver(bytes32 agreementId, bytes32 deliveryProof) external {
        Agreement storage a = agreements[agreementId];
        require(a.status == Status.FUNDED, "BAD_STATUS");
        require(msg.sender == a.seller, "NOT_SELLER");
        require(block.timestamp <= a.deliverBy, "AGREEMENT_EXPIRED");
        require(deliveryProof != bytes32(0), "PROOF_MISSING");

        OfferRegistry.Offer memory o = offers.offers(a.offerId);

        a.deliveryProof = deliveryProof;
        a.deliveryAt = block.timestamp;
        a.disputeBy = block.timestamp + o.disputeWindowSeconds;
        a.status = Status.DELIVERED;

        emit Delivered(agreementId, deliveryProof, a.deliveryAt, a.disputeBy);
    }

    function accept(bytes32 agreementId) external {
        Agreement storage a = agreements[agreementId];
        require(a.status == Status.DELIVERED, "BAD_STATUS");
        require(msg.sender == a.buyer, "NOT_BUYER");
        require(block.timestamp <= a.disputeBy, "DISPUTE_WINDOW_CLOSED");

        a.status = Status.ACCEPTED;
        emit Accepted(agreementId);
    }

    function release(bytes32 agreementId) external {
        Agreement storage a = agreements[agreementId];
        require(a.status == Status.ACCEPTED, "BAD_STATUS");

        a.status = Status.RELEASED;
        mnee.safeTransfer(a.seller, a.amount);

        emit Released(agreementId, a.amount);
    }

    function refundIfExpired(bytes32 agreementId) external {
        Agreement storage a = agreements[agreementId];
        require(a.status == Status.FUNDED, "BAD_STATUS");
        require(block.timestamp > a.deliverBy, "NOT_EXPIRED");

        a.status = Status.REFUNDED;
        mnee.safeTransfer(a.buyer, a.amount);

        emit Refunded(agreementId, a.amount);
    }

    function dispute(bytes32 agreementId) external {
        Agreement storage a = agreements[agreementId];
        require(a.status == Status.DELIVERED, "BAD_STATUS");
        require(msg.sender == a.buyer, "NOT_BUYER");
        require(block.timestamp <= a.disputeBy, "DISPUTE_WINDOW_CLOSED");

        a.status = Status.DISPUTED;
        emit Disputed(agreementId, msg.sender);
    }

    // Deterministic resolver: if disputed and proof missing/zero -> buyer wins, else seller wins (simple baseline)
    // Enterprise extension: plug in rule modules (see risk tiers section below).
    function resolve(bytes32 agreementId) external onlyRole(ESCROW_ADMIN_ROLE) {
        Agreement storage a = agreements[agreementId];
        require(a.status == Status.DISPUTED, "BAD_STATUS");

        bool buyerWins = (a.deliveryProof == bytes32(0));

        a.status = Status.RESOLVED;

        if (buyerWins) {
            mnee.safeTransfer(a.buyer, a.amount);
        } else {
            mnee.safeTransfer(a.seller, a.amount);
        }

        emit Resolved(agreementId, buyerWins);
    }
}
```

That is already a complete minimal protocol: agreement → fund → deliver → accept → release OR timeout refund OR dispute/resolve.

---

# 11) Enterprise extensions (to match Product-1 hardening style)

## 11.1 Policy versioning for commerce rules (like Product-1 policy versions)
You need offer-level governance without mutating history:
- policyId/version attached to offer
- future offers use new versions

Example policy dimensions:
- max price per service tier
- mandatory dispute window min
- mandatory seller KYC flag (if you want)
- allowlist of sellers per buyer org

## 11.2 Risk tiers for sellers/services (commerce-specific)
Same concept as Product-1 risk tiers, but for sellers/services:
- R0 trusted (auto-accept optional)
- R2 medium (buyer must accept manually)
- R3 high (multi-approval + longer dispute window)
- R4 blocked (cannot create agreements)

## 11.3 Accounting and SIEM event completeness
Every emitted event already contains:
- agreementId, buyer, seller, amount, timestamps, proof hash
Add:
- policyId/version
- risk tier
- reason codes on failures (deny events) if you implement pre-check gating in create/fund.

---

# 12) Accounting (ERP-ready journals) — detailed
You asked “enterprise grade”. Here is the clean mapping.

## 12.1 What finance needs per agreement
- what was bought (offerId/serviceId)
- from whom (seller)
- for which cost center (costCenterId)
- amount (MNEE + USD equivalent at posting time)
- evidence (tx hash, contract address)

## 12.2 Journal entry rules (double-entry)
### When funded (EscrowFunded)
- **Debit:** Escrow Asset (MNEE Escrow)
- **Credit:** Treasury / Cash (MNEE Treasury)

### When released (Released)
- **Debit:** Expense / COGS (cost center mapped)
- **Credit:** Escrow Asset

### When refunded (Refunded or Resolved buyerWins)
- **Debit:** Treasury / Cash
- **Credit:** Escrow Asset

### Optional: platform fee
If you add platform fee:
- **Debit:** Expense (or Fee expense)
- **Credit:** Treasury
or split seller payout vs fee payout.

## 12.3 Canonical export JSON (what indexer outputs)
```json
{
  "je_id": "A2A-JE-2026-01-000045",
  "tx_hash": "0x...",
  "event": "RELEASED",
  "agreement_id": "0xAG...",
  "offer_id": "0xOF...",
  "service_id": "0xSV...",
  "buyer": "0xBuyer...",
  "seller": "0xSeller...",
  "cost_center_id": "CC-OPS",
  "amount_mnee": "25.000000000000000000",
  "gl_debit": "6100-OPS-COGS",
  "gl_credit": "1400-ESCROW-ASSET",
  "timestamp_utc": "2026-01-10T00:00:00Z",
  "evidence": { "contract": "0xEscrow...", "block": 0 }
}
```

---

# 13) SIEM (security telemetry) — detailed
## 13.1 What security wants to detect
- repeated disputes from same seller
- repeated timeouts / non-delivery
- spike in escrow funding from a buyer agent
- many agreements created without funding (probing)
- abnormal seller churn (new sellers)

## 13.2 SIEM event schema (normalized)
```json
{
  "event_type": "A2A_DISPUTE_RAISED",
  "severity": "MEDIUM",
  "agreement_id": "0xAG...",
  "buyer": "0xBuyer...",
  "seller": "0xSeller...",
  "offer_id": "0xOF...",
  "amount_mnee": "25",
  "timestamp_utc": "2026-01-10T00:00:00Z"
}
```

## 13.3 Severity mapping
- `EscrowFunded` → INFO
- `Delivered` → INFO
- `Disputed` → MEDIUM
- `Refunded due to expiry` → HIGH (seller reliability risk)
- `Repeated disputes by seller > N` → HIGH/CRITICAL

---

# 14) Runbooks (ops playbooks) — detailed
## I2-01 Seller non-delivery (timeout)
**Trigger:** agreement FUNDED and `block.timestamp > deliverBy`  
**Action:**
1) Call `refundIfExpired(agreementId)` (anyone can call)
2) Indexer marks seller reliability hit
3) Risk tier escalates seller (off-chain governance or on-chain registry update)
4) SIEM alert “seller non-delivery burst” if repeated

## I2-02 Wrong delivery (buyer disputes)
**Trigger:** buyer disputes within dispute window  
**Action:**
1) `dispute(agreementId)`
2) `resolve(agreementId)` by resolver admin (or deterministic module)
3) If buyerWins → refund buyer; else pay seller
4) Record dispute outcome for governance / tiering

## I2-03 Buyer agent compromised
**Trigger:** many agreements created/funded fast  
**Action:**
1) freeze buyer agent in enterprise layer (in Product-1 ATG)
2) block create/fund using allowlists/policy gating (extension)
3) export evidence: tx hashes + agreements list

---

# 15) 100% Manual testing (step-by-step)
Below is **literal click-by-click** style, like you asked in Product-1.

## M2-01 Happy path (create → fund → deliver → accept → release)
**Pre-req:** buyer has MNEE and has approved escrow contract to spend MNEE.

1) Admin registers service  
   - Call `AgentServiceRegistry.registerService(serviceId, seller, schemaHash)`
2) Admin publishes offer  
   - Call `OfferRegistry.publishOffer(offerId, serviceId, price, sla, disputeWindow, termsHash, proofSchemaHash)`
3) Buyer creates agreement  
   - Call `A2AEscrow.createAgreement(agreementId, offerId, costCenterId)`
   - Verify event `AgreementCreated`
4) Buyer funds escrow  
   - Call `A2AEscrow.fund(agreementId)`
   - Verify:
     - `EscrowFunded` event
     - escrow contract MNEE balance increased by `price`
5) Seller delivers proof  
   - Call `A2AEscrow.deliver(agreementId, deliveryProof)`
   - Verify `Delivered` event contains proof + disputeBy
6) Buyer accepts  
   - Call `A2AEscrow.accept(agreementId)`
   - Verify `Accepted` event
7) Release payment  
   - Call `A2AEscrow.release(agreementId)`
   - Verify:
     - `Released` event
     - seller balance increased
     - escrow balance decreased

**Pass condition:** escrow final status is `RELEASED`, balances correct.

---

## M2-02 Timeout refund (funded but not delivered)
1) Create + fund agreement
2) Do NOT deliver
3) Advance time beyond SLA (in real chain: wait; in local: time travel)
4) Call `refundIfExpired(agreementId)`
5) Verify `Refunded` and buyer balance restored

---

## M2-03 Dispute path (delivered but buyer disputes)
1) Create + fund agreement
2) Seller delivers proof (could be “wrong” by buyer expectation)
3) Buyer calls `dispute(agreementId)` before disputeBy
4) Resolver admin calls `resolve(agreementId)`
5) Verify `Resolved` event and funds routed (buyerWins or sellerWins)

---

## M2-04 Negative permissions tests
- Seller tries to `fund` → must fail `NOT_BUYER`
- Buyer tries to `deliver` → must fail `NOT_SELLER`
- Release without accept → must fail `BAD_STATUS`

---

# 16) 100% Automation testing (Hardhat)
This is the **actual automated test suite shape**, not “it(…)”. You can copy this into a dev page later, but the logic is complete here.

## 16.1 Test file: `test/a2a-escrow.spec.ts`
```ts
import { expect } from "chai";
import { ethers } from "hardhat";

describe("A2A-CN Escrow", function () {
  async function deploy() {
    const [admin, buyer, seller] = await ethers.getSigners();

    // Simple ERC20 mock for MNEE in local tests
    const M = await ethers.getContractFactory("ERC20Mock");
    const mnee = await M.deploy("MNEE", "MNEE", buyer.address, ethers.parseEther("1000"));

    const R = await ethers.getContractFactory("AgentServiceRegistry");
    const registry = await R.deploy(admin.address);

    const O = await ethers.getContractFactory("OfferRegistry");
    const offers = await O.deploy(admin.address, await registry.getAddress());

    const E = await ethers.getContractFactory("A2AEscrow");
    const escrow = await E.deploy(admin.address, await mnee.getAddress(), await offers.getAddress());

    return { admin, buyer, seller, mnee, registry, offers, escrow };
  }

  it("T2-01 happy path releases payment after accept", async () => {
    const { admin, buyer, seller, mnee, registry, offers, escrow } = await deploy();

    const serviceId = ethers.keccak256(ethers.toUtf8Bytes("svc.dataset.v1"));
    const offerId = ethers.keccak256(ethers.toUtf8Bytes("offer.dataset.25"));
    const agreementId = ethers.keccak256(ethers.toUtf8Bytes("agreement.1"));
    const schemaHash = ethers.keccak256(ethers.toUtf8Bytes("schema"));
    const termsHash = ethers.keccak256(ethers.toUtf8Bytes("terms"));
    const proofSchemaHash = ethers.keccak256(ethers.toUtf8Bytes("proofschema"));
    const costCenterId = ethers.keccak256(ethers.toUtf8Bytes("CC-OPS"));

    await registry.connect(admin).registerService(serviceId, seller.address, schemaHash);
    await offers.connect(admin).publishOffer(offerId, serviceId, ethers.parseEther("25"), 600, 600, termsHash, proofSchemaHash);

    await escrow.connect(buyer).createAgreement(agreementId, offerId, costCenterId);

    await mnee.connect(buyer).approve(await escrow.getAddress(), ethers.parseEther("25"));
    await escrow.connect(buyer).fund(agreementId);

    const proof = ethers.keccak256(ethers.toUtf8Bytes("delivered_hash"));
    await escrow.connect(seller).deliver(agreementId, proof);

    await escrow.connect(buyer).accept(agreementId);

    const sellerBefore = await mnee.balanceOf(seller.address);
    await escrow.connect(buyer).release(agreementId);
    const sellerAfter = await mnee.balanceOf(seller.address);

    expect(sellerAfter - sellerBefore).to.equal(ethers.parseEther("25"));
  });

  it("T2-02 timeout refund returns funds to buyer", async () => {
    const { admin, buyer, seller, mnee, registry, offers, escrow } = await deploy();

    const serviceId = ethers.keccak256(ethers.toUtf8Bytes("svc.inference.v1"));
    const offerId = ethers.keccak256(ethers.toUtf8Bytes("offer.inference.10"));
    const agreementId = ethers.keccak256(ethers.toUtf8Bytes("agreement.2"));
    const schemaHash = ethers.keccak256(ethers.toUtf8Bytes("schema"));
    const termsHash = ethers.keccak256(ethers.toUtf8Bytes("terms"));
    const proofSchemaHash = ethers.keccak256(ethers.toUtf8Bytes("proofschema"));
    const costCenterId = ethers.keccak256(ethers.toUtf8Bytes("CC-AI"));

    await registry.connect(admin).registerService(serviceId, seller.address, schemaHash);
    await offers.connect(admin).publishOffer(offerId, serviceId, ethers.parseEther("10"), 10, 10, termsHash, proofSchemaHash);

    await escrow.connect(buyer).createAgreement(agreementId, offerId, costCenterId);

    await mnee.connect(buyer).approve(await escrow.getAddress(), ethers.parseEther("10"));
    await escrow.connect(buyer).fund(agreementId);

    // move time past SLA
    await ethers.provider.send("evm_increaseTime", [20]);
    await ethers.provider.send("evm_mine", []);

    const buyerBefore = await mnee.balanceOf(buyer.address);
    await escrow.connect(buyer).refundIfExpired(agreementId);
    const buyerAfter = await mnee.balanceOf(buyer.address);

    expect(buyerAfter).to.be.greaterThan(buyerBefore);
  });

  it("T2-03 permissions: only seller can deliver; only buyer can accept", async () => {
    const { admin, buyer, seller, mnee, registry, offers, escrow } = await deploy();
    const [_, attacker] = await ethers.getSigners();

    const serviceId = ethers.keccak256(ethers.toUtf8Bytes("svc.v1"));
    const offerId = ethers.keccak256(ethers.toUtf8Bytes("offer.v1"));
    const agreementId = ethers.keccak256(ethers.toUtf8Bytes("agreement.3"));
    const schemaHash = ethers.keccak256(ethers.toUtf8Bytes("schema"));
    const termsHash = ethers.keccak256(ethers.toUtf8Bytes("terms"));
    const proofSchemaHash = ethers.keccak256(ethers.toUtf8Bytes("proofschema"));
    const costCenterId = ethers.keccak256(ethers.toUtf8Bytes("CC-SEC"));

    await registry.connect(admin).registerService(serviceId, seller.address, schemaHash);
    await offers.connect(admin).publishOffer(offerId, serviceId, ethers.parseEther("1"), 600, 600, termsHash, proofSchemaHash);

    await escrow.connect(buyer).createAgreement(agreementId, offerId, costCenterId);
    await mnee.connect(buyer).approve(await escrow.getAddress(), ethers.parseEther("1"));
    await escrow.connect(buyer).fund(agreementId);

    const proof = ethers.keccak256(ethers.toUtf8Bytes("x"));

    await expect(escrow.connect(attacker).deliver(agreementId, proof)).to.be.revertedWith("NOT_SELLER");
    await escrow.connect(seller).deliver(agreementId, proof);
    await expect(escrow.connect(attacker).accept(agreementId)).to.be.revertedWith("NOT_BUYER");
  });
});
```

> Note: this uses a local `ERC20Mock`. In real deployment, that’s MNEE token.
