# 19) Enterprise extension: Seller/Service Risk Tiers (Commerce-side)
### 19.1 Why risk tiers are mandatory in agent commerce
In A2A commerce, the risk isn’t just “overspend” (Product-1). The risk is:

- **Non-delivery**
- **Wrong delivery / poisoned delivery**
- **Malicious seller**
- **Seller disappears**
- **Collusion / wash trading**
- **Buyer compromise → mass purchases**

So we need deterministic risk classes that drive *contract behavior*.

### 19.2 Risk tiers definition (commerce context)
We define tiers for **seller** and optionally **service**:

| Tier | Meaning | Typical rule outcome |
|---|---|---|
| R0 Trusted | internal / audited vendor-agent | auto-accept allowed, short dispute |
| R1 Known | established seller, good history | normal flow |
| R2 Medium | new seller or new service type | buyer must explicitly accept; longer dispute |
| R3 High | high-value or suspicious seller | multi-approval required; escrow hold; longer dispute |
| R4 Blocked | prohibited | agreement creation blocked |

### 19.3 Deterministic mapping: tier → contract constraints
This is the key: **tier changes rules**, not “human judgement”.

For each tier we control:
- max price multiplier
- minimum SLA seconds (avoid “instant” scams)
- minimum dispute window
- approvals required
- auto-accept allowed or not
- allow/deny

---

# 20) On-chain: RiskTierRegistry for sellers/services
This is the commerce equivalent of Product-1’s risk tier registry.

## 20.1 `SellerRiskTierRegistry.sol`
```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "@openzeppelin/contracts/access/AccessControl.sol";

contract SellerRiskTierRegistry is AccessControl {
    bytes32 public constant RISK_ADMIN_ROLE = keccak256("RISK_ADMIN_ROLE");

    enum Tier { R0, R1, R2, R3, R4 }

    mapping(address => Tier) public sellerTier;
    mapping(bytes32 => Tier) public serviceTier; // optional: per-service override

    event SellerTierSet(address indexed seller, Tier tier);
    event ServiceTierSet(bytes32 indexed serviceId, Tier tier);

    constructor(address admin) {
        _grantRole(DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE, admin);
        _grantRole(RISK_ADMIN_ROLE, admin);
    }

    function setSellerTier(address seller, Tier tier) external onlyRole(RISK_ADMIN_ROLE) {
        sellerTier[seller] = tier;
        emit SellerTierSet(seller, tier);
    }

    function setServiceTier(bytes32 serviceId, Tier tier) external onlyRole(RISK_ADMIN_ROLE) {
        serviceTier[serviceId] = tier;
        emit ServiceTierSet(serviceId, tier);
    }

    function effectiveTier(address seller, bytes32 serviceId) external view returns (Tier) {
        Tier st = serviceTier[serviceId];
        if (uint8(st) != 0) { // if set (note: if you need explicit "unset", add a sentinel)
            return st;
        }
        return sellerTier[seller];
    }
}
```

> Enterprise note: if you need “unset”, use `mapping(bytes32 => bool) isServiceTierSet` to avoid ambiguity.

---

# 21) On-chain: Policy versioning for commerce rules (like Product-1)
### 21.1 Why policy versioning matters here
Offers must not be “mutated” silently. Enterprises require:
- auditability (“what rule applied when we paid?”)
- predictable upgrades (“new rules apply to new agreements only”)

So: **policyId + version** is attached to the offer and then copied into the agreement.

### 21.2 Policy dimensions (commerce)
A commerce policy version controls:
- tier rules (allow/deny, multipliers, min windows)
- max price per tier
- max concurrent agreements per buyer agent
- require buyer approvals for R3+
- allow auto-accept for R0 only
- refund rules on dispute (deterministic)

---

# 22) On-chain: PolicyRules contract (tier rules, versioned)
## 22.1 `CommercePolicyRules.sol`
```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "@openzeppelin/contracts/access/AccessControl.sol";
import "./SellerRiskTierRegistry.sol";

contract CommercePolicyRules is AccessControl {
    bytes32 public constant POLICY_ADMIN_ROLE = keccak256("POLICY_ADMIN_ROLE");

    struct TierRule {
        bool allowed;
        uint16 maxPriceBps;        // multiplier vs offer.price (10000=1x, 5000=0.5x)
        uint32 minSlaSeconds;
        uint32 minDisputeSeconds;
        uint8  minApprovals;       // required approvals at/above this tier
        bool   autoAcceptAllowed;  // if true, protocol can auto-accept (still deterministic)
    }

    // policyId => version => tier => rule
    mapping(uint256 => mapping(uint256 => mapping(SellerRiskTierRegistry.Tier => TierRule))) public rule;

    event TierRuleSet(uint256 indexed policyId, uint256 indexed version, SellerRiskTierRegistry.Tier tier, TierRule tr);

    constructor(address admin) {
        _grantRole(DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE, admin);
        _grantRole(POLICY_ADMIN_ROLE, admin);
    }

    function setTierRule(
        uint256 policyId,
        uint256 version,
        SellerRiskTierRegistry.Tier tier,
        TierRule calldata tr
    ) external onlyRole(POLICY_ADMIN_ROLE) {
        rule[policyId][version][tier] = tr;
        emit TierRuleSet(policyId, version, tier, tr);
    }
}
```

---

# 23) Enterprise extension: Multi-approval for high-risk commerce (R3+)
### 23.1 Why multi-approval is needed in commerce
For R3 sellers/services:
- a single buyer agent should not be allowed to finalize acceptance alone
- approvals create “two-man rule” for high-risk procurements

### 23.2 What approvals apply to (important)
In commerce, approvals can apply to:
- **creating the agreement**
- **funding escrow**
- **accepting delivery**
- **resolving dispute**

Enterprise default:
- approvals gate **funding** and **acceptance**.

---

# 24) On-chain: ApprovalManager (EIP-712 approvals)
We do approvals as signed messages (cheap, auditable).

## 24.1 `ApprovalManager.sol` (conceptual code)
```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "@openzeppelin/contracts/utils/cryptography/ECDSA.sol";

contract ApprovalManager {
    using ECDSA for bytes32;

    // approvalHash => approver => used?
    mapping(bytes32 => mapping(address => bool)) public used;

    function verifyAndConsume(bytes32 approvalHash, address approver, bytes calldata sig) external returns (bool) {
        require(!used[approvalHash][approver], "APPROVAL_USED");
        address signer = approvalHash.toEthSignedMessageHash().recover(sig);
        require(signer == approver, "BAD_APPROVAL_SIG");
        used[approvalHash][approver] = true;
        return true;
    }
}
```

> In a full build, you’d use EIP-712 typed data instead of `toEthSignedMessageHash()`, but the enterprise principle is the same.

---

# 25) Enforcing tier rules at runtime (where exactly it happens)
### 25.1 Enforcement must happen before funds move
Tier rules should be checked:
- at `createAgreement` (deny R4)
- at `fund` (deny if price too high vs tier constraints)
- at `deliver` (deny if after SLA)
- at `accept` (deny auto-accept for non-R0, require approvals for R3)
- at `resolve` (deterministic path)

### 25.2 Deterministic enforcement order (important)
1) Offer active
2) Seller/service tier resolved
3) Policy rule for tier loaded
4) allowed? else deny
5) min SLA/dispute windows enforced (offer must satisfy)
6) approvals required computed
7) state transition allowed
8) funds move

This prevents “partially progressed agreements” that pollute ops.

---

# 26) Upgrade A2AEscrow to be policy+tier aware
Here’s the enterprise modification: agreement stores **policyId/version** and tier at creation time.

## 26.1 Agreement fields added
- `policyId`
- `policyVersion`
- `tierAtCreation`
- `requiredApprovals`
- `approvalsCollected`

---

# 27) Code: `A2AEscrowV2` enforcement sketch (real logic)
Below is the *core* enforcement logic (the parts that matter most).

```solidity
// inside createAgreement(...)
OfferRegistry.Offer memory o = offers.offers(offerId);
require(o.active, "OFFER_INACTIVE");

SellerRiskTierRegistry.Tier tier = risk.effectiveTier(o.seller, o.serviceId);
CommercePolicyRules.TierRule memory tr = policy.rule(policyId, policyVersion, tier);

require(tr.allowed, "TIER_BLOCKED");

// Offer must satisfy minimums
require(o.slaSeconds >= tr.minSlaSeconds, "SLA_TOO_SHORT");
require(o.disputeWindowSeconds >= tr.minDisputeSeconds, "DISPUTE_TOO_SHORT");

agreements[agreementId].tierAtCreation = tier;
agreements[agreementId].policyId = policyId;
agreements[agreementId].policyVersion = policyVersion;

// approvals required locked here
agreements[agreementId].requiredApprovals = tr.minApprovals;
```

```solidity
// inside accept(...)
require(a.status == DELIVERED, "BAD_STATUS");

// If approvals required, validate they were attached/consumed (off-chain signatures)
require(a.approvalsCollected >= a.requiredApprovals, "INSUFFICIENT_APPROVALS");

// Auto-accept logic: only if tr.autoAcceptAllowed AND buyer opted in
```

---

# 28) Enterprise extension: Replay protection for buyer/seller actions
### 28.1 Why replay matters
Attack scenario:
- attacker replays a prior “accept” signature
- or replays deliver proof to wrong agreement

Fix:
- approvals include `agreementId`, `chainId`, `contract address`, `nonce`
- contract consumes signature once (like Product-1’s intent pattern)

---

# 29) Enterprise extension: Rate limiting (anti-flood)
### 29.1 Why
A compromised buyer agent can create 1000 agreements in minutes, even if each is small.

### 29.2 Deterministic rate-limit model
Per buyer:
- max active agreements
- max funded agreements per rolling window
- max total MNEE escrowed per rolling window

This mirrors Product-1 rolling spend windows, but for **commerce agreements**.

---

# 30) On-chain: Rolling window for “active funded escrow”
Use a similar rolling window library as Product-1 (window size + consumed amount).

Concept:
- `fund()` calls consume from buyer’s funded escrow window
- refund/release reduces active exposure (or you track “activeEscrowExposure”)

---

# 31) Enterprise extension: Partial delivery + milestone escrow
### 31.1 Why
Some services are multi-step (data + validation + support).

### 31.2 Model
Escrow supports milestones:
- 40% after dataset hash
- 60% after validation proof

Enterprise-grade because it reduces dispute severity and improves cashflow fairness.

---

# 32) On-chain: Milestone escrow design (data structure)
```solidity
struct Milestone {
  bytes32 proof;
  uint256 amount;
  bool released;
}

mapping(bytes32 => Milestone[]) public milestones;
```

Rules:
- each milestone has its own proof schema hash
- release only when that milestone is accepted

---

# 33) Enterprise extension: Deterministic dispute policies (not “admin decides”)
Your earlier base `resolve()` used a simple rule. Enterprise needs **policy-based determinism**.

Dispute policy examples:
- If seller missed SLA → buyer wins automatically
- If proof schema mismatch → buyer wins
- If buyer disputed after dispute window → seller wins
- If buyer accepted and then disputes → deny

---

# 34) On-chain: DisputePolicyModule interface
```solidity
interface IDisputePolicy {
    function decide(bytes32 agreementId) external view returns (bool buyerWins, bytes32 reason);
}
```

Then `resolve()` calls policy module.  
No human arbitration needed for baseline.

---

# 35) Security hardening: Invariants (must never break)
These are audit-grade invariants:

- **INV2-01:** Funds can only exit escrow via `Released` or `Refunded/Resolved`.
- **INV2-02:** A given `agreementId` cannot be released twice.
- **INV2-03:** Only seller can deliver; only buyer can accept/dispute.
- **INV2-04:** Delivery cannot happen after `deliverBy`.
- **INV2-05:** Dispute cannot happen after `disputeBy`.
- **INV2-06:** Approval signatures cannot be reused.

These become automated tests later.

---

# 36) Event model hardening (for audit + ERP + SIEM)
### 36.1 Why events must be “complete”
Indexers and auditors should reconstruct everything without off-chain guessing.

So events must include:
- agreementId
- offerId
- serviceId
- buyer
- seller
- amount
- tier
- policyId/version
- timestamps
- reason codes on denials

### 36.2 Add “denied” event (enterprise-grade)
Whenever you revert, you lose evidence. Enterprise prefers:
- pre-check function that emits `Denied` and returns false  
or
- custom errors + off-chain decode  
But for SIEM, explicit `Denied` events are gold.

---

# 37) Code: `Denied` event pattern
```solidity
event ActionDenied(bytes32 indexed agreementId, string action, bytes32 reason);

function canFund(bytes32 id) external returns (bool) {
  if (agreements[id].status != Status.CREATED) { emit ActionDenied(id, "FUND", A2AReason.BAD_STATUS); return false; }
  return true;
}
```

> In dev you can choose either revert-only or event-based deny. Enterprise ops love event-based.

---

# 38) Accounting extension: Accrual vs cash posting
### 38.1 Why
Enterprises may post:
- at funding time (escrow asset)
- at release time (expense)
- at dispute resolution time (adjustment)

### 38.2 Accounting decision table
- Funding → move cash to escrow asset
- Release → expense recognized
- Refund → cash restored
- Dispute buyerWins after release (if milestone model) → reversal entry

---

# 39) Accounting extension: Service metadata for GL mapping
Each service should map to:
- GL account
- tax category
- cost center rule

We store only `serviceId` + `costCenterId` on-chain, and keep mapping off-chain in ERP integration.

---

# 40) SIEM extension: Detection rules (enterprise-grade)
Rules you can ship as default:

- **D2-01 Seller Non-Delivery Burst:**  
  `RefundedDueToExpiry > 3 in 1h for same seller → HIGH`

- **D2-02 Dispute Spike:**  
  `Disputed > 5/day for seller → HIGH`

- **D2-03 Buyer Compromise Pattern:**  
  `Funded agreements count > baseline×3 in 10m → CRITICAL`

- **D2-04 Wash Trading:**  
  buyer=seller patterns across wallets (if applicable) → HIGH

---

# 41) Ops runbook: Incident response (expanded like Product-1)
## 41.1 I2-01 Seller non-delivery
1) Identify agreements where `now > deliverBy` and status FUNDED  
2) Call `refundIfExpired()` (can be automated bot)  
3) Mark seller reliability degraded (risk tier to R2/R3)  
4) Block new offers if repeated (tier to R4)  
5) Export evidence: agreementIds + tx hashes

## 41.2 I2-02 Poisoned delivery suspected
1) Buyer disputes immediately  
2) Freeze further purchases from seller by tier escalation  
3) Run off-chain validation pipeline (antivirus, checksum, sandbox)  
4) Resolve dispute per deterministic policy  
5) SIEM incident ticket created

## 41.3 I2-03 Buyer compromise
1) Freeze buyer in Product-1 treasury (ATG) OR commerce rate-limits  
2) Stop new funding by policy update  
3) Rotate buyer agent keys  
4) Review all funded agreements not yet delivered  
5) Refund or dispute as per rules

---

# 42) Integration with Product-1 (ATG) without mixing products
Product-2 can run standalone, but enterprise deployments often route actual funds through Product-1.

Two modes:
- **Mode A (Standalone):** buyer funds escrow directly from buyer wallet  
- **Mode B (Enterprise Treasury):** buyer agent requests funds from ATG policy, then funds escrow

This keeps:
- commerce logic in Product-2
- spend control in Product-1

# 44) Upgrade strategy (immutable vs proxy)
Enterprise options:

**Option 1: Immutable (simpler)**
- deploy new version
- migrate by publishing new offers under new addresses
- old agreements finish on old contracts

**Option 2: Proxy upgrade**
- more complex
- requires timelock + upgrade governance
- stronger “single address” stability

Hackathon: immutable is usually acceptable; enterprise doc must mention both.

- **Tampering:** proof altered → hash proof + signatures prevent
- **Repudiation:** “I didn’t deliver” → on-chain events prevent
- **Information disclosure:** avoid storing plaintext delivery data on-chain
- **Denial of service:** flood agreements → rate-limits + max active
- **Elevation of privilege:** misuse admin roles → least privilege + timelock (optional)

# 61) Enterprise pillar: EIP-712 typed approvals (real, auditable, replay-safe)
### 61.1 Why EIP-712 (not “plain signatures”)
Enterprise approvals must be:
- human-verifiable (typed fields)
- replay-safe (nonce + domain separator)
- contract-specific (verifies chainId + verifyingContract)
- evidence-friendly (audit can inspect exact approval intent)

Plain `toEthSignedMessageHash` is weaker (ambiguous payloads, higher replay risk).

### 61.2 Approval semantics (what approvals approve)
We define approvals for **specific actions**:
- `FUND` approval: permits funding escrow for an agreement
- `ACCEPT` approval: permits acceptance of delivery
- Optional: `RESOLVE` approval (if you choose multi-approval dispute resolution)

Each approval includes:
- agreementId
- buyer
- offerId
- amount
- policyId/version
- risk tier
- deadline
- nonce

---

# 62) On-chain module: EIP-712 ApprovalVerifier (complete baseline)
This is a usable enterprise-grade module.

```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "@openzeppelin/contracts/utils/cryptography/EIP712.sol";
import "@openzeppelin/contracts/utils/cryptography/ECDSA.sol";

contract ApprovalVerifier is EIP712 {
    using ECDSA for bytes32;

    // approver => nonce => used?
    mapping(address => mapping(uint256 => bool)) public usedNonce;

    bytes32 public constant APPROVAL_TYPEHASH = keccak256(
        "Approval(bytes32 agreementId,address buyer,bytes32 offerId,uint256 amount,uint256 policyId,uint256 policyVersion,uint8 action,uint8 tier,uint256 deadline,uint256 nonce)"
    );

    enum Action { FUND, ACCEPT, RESOLVE }

    constructor(string memory name, string memory version)
        EIP712(name, version)
    {}

    function hashApproval(
        bytes32 agreementId,
        address buyer,
        bytes32 offerId,
        uint256 amount,
        uint256 policyId,
        uint256 policyVersion,
        Action action,
        uint8 tier,
        uint256 deadline,
        uint256 nonce
    ) public view returns (bytes32) {
        bytes32 structHash = keccak256(
            abi.encode(
                APPROVAL_TYPEHASH,
                agreementId,
                buyer,
                offerId,
                amount,
                policyId,
                policyVersion,
                uint8(action),
                tier,
                deadline,
                nonce
            )
        );
        return _hashTypedDataV4(structHash);
    }

    function verifyAndConsume(
        address approver,
        bytes32 digest,
        uint256 deadline,
        uint256 nonce,
        bytes calldata signature
    ) external returns (bool) {
        require(block.timestamp <= deadline, "APPROVAL_EXPIRED");
        require(!usedNonce[approver][nonce], "NONCE_USED");
        address signer = digest.recover(signature);
        require(signer == approver, "BAD_APPROVAL_SIG");
        usedNonce[approver][nonce] = true;
        return true;
    }
}
```

Enterprise notes:
- Nonces are **per approver** (so separate approvers don’t collide).
- Approval payload is typed and anchored to chainId + contract via EIP-712.

---

# 63) Wiring approvals into A2AEscrow (real enforcement pattern)
### 63.1 Where approvals are enforced
- **Before funding** for high-tier sellers (R3+)
- **Before accept** for high-tier sellers (R2/R3 depending on policy)

### 63.2 Data model additions in Agreement
Add:
- `uint8 requiredApprovalsFund`
- `uint8 requiredApprovalsAccept`
- `uint8 approvalsFundCount`
- `uint8 approvalsAcceptCount`

### 63.3 Contract function: submit approvals
Store only counts; consume signatures so they are audit evidence in logs.

```solidity
event ApprovalConsumed(bytes32 indexed agreementId, address indexed approver, uint8 action, uint256 nonce);

function submitApprovalFund(
  bytes32 agreementId,
  address approver,
  uint256 nonce,
  uint256 deadline,
  bytes calldata sig
) external {
  Agreement storage a = agreements[agreementId];
  bytes32 digest = approvalVerifier.hashApproval(
      agreementId, a.buyer, a.offerId, a.amount, a.policyId, a.policyVersion,
      ApprovalVerifier.Action.FUND, uint8(a.tierAtCreation), deadline, nonce
  );
  approvalVerifier.verifyAndConsume(approver, digest, deadline, nonce, sig);
  a.approvalsFundCount += 1;
  emit ApprovalConsumed(agreementId, approver, uint8(ApprovalVerifier.Action.FUND), nonce);
}
```

Then in `fund()`:
```solidity
require(a.approvalsFundCount >= a.requiredApprovalsFund, "INSUFFICIENT_APPROVALS_FUND");
```

Same pattern for accept approvals.

---

# 64) Manual tests: EIP-712 approvals (100% step-by-step)
## M2-13 FUND approval required (R3)
1) Set seller tier to R3  
2) Policy sets `minApprovals = 2` for R3  
3) Create agreement  
4) Attempt `fund()` without approvals → expect revert `INSUFFICIENT_APPROVALS_FUND`  
5) Approver A signs typed data (agreementId, buyer, offerId, amount, policy, tier, deadline, nonce)  
6) Call `submitApprovalFund(...)` with sigA → `ApprovalConsumed` emitted  
7) Approver B signs and submit → second `ApprovalConsumed`  
8) Call `fund()` → succeeds  
9) Attempt reuse nonce A → revert `NONCE_USED` (replay protection)

## M2-14 ACCEPT approval required (R3)
1) After delivery, attempt accept without approvals → blocked  
2) Submit approvals (Action.ACCEPT) → accept succeeds

# 66) Enterprise pillar: Exposure window (rolling limits for commerce)
This is Product-1’s “rolling spend” concept adapted for **active escrow exposure**.

### 66.1 Why “exposure” not just “spend”
In commerce:
- you can have large amounts **locked** in escrow
- not yet spent, but at risk
- compromised buyer can lock huge funds quickly

So we constrain:
- max escrowed in window (e.g., 1h)
- max concurrent funded agreements
- per-seller exposure cap
- per-service exposure cap

---

# 67) On-chain module: ExposureWindow (deterministic rolling limit)
This is minimal and enterprise-correct for a hackathon baseline.

```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

contract ExposureWindow {
    struct Window {
        uint256 windowStart;
        uint256 exposure; // amount currently counted in this window
        uint32  activeCount;
    }

    mapping(address => Window) public buyerWindow;

    uint256 public immutable windowSeconds;
    uint256 public maxExposure;      // e.g. 1000 MNEE (in wei)
    uint32  public maxActiveCount;   // e.g. 20

    event ExposureConfigured(uint256 windowSeconds, uint256 maxExposure, uint32 maxActiveCount);

    constructor(uint256 _windowSeconds, uint256 _maxExposure, uint32 _maxActiveCount) {
        windowSeconds = _windowSeconds;
        maxExposure = _maxExposure;
        maxActiveCount = _maxActiveCount;
        emit ExposureConfigured(_windowSeconds, _maxExposure, _maxActiveCount);
    }

    function _roll(address buyer) internal {
        Window storage w = buyerWindow[buyer];
        if (block.timestamp >= w.windowStart + windowSeconds) {
            w.windowStart = block.timestamp;
            w.exposure = 0;
            w.activeCount = 0;
        }
    }

    function canIncrease(address buyer, uint256 amount) external returns (bool) {
        _roll(buyer);
        Window storage w = buyerWindow[buyer];
        if (w.exposure + amount > maxExposure) return false;
        if (w.activeCount + 1 > maxActiveCount) return false;
        return true;
    }

    function increase(address buyer, uint256 amount) external {
        _roll(buyer);
        Window storage w = buyerWindow[buyer];
        require(w.exposure + amount <= maxExposure, "EXPOSURE_LIMIT");
        require(w.activeCount + 1 <= maxActiveCount, "ACTIVE_LIMIT");
        w.exposure += amount;
        w.activeCount += 1;
    }

    function decrease(address buyer, uint256 amount) external {
        // Called on release/refund to reduce exposure (best-effort; keep safe)
        Window storage w = buyerWindow[buyer];
        if (w.exposure >= amount) w.exposure -= amount;
        if (w.activeCount > 0) w.activeCount -= 1;
    }
}
```

Enterprise note:
- This is “windowed” not “true rolling”; it’s acceptable as a deterministic baseline.
- If you want exact rolling (bucketed), we can add bucket arrays, but this is already enterprise-usable for guardrails.

---

# 68) Integrating ExposureWindow into escrow funding
In `fund()`:
1) check `canIncrease(buyer, amount)`
2) `increase(buyer, amount)`
3) lock funds

On release/refund:
- call `decrease(buyer, amount)`

This ensures exposure is bounded and automatically reduced on completion.

# 71) Enterprise pillar: Milestone escrow (multi-stage delivery)
### 71.1 Why milestones are required in real commerce
Many services aren’t “one blob”:
- data delivered first
- validation delivered second
- support delivered third

Milestone escrow:
- reduces disputes
- improves fairness
- supports partial success

---

# 72) Milestone model (deterministic)
Agreement includes milestones:
- each milestone has amount + proof schema hash
- each milestone can be delivered and accepted independently
- settlement occurs per milestone

Rules:
- sum(milestones.amount) == agreement.amount
- milestone i cannot be released twice
- dispute applies to specific milestone (or entire agreement, policy dependent)

---

# 73) On-chain: milestone data structures
```solidity
struct Milestone {
  uint256 amount;
  bytes32 proofSchemaHash;
  bytes32 proof;       // delivered proof
  uint256 deliveredAt;
  uint256 disputeBy;
  bool accepted;
  bool released;
}

mapping(bytes32 => Milestone[]) public agreementMilestones;
```

---

# 74) Milestone functions (enterprise semantics)
- `setMilestones(agreementId, Milestone[] ...)` (only at creation; immutable afterward)
- `deliverMilestone(agreementId, idx, proof)`
- `acceptMilestone(agreementId, idx)`
- `releaseMilestone(agreementId, idx)`
- `refundMilestoneIfExpired(agreementId, idx)` (if milestone SLA missed)

Enterprise note: You can either:
- keep one SLA for agreement (simpler), or
- per-milestone SLA (more precise). Baseline: agreement SLA + dispute windows per milestone.

## M2-18 Milestone dispute only on stage 2

# 77) Enterprise pillar: Dispute policy matrix (deterministic, not “admin picks”)
### 77.1 Why deterministic dispute matrix matters
Enterprises dislike subjective arbitration for machine commerce because:
- it doesn’t scale
- it introduces legal ambiguity
- it creates “admin key risk”

So disputes resolve by **explicit policy matrix**.

---

# 78) Dispute decision matrix (baseline enterprise policy)
Inputs:
- status
- timestamps vs SLA/dispute window
- proof presence
- buyer acceptance status

Outcomes:

| Condition | Winner | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Seller missed SLA (no deliver before deliverBy) | Buyer | `MISS_SLA` |
| Buyer disputes after disputeBy | Seller | `LATE_DISPUTE` |
| Seller proof missing/zero | Buyer | `NO_PROOF` |
| Buyer already accepted milestone | Seller | `ALREADY_ACCEPTED` |
| Delivery on time + proof present + buyer disputes within window | Policy-dependent (e.g., buyerWins if schema mismatch) | `SCHEMA_MISMATCH` |

---

# 79) On-chain: DisputePolicyModule implementation (usable baseline)
```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

interface IA2AView {
    function getAgreement(bytes32 id) external view returns (
        address buyer,
        address seller,
        uint256 amount,
        uint256 fundedAt,
        uint256 deliverBy,
        uint256 disputeBy,
        uint256 deliveryAt,
        bytes32 deliveryProof,
        uint8 status
    );
}

contract DisputePolicyMatrix {
    // Returns (buyerWins, reason)
    function decide(IA2AView esc, bytes32 id) external view returns (bool, bytes32) {
        (
            , , ,
            uint256 fundedAt,
            uint256 deliverBy,
            uint256 disputeBy,
            uint256 deliveryAt,
            bytes32 proof,
            uint8 status
        ) = esc.getAgreement(id);

        // If never delivered and SLA passed -> buyer wins
        if (status == 2 /*FUNDED*/ && block.timestamp > deliverBy) {
            return (true, keccak256("MISS_SLA"));
        }

        // If delivered but proof missing -> buyer wins
        if (status == 3 /*DELIVERED*/ && proof == bytes32(0)) {
            return (true, keccak256("NO_PROOF"));
        }

        // If dispute window passed -> seller wins
        if (block.timestamp > disputeBy) {
            return (false, keccak256("LATE_DISPUTE"));
        }

        // Baseline: if delivered on time and proof present, seller wins
        // (enterprise extension: add schema validation hooks off-chain)
        if (deliveryAt > 0 && deliveryAt <= deliverBy && proof != bytes32(0)) {
            return (false, keccak256("PROOF_PRESENT"));
        }

        // Default conservative: buyer wins
        return (true, keccak256("DEFAULT_BUYER"));
    }
}
```

Enterprise extension:
- “schema mismatch” requires off-chain verification; policy can incorporate an oracle or attestation, but baseline deterministic matrix is still a strong enterprise story.

# 80) Manual tests: dispute matrix
## M2-19 SLA miss auto buyer wins
1) Fund, don’t deliver, wait > deliverBy  
2) Dispute/resolve → buyer wins with reason `MISS_SLA`

## M2-20 Late dispute auto seller wins
1) Deliver  
2) Wait > disputeBy  
3) Dispute attempt → blocked OR resolve → seller wins `LATE_DISPUTE` (depending on whether you allow dispute call after window; enterprise baseline blocks dispute call)

- missed SLA returns buyerWins
- proof missing returns buyerWins
- late dispute returns sellerWins

# 82) Enterprise-grade observability: Event → SIEM → Runbook linkage
### 82.1 Why linkage matters
Security teams don’t just want alerts; they want:
- what to do next
- what evidence to collect
- how to stop recurrence

So each SIEM detection maps to:
- runbook id (I2-xx)
- escalation tier
- evidence list

Example mapping:
- `D2-01 Seller Non-Delivery Burst` → Runbook `I2-01` + evidence (agreementIds, tx hashes)

# 83) Enterprise-grade accounting: Dispute outcomes & adjustments
If milestone escrow exists, accounting must handle:
- partial expense recognition
- partial refunds

Example:
- milestone1 released (expense recognized)
- milestone2 refunded (no expense)
- disputes produce adjustment entries (rare if policy prevents releasing before acceptance)

This is deterministic because each milestone emits events that produce JEs.

# 87) Reason-code taxonomy (complete, enterprise-grade)
### 87.1 Why you need a taxonomy (not random strings)
Enterprise needs:
- consistent failure analytics
- SIEM correlation
- audit explainability
- deterministic customer support responses

So we define a **stable reason-code namespace**.

### 87.2 Canonical reason codes (A2A-CN)
Use `bytes32` hashes of stable strings to prevent typos.

**Offer / Registry**
- `SERVICE_INACTIVE`
- `OFFER_INACTIVE`
- `SELLER_BLOCKED`
- `TIER_BLOCKED`
- `POLICY_NOT_FOUND`

**Agreement state**
- `BAD_STATUS`
- `AGREEMENT_EXISTS`
- `AGREEMENT_NOT_FOUND`
- `AGREEMENT_EXPIRED`

**Actor permissions**
- `NOT_BUYER`
- `NOT_SELLER`
- `NOT_ADMIN`
- `MISSING_ROLE`

**Proof / delivery**
- `PROOF_MISSING`
- `PROOF_SCHEMA_MISMATCH` (if schema validation enabled)
- `DELIVER_LATE`
- `ACCEPT_LATE`

**Dispute**
- `DISPUTE_WINDOW_CLOSED`
- `DISPUTE_NOT_ALLOWED`
- `DISPUTE_ALREADY_OPEN`
- `RESOLUTION_NOT_ALLOWED`

**Approvals**
- `INSUFFICIENT_APPROVALS_FUND`
- `INSUFFICIENT_APPROVALS_ACCEPT`
- `APPROVAL_EXPIRED`
- `NONCE_USED`
- `BAD_APPROVAL_SIG`

**Exposure / throttles**
- `EXPOSURE_LIMIT`
- `ACTIVE_LIMIT`
- `SELLER_EXPOSURE_LIMIT`
- `SERVICE_EXPOSURE_LIMIT`

---

# 88) Deny-event pattern (enterprise observability without losing evidence)
### 88.1 The real problem
If you `revert`, you lose structured evidence unless you decode custom errors off-chain.  
Enterprise ops prefer a **preflight check** that emits a “deny” event with a reason.

### 88.2 How to do it cleanly
Provide parallel “check” endpoints:
- `checkCreateAgreement(...)`
- `checkFund(...)`
- `checkDeliver(...)`
- `checkAccept(...)`
- `checkDispute(...)`

These functions:
- do not change state
- do not move funds
- emit a denial event if false
- return `true/false`

Then your agent runtime calls check first, then executes the real call.

---

# 89) Code: Deny-events + check functions (concrete)
```solidity
event ActionDenied(bytes32 indexed agreementId, string action, bytes32 reason, address actor);

function checkFund(bytes32 id) external returns (bool) {
    Agreement storage a = agreements[id];
    if (a.status != Status.CREATED) { emit ActionDenied(id,"FUND",keccak256("BAD_STATUS"),msg.sender); return false; }
    if (msg.sender != a.buyer) { emit ActionDenied(id,"FUND",keccak256("NOT_BUYER"),msg.sender); return false; }
    if (a.approvalsFundCount < a.requiredApprovalsFund) {
        emit ActionDenied(id,"FUND",keccak256("INSUFFICIENT_APPROVALS_FUND"),msg.sender); return false;
    }
    // exposure checks etc...
    return true;
}
```

Enterprise benefit:
- SIEM can alert on repeated `ActionDenied` bursts
- Audit can show “attempted fraud” patterns

---

# 90) SIEM detections upgraded using deny events
### 90.1 New detection signals (valuable)
- `ActionDenied:FUND:NOT_BUYER` → potential spoofing attempt
- `ActionDenied:ACCEPT:INSUFFICIENT_APPROVALS_ACCEPT` → governance bypass attempt
- `ActionDenied:DELIVER:NOT_SELLER` → malicious actor trying to fake delivery

### 90.2 Severity mapping update
- Permission bypass attempts → HIGH
- Exposure limit hits → MEDIUM (compromise risk)
- Proof missing / deliver late → MEDIUM/HIGH depending on seller tier

---

# 91) Enterprise ops: Roles & responsibilities (who holds what)
You need explicit separation of duties.

### 91.1 Roles (minimum set)
- `DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE` (root) — **timelocked / cold**  
- `REGISTRY_ADMIN_ROLE` — controls service registry (vendor onboarding)  
- `OFFER_ADMIN_ROLE` — controls offer publishing (catalog governance)  
- `RISK_ADMIN_ROLE` — sets seller/service tiers (risk/compliance)  
- `POLICY_ADMIN_ROLE` — sets policy versions (finance/risk board)  
- `ESCROW_ADMIN_ROLE` — dispute resolution module admin (only if needed; prefer deterministic module)

### 91.2 Separation of duties rule (enterprise standard)
- Policy admins should not also be escrow admins  
- Risk admins should not also be offer admins  
- Default admin should not be used day-to-day

---

# 92) Key management (step-by-step runbook, enterprise)
### 92.1 Key types
- **Cold keys**: root admin (rare use)
- **Warm keys**: operational admins (time-bound)
- **Service keys**: seller agent addresses
- **Buyer keys**: buyer agent addresses
- **Approver keys**: human or bot approvers signing EIP-712

### 92.2 Runbook: Initial key setup (step-by-step)
1) Create a cold admin wallet (hardware)  
2) Create operational admin wallets for each role  
3) Deploy contracts with cold admin as default admin  
4) Grant roles to operational wallets  
5) Revoke role from deployer hot wallet  
6) Store role assignments in an audit doc (role → address → owner)

### 92.3 Runbook: Key rotation (step-by-step)
1) Generate new wallet for that role  
2) Grant role to new wallet  
3) Verify role works (dry-run check functions)  
4) Revoke role from old wallet  
5) Record rotation event + reason + date

---

# 93) Break-glass / emergency controls (enterprise)
### 93.1 Why needed
If a vulnerability is found mid-hackathon or in production, you need a deterministic stop.

### 93.2 What “break-glass” means here
- Disable offers globally (set inactive)
- Set seller tier R4 (block)
- Pause new funding (policy denies FUND)
- Allow existing agreements to finish (optional)

### 93.3 Runbook: Emergency freeze
1) Set policy version to deny new FUND for all tiers  
2) Set suspicious sellers to R4  
3) Disable their offers  
4) Announce incident + export evidence

---

# 94) Audit template: Formal invariants checklist (like Product-1 “hardening”)
This is the checklist an auditor/judge would love.

### Invariants (audit-grade)
- **INV2-01** Funds can only move out via Release/Refund/Resolved  
- **INV2-02** Exactly one terminal settlement per agreement (Released XOR Refunded XOR Resolved)  
- **INV2-03** Only buyer can fund/accept/dispute  
- **INV2-04** Only seller can deliver  
- **INV2-05** No deliver after SLA  
- **INV2-06** No dispute after dispute window  
- **INV2-07** EIP-712 approvals cannot be replayed (nonce used once)  
- **INV2-08** Exposure window limits cannot be bypassed  
- **INV2-09** Tier R4 must always block agreement creation/funding  
- **INV2-10** Policy version pinned at agreement creation (cannot change retroactively)

Each invariant gets:
- implementation location (function)
- automation test id

---

# 95) Mapping invariants to code (explicit)
Example mapping:

- INV2-03 enforced in:
  - `fund()` require buyer
  - `accept()` require buyer
  - `dispute()` require buyer

- INV2-05 enforced in:
  - `deliver()` require `block.timestamp <= deliverBy`

- INV2-07 enforced in:
  - `ApprovalVerifier.usedNonce[approver][nonce]`

This is what “enterprise traceability” means.

---

# 96) Gas & performance budgeting (enterprise discipline)
### 96.1 Why this matters
Judges score “engineering quality”.  
Enterprise architecture includes cost predictability.

### 96.2 Gas hot paths
Most-used functions:
- createAgreement
- fund
- deliver
- accept
- release
- refundIfExpired

### 96.3 Expected complexity
- All O(1) per call
- Storage writes dominate gas

### 96.4 Gas minimization principles used
- store hashes, not large strings
- use `bytes32` ids (offerId, serviceId)
- keep proofs as hashes
- avoid on-chain loops except milestones (bounded)

---

# 97) Milestones gas safety rule (enterprise)
If milestone escrow is enabled:
- cap milestones count per agreement (e.g., max 5)
- enforce cap at creation
- each milestone release is O(1)

This prevents denial-of-service via huge arrays.

# 107) `contracts/ApprovalVerifier.sol` (EIP-712 approvals)
```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "@openzeppelin/contracts/utils/cryptography/EIP712.sol";
import "@openzeppelin/contracts/utils/cryptography/ECDSA.sol";

contract ApprovalVerifier is EIP712 {
    using ECDSA for bytes32;

    mapping(address => mapping(uint256 => bool)) public usedNonce;

    bytes32 public constant APPROVAL_TYPEHASH = keccak256(
        "Approval(bytes32 agreementId,address buyer,bytes32 offerId,uint256 amount,uint256 policyId,uint256 policyVersion,uint8 action,uint8 tier,uint256 deadline,uint256 nonce)"
    );

    enum Action { FUND, ACCEPT, RESOLVE }

    constructor(string memory name, string memory version) EIP712(name, version) {}

    function hashApproval(
        bytes32 agreementId,
        address buyer,
        bytes32 offerId,
        uint256 amount,
        uint256 policyId,
        uint256 policyVersion,
        Action action,
        uint8 tier,
        uint256 deadline,
        uint256 nonce
    ) public view returns (bytes32) {
        bytes32 structHash = keccak256(
            abi.encode(
                APPROVAL_TYPEHASH,
                agreementId,
                buyer,
                offerId,
                amount,
                policyId,
                policyVersion,
                uint8(action),
                tier,
                deadline,
                nonce
            )
        );
        return _hashTypedDataV4(structHash);
    }

    function verifyAndConsume(
        address approver,
        bytes32 digest,
        uint256 deadline,
        uint256 nonce,
        bytes calldata signature
    ) external returns (bool) {
        require(block.timestamp <= deadline, "APPROVAL_EXPIRED");
        require(!usedNonce[approver][nonce], "NONCE_USED");
        address signer = digest.recover(signature);
        require(signer == approver, "BAD_APPROVAL_SIG");
        usedNonce[approver][nonce] = true;
        return true;
    }
}
```

# 108) `contracts/ExposureWindow.sol`
```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

contract ExposureWindow {
    struct Window {
        uint256 windowStart;
        uint256 exposure;
        uint32  activeCount;
    }

    mapping(address => Window) public buyerWindow;

    uint256 public immutable windowSeconds;
    uint256 public maxExposure;
    uint32  public maxActiveCount;

    constructor(uint256 _windowSeconds, uint256 _maxExposure, uint32 _maxActiveCount) {
        windowSeconds = _windowSeconds;
        maxExposure = _maxExposure;
        maxActiveCount = _maxActiveCount;
    }

    function _roll(address buyer) internal {
        Window storage w = buyerWindow[buyer];
        if (w.windowStart == 0) {
            w.windowStart = block.timestamp;
        }
        if (block.timestamp >= w.windowStart + windowSeconds) {
            w.windowStart = block.timestamp;
            w.exposure = 0;
            w.activeCount = 0;
        }
    }

    function increase(address buyer, uint256 amount) external {
        _roll(buyer);
        Window storage w = buyerWindow[buyer];
        require(w.exposure + amount <= maxExposure, "EXPOSURE_LIMIT");
        require(w.activeCount + 1 <= maxActiveCount, "ACTIVE_LIMIT");
        w.exposure += amount;
        w.activeCount += 1;
    }

    function decrease(address buyer, uint256 amount) external {
        Window storage w = buyerWindow[buyer];
        if (w.exposure >= amount) w.exposure -= amount;
        if (w.activeCount > 0) w.activeCount -= 1;
    }
}
```

# 109) `contracts/DisputePolicyMatrix.sol`
```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

interface IA2AView {
    function getAgreementView(bytes32 id) external view returns (
        address buyer,
        address seller,
        bytes32 offerId,
        bytes32 serviceId,
        uint256 amount,
        uint256 fundedAt,
        uint256 deliverBy,
        uint256 disputeBy,
        uint256 deliveryAt,
        bytes32 deliveryProof,
        uint8 status // 1=CREATED,2=FUNDED,3=DELIVERED,4=ACCEPTED,5=RELEASED,6=REFUNDED,7=DISPUTED,8=RESOLVED
    );
}

contract DisputePolicyMatrix {
    function decide(IA2AView esc, bytes32 id) external view returns (bool buyerWins, bytes32 reason) {
        (
            ,
            ,
            ,
            ,
            ,
            ,
            uint256 deliverBy,
            uint256 disputeBy,
            uint256 deliveryAt,
            bytes32 proof,
            uint8 status
        ) = esc.getAgreementView(id);

        if (status == 2 /*FUNDED*/ && block.timestamp > deliverBy) {
            return (true, keccak256("MISS_SLA"));
        }
        if (status == 7 /*DISPUTED*/ && block.timestamp > disputeBy) {
            return (false, keccak256("LATE_DISPUTE"));
        }
        if ((status == 3 /*DELIVERED*/ || status == 7 /*DISPUTED*/) && proof == bytes32(0)) {
            return (true, keccak256("NO_PROOF"));
        }
        if (deliveryAt > 0 && deliveryAt <= deliverBy && proof != bytes32(0)) {
            return (false, keccak256("PROOF_PRESENT"));
        }
        return (true, keccak256("DEFAULT_BUYER"));
    }
}
```

# 110) `contracts/A2AEscrowEnterprise.sol` (the glue used by scripts/tests)
This version supports: **offer active check, SLA/dispute windows, EIP-712 approvals, exposure window, tier R4 block, dispute matrix policy**.

```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "@openzeppelin/contracts/token/ERC20/utils/SafeERC20.sol";
import "@openzeppelin/contracts/access/AccessControl.sol";

import "./OfferRegistry.sol";
import "./SellerRiskTierRegistry.sol";
import "./CommercePolicyRules.sol";
import "./ApprovalVerifier.sol";
import "./ExposureWindow.sol";
import "./DisputePolicyMatrix.sol";

contract A2AEscrowEnterprise is AccessControl, IA2AView {
    using SafeERC20 for IERC20;

    bytes32 public constant ESCROW_ADMIN_ROLE = keccak256("ESCROW_ADMIN_ROLE");

    IERC20 public immutable mnee;
    OfferRegistry public immutable offers;
    SellerRiskTierRegistry public immutable risk;
    CommercePolicyRules public immutable policyRules;
    ApprovalVerifier public immutable approvals;
    ExposureWindow public immutable exposure;
    DisputePolicyMatrix public immutable disputePolicy;

    enum Status { NONE, CREATED, FUNDED, DELIVERED, ACCEPTED, RELEASED, REFUNDED, DISPUTED, RESOLVED }

    struct Agreement {
        bytes32 id;
        bytes32 offerId;
        bytes32 serviceId;
        address buyer;
        address seller;
        uint256 amount;

        uint256 policyId;
        uint256 policyVersion;

        SellerRiskTierRegistry.Tier tierAtCreation;

        uint256 fundedAt;
        uint256 deliverBy;
        uint256 deliveryAt;
        bytes32 deliveryProof;
        uint256 disputeBy;

        uint8 requiredApprovalsFund;
        uint8 requiredApprovalsAccept;
        uint8 approvalsFundCount;
        uint8 approvalsAcceptCount;

        Status status;
    }

    mapping(bytes32 => Agreement) public agreements;

    event AgreementCreated(bytes32 indexed id, bytes32 indexed offerId, address indexed buyer, address seller, uint256 amount, uint256 policyId, uint256 policyVersion, uint8 tier);
    event EscrowFunded(bytes32 indexed id, uint256 fundedAt, uint256 deliverBy);
    event Delivered(bytes32 indexed id, bytes32 proof, uint256 deliveredAt, uint256 disputeBy);
    event Accepted(bytes32 indexed id);
    event Released(bytes32 indexed id, uint256 amount);
    event Refunded(bytes32 indexed id, uint256 amount);
    event Disputed(bytes32 indexed id, address indexed buyer);
    event Resolved(bytes32 indexed id, bool buyerWins, bytes32 reason);

    event ApprovalConsumed(bytes32 indexed id, address indexed approver, uint8 action, uint256 nonce);

    constructor(
        address admin,
        address mneeToken,
        address offerRegistry,
        address riskRegistry,
        address policyRules_,
        address approvalVerifier,
        address exposureWindow,
        address disputePolicy_
    ) {
        _grantRole(DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE, admin);
        _grantRole(ESCROW_ADMIN_ROLE, admin);

        mnee = IERC20(mneeToken);
        offers = OfferRegistry(offerRegistry);
        risk = SellerRiskTierRegistry(riskRegistry);
        policyRules = CommercePolicyRules(policyRules_);
        approvals = ApprovalVerifier(approvalVerifier);
        exposure = ExposureWindow(exposureWindow);
        disputePolicy = DisputePolicyMatrix(disputePolicy_);
    }

    function createAgreement(bytes32 id, bytes32 offerId, uint256 policyId, uint256 policyVersion) external {
        require(agreements[id].status == Status.NONE, "AGREEMENT_EXISTS");

        OfferRegistry.Offer memory o = offers.offers(offerId);
        require(o.active, "OFFER_INACTIVE");

        SellerRiskTierRegistry.Tier tier = risk.effectiveTier(o.seller, o.serviceId);

        // block R4 outright
        require(uint8(tier) != uint8(SellerRiskTierRegistry.Tier.R4), "TIER_BLOCKED");

        CommercePolicyRules.TierRule memory tr = policyRules.rule(policyId, policyVersion, tier);
        require(tr.allowed, "TIER_BLOCKED");

        require(o.slaSeconds >= tr.minSlaSeconds, "SLA_TOO_SHORT");
        require(o.disputeWindowSeconds >= tr.minDisputeSeconds, "DISPUTE_TOO_SHORT");

        agreements[id] = Agreement({
            id: id,
            offerId: offerId,
            serviceId: o.serviceId,
            buyer: msg.sender,
            seller: o.seller,
            amount: o.price,
            policyId: policyId,
            policyVersion: policyVersion,
            tierAtCreation: tier,
            fundedAt: 0,
            deliverBy: 0,
            deliveryAt: 0,
            deliveryProof: bytes32(0),
            disputeBy: 0,
            requiredApprovalsFund: tr.minApprovals,
            requiredApprovalsAccept: tr.minApprovals,
            approvalsFundCount: 0,
            approvalsAcceptCount: 0,
            status: Status.CREATED
        });

        emit AgreementCreated(id, offerId, msg.sender, o.seller, o.price, policyId, policyVersion, uint8(tier));
    }

    function submitApproval(bytes32 id, address approver, ApprovalVerifier.Action action, uint256 nonce, uint256 deadline, bytes calldata sig) external {
        Agreement storage a = agreements[id];
        require(a.status != Status.NONE, "AGREEMENT_NOT_FOUND");

        bytes32 digest = approvals.hashApproval(
            id, a.buyer, a.offerId, a.amount, a.policyId, a.policyVersion, action, uint8(a.tierAtCreation), deadline, nonce
        );

        approvals.verifyAndConsume(approver, digest, deadline, nonce, sig);

        if (action == ApprovalVerifier.Action.FUND) a.approvalsFundCount += 1;
        else if (action == ApprovalVerifier.Action.ACCEPT) a.approvalsAcceptCount += 1;

        emit ApprovalConsumed(id, approver, uint8(action), nonce);
    }

    function fund(bytes32 id) external {
        Agreement storage a = agreements[id];
        require(a.status == Status.CREATED, "BAD_STATUS");
        require(msg.sender == a.buyer, "NOT_BUYER");

        OfferRegistry.Offer memory o = offers.offers(a.offerId);
        require(o.active, "OFFER_INACTIVE");

        require(a.approvalsFundCount >= a.requiredApprovalsFund, "INSUFFICIENT_APPROVALS_FUND");

        exposure.increase(a.buyer, a.amount);

        a.fundedAt = block.timestamp;
        a.deliverBy = block.timestamp + o.slaSeconds;
        a.status = Status.FUNDED;

        mnee.safeTransferFrom(a.buyer, address(this), a.amount);

        emit EscrowFunded(id, a.fundedAt, a.deliverBy);
    }

    function deliver(bytes32 id, bytes32 proof) external {
        Agreement storage a = agreements[id];
        require(a.status == Status.FUNDED, "BAD_STATUS");
        require(msg.sender == a.seller, "NOT_SELLER");
        require(block.timestamp <= a.deliverBy, "AGREEMENT_EXPIRED");
        require(proof != bytes32(0), "PROOF_MISSING");

        OfferRegistry.Offer memory o = offers.offers(a.offerId);

        a.deliveryProof = proof;
        a.deliveryAt = block.timestamp;
        a.disputeBy = block.timestamp + o.disputeWindowSeconds;
        a.status = Status.DELIVERED;

        emit Delivered(id, proof, a.deliveryAt, a.disputeBy);
    }

    function accept(bytes32 id) external {
        Agreement storage a = agreements[id];
        require(a.status == Status.DELIVERED, "BAD_STATUS");
        require(msg.sender == a.buyer, "NOT_BUYER");
        require(block.timestamp <= a.disputeBy, "DISPUTE_WINDOW_CLOSED");
        require(a.approvalsAcceptCount >= a.requiredApprovalsAccept, "INSUFFICIENT_APPROVALS_ACCEPT");

        a.status = Status.ACCEPTED;
        emit Accepted(id);
    }

    function release(bytes32 id) external {
        Agreement storage a = agreements[id];
        require(a.status == Status.ACCEPTED, "BAD_STATUS");

        a.status = Status.RELEASED;
        mnee.safeTransfer(a.seller, a.amount);
        exposure.decrease(a.buyer, a.amount);

        emit Released(id, a.amount);
    }

    function refundIfExpired(bytes32 id) external {
        Agreement storage a = agreements[id];
        require(a.status == Status.FUNDED, "BAD_STATUS");
        require(block.timestamp > a.deliverBy, "NOT_EXPIRED");

        a.status = Status.REFUNDED;
        mnee.safeTransfer(a.buyer, a.amount);
        exposure.decrease(a.buyer, a.amount);

        emit Refunded(id, a.amount);
    }

    function dispute(bytes32 id) external {
        Agreement storage a = agreements[id];
        require(a.status == Status.DELIVERED, "BAD_STATUS");
        require(msg.sender == a.buyer, "NOT_BUYER");
        require(block.timestamp <= a.disputeBy, "DISPUTE_WINDOW_CLOSED");

        a.status = Status.DISPUTED;
        emit Disputed(id, msg.sender);
    }

    function resolve(bytes32 id) external onlyRole(ESCROW_ADMIN_ROLE) {
        Agreement storage a = agreements[id];
        require(a.status == Status.DISPUTED, "BAD_STATUS");

        (bool buyerWins, bytes32 reason) = disputePolicy.decide(this, id);

        a.status = Status.RESOLVED;
        if (buyerWins) {
            mnee.safeTransfer(a.buyer, a.amount);
        } else {
            mnee.safeTransfer(a.seller, a.amount);
        }
        exposure.decrease(a.buyer, a.amount);

        emit Resolved(id, buyerWins, reason);
    }

    // IA2AView
    function getAgreementView(bytes32 id) external view returns (
        address buyer,
        address seller,
        bytes32 offerId,
        bytes32 serviceId,
        uint256 amount,
        uint256 fundedAt,
        uint256 deliverBy,
        uint256 disputeBy,
        uint256 deliveryAt,
        bytes32 deliveryProof,
        uint8 status
    ) {
        Agreement storage a = agreements[id];
        return (a.buyer, a.seller, a.offerId, a.serviceId, a.amount, a.fundedAt, a.deliverBy, a.disputeBy, a.deliveryAt, a.deliveryProof, uint8(a.status));
    }
}
```

# 111) `scripts/deploy.ts` (full)
```ts
import { ethers } from "hardhat";

async function main() {
  const [admin] = await ethers.getSigners();

  // 1) Deploy ERC20 mock as local MNEE
  const ERC20Mock = await ethers.getContractFactory("ERC20Mock");
  const mnee = await ERC20Mock.deploy("MNEE", "MNEE", admin.address, ethers.parseEther("1000000"));
  await mnee.waitForDeployment();

  // 2) Core registries
  const AgentServiceRegistry = await ethers.getContractFactory("AgentServiceRegistry");
  const registry = await AgentServiceRegistry.deploy(admin.address);
  await registry.waitForDeployment();

  const OfferRegistry = await ethers.getContractFactory("OfferRegistry");
  const offers = await OfferRegistry.deploy(admin.address, await registry.getAddress());
  await offers.waitForDeployment();

  // 3) Risk + Policy
  const SellerRiskTierRegistry = await ethers.getContractFactory("SellerRiskTierRegistry");
  const risk = await SellerRiskTierRegistry.deploy(admin.address);
  await risk.waitForDeployment();

  const CommercePolicyRules = await ethers.getContractFactory("CommercePolicyRules");
  const policy = await CommercePolicyRules.deploy(admin.address);
  await policy.waitForDeployment();

  // 4) EIP-712 approvals
  const ApprovalVerifier = await ethers.getContractFactory("ApprovalVerifier");
  const approval = await ApprovalVerifier.deploy("A2A-CN", "1");
  await approval.waitForDeployment();

  // 5) Exposure window (1 hour, 10,000 MNEE max, 50 active)
  const ExposureWindow = await ethers.getContractFactory("ExposureWindow");
  const exposure = await ExposureWindow.deploy(3600, ethers.parseEther("10000"), 50);
  await exposure.waitForDeployment();

  // 6) Dispute policy
  const DisputePolicyMatrix = await ethers.getContractFactory("DisputePolicyMatrix");
  const disputePolicy = await DisputePolicyMatrix.deploy();
  await disputePolicy.waitForDeployment();

  // 7) Escrow enterprise
  const A2AEscrowEnterprise = await ethers.getContractFactory("A2AEscrowEnterprise");
  const escrow = await A2AEscrowEnterprise.deploy(
    admin.address,
    await mnee.getAddress(),
    await offers.getAddress(),
    await risk.getAddress(),
    await policy.getAddress(),
    await approval.getAddress(),
    await exposure.getAddress(),
    await disputePolicy.getAddress()
  );
  await escrow.waitForDeployment();

  console.log("DEPLOYED:");
  console.log("MNEE:", await mnee.getAddress());
  console.log("ServiceRegistry:", await registry.getAddress());
  console.log("OfferRegistry:", await offers.getAddress());
  console.log("RiskRegistry:", await risk.getAddress());
  console.log("PolicyRules:", await policy.getAddress());
  console.log("ApprovalVerifier:", await approval.getAddress());
  console.log("ExposureWindow:", await exposure.getAddress());
  console.log("DisputePolicy:", await disputePolicy.getAddress());
  console.log("Escrow:", await escrow.getAddress());
}

main().catch((e) => {
  console.error(e);
  process.exit(1);
});
```

# 112) `scripts/demo_happy_path.ts` (full, prints evidence)
```ts
import { ethers } from "hardhat";

function b32(s: string) {
  return ethers.keccak256(ethers.toUtf8Bytes(s));
}

async function main() {
  const [admin, buyer, seller, approverA, approverB] = await ethers.getSigners();

  // Deploy stack (reuse deploy logic inline to keep demo self-contained)
  const ERC20Mock = await ethers.getContractFactory("ERC20Mock");
  const mnee = await ERC20Mock.deploy("MNEE", "MNEE", buyer.address, ethers.parseEther("1000"));
  await mnee.waitForDeployment();
  await mnee.mint(approverA.address, ethers.parseEther("1"));
  await mnee.mint(approverB.address, ethers.parseEther("1"));

  const R = await ethers.getContractFactory("AgentServiceRegistry");
  const registry = await R.deploy(admin.address);
  await registry.waitForDeployment();

  const O = await ethers.getContractFactory("OfferRegistry");
  const offers = await O.deploy(admin.address, await registry.getAddress());
  await offers.waitForDeployment();

  const Risk = await ethers.getContractFactory("SellerRiskTierRegistry");
  const risk = await Risk.deploy(admin.address);
  await risk.waitForDeployment();

  const Policy = await ethers.getContractFactory("CommercePolicyRules");
  const policy = await Policy.deploy(admin.address);
  await policy.waitForDeployment();

  const Approval = await ethers.getContractFactory("ApprovalVerifier");
  const approval = await Approval.deploy("A2A-CN", "1");
  await approval.waitForDeployment();

  const Exposure = await ethers.getContractFactory("ExposureWindow");
  const exposure = await Exposure.deploy(3600, ethers.parseEther("100"), 10);
  await exposure.waitForDeployment();

  const DP = await ethers.getContractFactory("DisputePolicyMatrix");
  const disputePolicy = await DP.deploy();
  await disputePolicy.waitForDeployment();

  const Esc = await ethers.getContractFactory("A2AEscrowEnterprise");
  const escrow = await Esc.deploy(
    admin.address,
    await mnee.getAddress(),
    await offers.getAddress(),
    await risk.getAddress(),
    await policy.getAddress(),
    await approval.getAddress(),
    await exposure.getAddress(),
    await disputePolicy.getAddress()
  );
  await escrow.waitForDeployment();

  // --- Configure policy for R3: allowed, minApprovals=2, minSLA/dispute
  const policyId = 1n;
  const version = 1n;

  // TierRule struct: allowed, maxPriceBps, minSlaSeconds, minDisputeSeconds, minApprovals, autoAcceptAllowed
  await policy.connect(admin).setTierRule(policyId, version, 3, {
    allowed: true,
    maxPriceBps: 10000,
    minSlaSeconds: 1,
    minDisputeSeconds: 1,
    minApprovals: 2,
    autoAcceptAllowed: false
  });

  // --- Register service + offer
  const serviceId = b32("svc.dataset.v1");
  const offerId = b32("offer.dataset.25");
  const agreementId = b32("agreement.demo.1");

  const schemaHash = b32("schema");
  await registry.connect(admin).registerService(serviceId, seller.address, schemaHash);

  // Set seller tier R3
  await risk.connect(admin).setSellerTier(seller.address, 3);

  const termsHash = b32("terms");
  const proofSchemaHash = b32("proofschema");
  await offers.connect(admin).publishOffer(
    offerId,
    serviceId,
    ethers.parseEther("25"),
    600,
    600,
    termsHash,
    proofSchemaHash
  );

  // --- Create agreement
  await escrow.connect(buyer).createAgreement(agreementId, offerId, policyId, version);

  // --- Create approvals (EIP-712 typed)
  const chainId = (await ethers.provider.getNetwork()).chainId;
  const domain = {
    name: "A2A-CN",
    version: "1",
    chainId,
    verifyingContract: await approval.getAddress()
  };

  const types = {
    Approval: [
      { name: "agreementId", type: "bytes32" },
      { name: "buyer", type: "address" },
      { name: "offerId", type: "bytes32" },
      { name: "amount", type: "uint256" },
      { name: "policyId", type: "uint256" },
      { name: "policyVersion", type: "uint256" },
      { name: "action", type: "uint8" },
      { name: "tier", type: "uint8" },
      { name: "deadline", type: "uint256" },
      { name: "nonce", type: "uint256" }
    ]
  };

  const deadline = BigInt(Math.floor(Date.now() / 1000) + 3600);
  const tier = 3;

  async function signApproval(signer: any, nonce: bigint, action: number) {
    const value = {
      agreementId,
      buyer: buyer.address,
      offerId,
      amount: ethers.parseEther("25"),
      policyId,
      policyVersion: version,
      action,
      tier,
      deadline,
      nonce
    };
    return signer.signTypedData(domain, types, value);
  }

  const sigA = await signApproval(approverA, 1n, 0); // FUND
  const sigB = await signApproval(approverB, 2n, 0); // FUND

  await escrow.submitApproval(agreementId, approverA.address, 0, 1n, deadline, sigA);
  await escrow.submitApproval(agreementId, approverB.address, 0, 2n, deadline, sigB);

  // Buyer approves MNEE spending then funds
  await mnee.connect(buyer).approve(await escrow.getAddress(), ethers.parseEther("25"));

  const buyerBalBefore = await mnee.balanceOf(buyer.address);
  const sellerBalBefore = await mnee.balanceOf(seller.address);

  await escrow.connect(buyer).fund(agreementId);

  // Deliver + accept requires approvals too (R3 policy minApprovals=2)
  const sigA2 = await signApproval(approverA, 3n, 1); // ACCEPT
  const sigB2 = await signApproval(approverB, 4n, 1); // ACCEPT
  await escrow.submitApproval(agreementId, approverA.address, 1, 3n, deadline, sigA2);
  await escrow.submitApproval(agreementId, approverB.address, 1, 4n, deadline, sigB2);

  const proof = b32("delivered_hash");
  await escrow.connect(seller).deliver(agreementId, proof);
  await escrow.connect(buyer).accept(agreementId);
  await escrow.connect(buyer).release(agreementId);

  const buyerBalAfter = await mnee.balanceOf(buyer.address);
  const sellerBalAfter = await mnee.balanceOf(seller.address);

  console.log("EVIDENCE:");
  console.log("Escrow:", await escrow.getAddress());
  console.log("AgreementId:", agreementId);
  console.log("Buyer before/after:", buyerBalBefore.toString(), buyerBalAfter.toString());
  console.log("Seller before/after:", sellerBalBefore.toString(), sellerBalAfter.toString());
}

main().catch((e) => {
  console.error(e);
  process.exit(1);
});
```

# 113) `scripts/demo_denials.ts` (full: shows enterprise denial cases)
```ts
import { ethers } from "hardhat";

function b32(s: string) { return ethers.keccak256(ethers.toUtf8Bytes(s)); }

async function main() {
  const [admin, buyer, seller, attacker, approverA, approverB] = await ethers.getSigners();

  const ERC20Mock = await ethers.getContractFactory("ERC20Mock");
  const mnee = await ERC20Mock.deploy("MNEE", "MNEE", buyer.address, ethers.parseEther("1000"));
  await mnee.waitForDeployment();

  const R = await ethers.getContractFactory("AgentServiceRegistry");
  const registry = await R.deploy(admin.address);
  await registry.waitForDeployment();

  const O = await ethers.getContractFactory("OfferRegistry");
  const offers = await O.deploy(admin.address, await registry.getAddress());
  await offers.waitForDeployment();

  const Risk = await ethers.getContractFactory("SellerRiskTierRegistry");
  const risk = await Risk.deploy(admin.address);
  await risk.waitForDeployment();

  const Policy = await ethers.getContractFactory("CommercePolicyRules");
  const policy = await Policy.deploy(admin.address);
  await policy.waitForDeployment();

  const Approval = await ethers.getContractFactory("ApprovalVerifier");
  const approval = await Approval.deploy("A2A-CN", "1");
  await approval.waitForDeployment();

  const Exposure = await ethers.getContractFactory("ExposureWindow");
  const exposure = await Exposure.deploy(3600, ethers.parseEther("10"), 1); // tiny limits to trigger
  await exposure.waitForDeployment();

  const DP = await ethers.getContractFactory("DisputePolicyMatrix");
  const disputePolicy = await DP.deploy();
  await disputePolicy.waitForDeployment();

  const Esc = await ethers.getContractFactory("A2AEscrowEnterprise");
  const escrow = await Esc.deploy(
    admin.address,
    await mnee.getAddress(),
    await offers.getAddress(),
    await risk.getAddress(),
    await policy.getAddress(),
    await approval.getAddress(),
    await exposure.getAddress(),
    await disputePolicy.getAddress()
  );
  await escrow.waitForDeployment();

  const policyId = 1n, version = 1n;
  // Allow R1 with zero approvals; allow R3 with 2 approvals
  await policy.connect(admin).setTierRule(policyId, version, 1, {
    allowed: true, maxPriceBps: 10000, minSlaSeconds: 1, minDisputeSeconds: 1, minApprovals: 0, autoAcceptAllowed: false
  });
  await policy.connect(admin).setTierRule(policyId, version, 3, {
    allowed: true, maxPriceBps: 10000, minSlaSeconds: 1, minDisputeSeconds: 1, minApprovals: 2, autoAcceptAllowed: false
  });

  const serviceId = b32("svc.v1");
  const offerId = b32("offer.v1");
  const agreementId = b32("agreement.denials.1");

  await registry.connect(admin).registerService(serviceId, seller.address, b32("schema"));
  await risk.connect(admin).setSellerTier(seller.address, 1); // R1

  await offers.connect(admin).publishOffer(offerId, serviceId, ethers.parseEther("9"), 2, 2, b32("terms"), b32("proofschema"));

  await escrow.connect(buyer).createAgreement(agreementId, offerId, policyId, version);

  // Denial 1: attacker funds (not buyer)
  await mnee.connect(buyer).approve(await escrow.getAddress(), ethers.parseEther("9"));
  await escrow.connect(buyer).fund(agreementId);
  console.log("Funded. Now attempt deliver by attacker (NOT_SELLER)...");
  await escrow.connect(attacker).deliver(agreementId, b32("x")).catch(e => console.log("Expected deliver denial:", e.message));

  // Denial 2: deliver after SLA
  const agreementId2 = b32("agreement.denials.2");
  await escrow.connect(buyer).createAgreement(agreementId2, offerId, policyId, version);
  await mnee.connect(buyer).approve(await escrow.getAddress(), ethers.parseEther("9"));
  await escrow.connect(buyer).fund(agreementId2);
  await ethers.provider.send("evm_increaseTime", [3]);
  await ethers.provider.send("evm_mine", []);
  await escrow.connect(seller).deliver(agreementId2, b32("late")).catch(e => console.log("Expected late deliver:", e.message));

  // Denial 3: exposure limit (maxExposure=10, activeCount=1)
  const offerIdBig = b32("offer.big");
  await offers.connect(admin).publishOffer(offerIdBig, serviceId, ethers.parseEther("9"), 10, 10, b32("t"), b32("p"));
  const agreementId3 = b32("agreement.denials.3");
  await escrow.connect(buyer).createAgreement(agreementId3, offerIdBig, policyId, version);
  await mnee.connect(buyer).approve(await escrow.getAddress(), ethers.parseEther("9"));
  await escrow.connect(buyer).fund(agreementId3).catch(e => console.log("Expected exposure/active limit:", e.message));

  console.log("Done.");
}

main().catch((e) => { console.error(e); process.exit(1); });
```
