---

# 1) Executive intent (what CPI is)
**CPI** is a **stablecoin-native checkout + paywall + split payouts + settlement automation** system for creators, teams, marketplaces, and agent-driven commerce — using **MNEE ERC-20** as the settlement asset.

It’s not “a donate button”.  
It’s a **mini Stripe + Patreon + marketplace settlement** but **on-chain**, **auditable**, and **automatable**.

---

# 2) Pain points (why this exists)
## 2.1 Creators lose money & time because:
- **High fees** (platform + card + FX + chargebacks)
- **Payout delays** (weekly/monthly)
- **Fragmented tools** (paywall, invoicing, split payouts all separate)
- **No transparent revenue share** (team disputes)
- **No automated compliance** (business rules, withholding, audit evidence)

## 2.2 Who suffers (enterprise mapping)
- **Creator / SME**: cash flow unpredictability, admin burden  
- **Marketplace Ops**: manual split payout reconciliation  
- **Finance**: missing evidence + inconsistent ledger  
- **Compliance**: poor audit trail, ad hoc refunds/disputes  
- **Customer (buyer)**: friction, cancellations, unclear access rights

## 2.3 Measurable loss examples
- 3–7% platform+payment fees + FX spread
- 7–30 days payout latency (working capital hit)
- 2–5 hours/week admin per creator (invoicing + payout chasing)
- Team disputes = lost output + churn

---

# 3) Customers & users
**Primary users**
- Creators (solo, teams)
- Small studios (design/dev/music/education)
- Marketplaces selling digital goods/services
- Communities/DAOs paying contributors

**Secondary users**
- Buyers/subscribers
- Finance/accounting
- Support/ops (refunds, disputes)

---

# 4) Core use cases
1) **Checkout**: buyer pays MNEE for item/subscription  
2) **Paywall unlock**: content access granted on confirmed payment  
3) **Revenue splits**: auto distribution to creator + collaborators + platform fee  
4) **Refunds/disputes**: governed refund window, optional escrow  
5) **Batch payouts**: scheduled settlement, reporting, audit exports  
6) **Agent payments**: bots can purchase API/data/services with policy rules

---

# 5) Scenarios (enterprise-grade)
## S1 — Digital download sale
- Buyer pays MNEE → CPI mints “purchase entitlement” → splits payouts

## S2 — Subscription paywall (monthly)
- Buyer pays monthly MNEE → entitlement renewed → cancel stops renewal

## S3 — Team revenue split
- 70% creator, 20% editor, 10% platform fee → immutable split contract

## S4 — Dispute window
- Funds escrowed 24h → creator delivers → release; or refund allowed

## S5 — Marketplace settlement
- Many purchases → batch settle to multiple payees + accounting CSV export

---

# 6) Requirements (functional)
**R1 Checkout**: accept MNEE ERC-20 payments  
**R2 Paywall**: grant/revoke entitlement deterministically  
**R3 Splits**: immutable split config, auditable distributions  
**R4 Refund**: controlled refund policy + anti-fraud controls  
**R5 Subscriptions**: recurring billing (two modes)  
**R6 Evidence**: events for every decision (audit pack)  
**R7 Operations**: admin runbook + risk tiers + freeze/incident modes

---

# 7) Requirements (non-functional)
- **Deterministic**: no off-chain “trust me”
- **Security**: reentrancy safe, access control strict
- **Scalable**: O(1) mappings, no loops per payout except batch jobs
- **UX**: minimal steps checkout
- **Testable**: full Hardhat test suite

---

# 8) Architecture (high-level)
### On-chain (Solidity)
1) `CreatorRegistry` — creator profiles, payout address, compliance flags  
2) `ProductCatalog` — products (one-time or subscription), pricing, metadata hash  
3) `EntitlementNFT` (or ERC1155) — access rights minted/burned  
4) `CheckoutRouter` — payment collection, refund logic, calls split settlement  
5) `SplitWallet` (minimal clone pattern) — immutable split distribution  
6) `DisputeEscrow` (optional) — hold funds for dispute window  
7) `PayoutBatcher` — operational settlement + reporting events  
8) `IncidentController` — global freeze (reuse from Product-3)

### Off-chain (enterprise app)
- Next.js + wagmi/viem + ethers.js
- “Creator Console” (create product, split config, view sales)
- “Buyer Checkout” (pay + unlock)
- Exports: ledger CSV / JSON

---

# 9) Why these dev tools (resource mapping)
- **Hardhat**: industry standard local dev + tests
- **OpenZeppelin**: AccessControl, SafeERC20, ReentrancyGuard, ERC721/1155
- **ethers.js**: signing + contract calls
- **wagmi/viem**: modern wallet connection + React hooks
- **MetaMask/Coinbase/Rainbow**: broad wallet support

Why not others:
- Foundry is great but Hardhat is more common for hackathon onboarding + TS tests
- Custom ERC20 handling is unsafe; SafeERC20 is standard

---

# 10) Contract set (ENTERPRISE v1)
Below is a **complete minimal but enterprise-structured** contract suite.  
(You can extend to upgradeable/proxies later; hackathon version uses direct deployments.)

---

## 11) `contracts/CreatorRegistry.sol`
```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "@openzeppelin/contracts/access/AccessControl.sol";

contract CreatorRegistry is AccessControl {
  bytes32 public constant REGISTRY_ADMIN_ROLE = keccak256("REGISTRY_ADMIN_ROLE");

  struct Creator {
    address payout;
    bytes32 metadataHash; // IPFS/Arweave hash of profile
    bool kycFlag;         // optional compliance indicator (no PII on-chain)
    bool active;
  }

  mapping(address => Creator) public creators;

  event CreatorRegistered(address indexed creator, address payout, bytes32 metadataHash);
  event CreatorUpdated(address indexed creator, address payout, bytes32 metadataHash, bool kycFlag, bool active);

  constructor(address admin) {
    _grantRole(DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE, admin);
    _grantRole(REGISTRY_ADMIN_ROLE, admin);
  }

  function register(address payout, bytes32 metadataHash) external {
    require(payout != address(0), "BAD_PAYOUT");
    Creator storage c = creators[msg.sender];
    require(c.payout == address(0), "ALREADY_REGISTERED");
    creators[msg.sender] = Creator(payout, metadataHash, false, true);
    emit CreatorRegistered(msg.sender, payout, metadataHash);
  }

  function adminUpdate(address creator, address payout, bytes32 metadataHash, bool kycFlag, bool active)
    external onlyRole(REGISTRY_ADMIN_ROLE)
  {
    require(payout != address(0), "BAD_PAYOUT");
    creators[creator] = Creator(payout, metadataHash, kycFlag, active);
    emit CreatorUpdated(creator, payout, metadataHash, kycFlag, active);
  }

  function requireActive(address creator) external view {
    Creator memory c = creators[creator];
    require(c.active, "CREATOR_INACTIVE");
    require(c.payout != address(0), "CREATOR_NOT_REGISTERED");
  }
}
```

---

## 12) `contracts/ProductCatalog.sol`
```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "@openzeppelin/contracts/access/AccessControl.sol";

contract ProductCatalog is AccessControl {
  bytes32 public constant CATALOG_ADMIN_ROLE = keccak256("CATALOG_ADMIN_ROLE");

  enum ProductType { ONE_TIME, SUBSCRIPTION }

  struct Product {
    address creator;
    uint256 price;          // in MNEE smallest unit
    ProductType pType;
    uint64 billingPeriod;   // seconds (for subscription)
    bytes32 contentHash;    // points to content metadata / paywall config
    bytes32 splitId;        // reference to split wallet config
    bool active;
  }

  mapping(bytes32 => Product) public products;

  event ProductCreated(bytes32 indexed productId, address indexed creator, uint256 price, ProductType pType, bytes32 splitId);
  event ProductUpdated(bytes32 indexed productId, uint256 price, bool active);

  constructor(address admin) {
    _grantRole(DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE, admin);
    _grantRole(CATALOG_ADMIN_ROLE, admin);
  }

  function createProduct(
    bytes32 productId,
    address creator,
    uint256 price,
    ProductType pType,
    uint64 billingPeriod,
    bytes32 contentHash,
    bytes32 splitId
  ) external onlyRole(CATALOG_ADMIN_ROLE) {
    require(products[productId].creator == address(0), "PRODUCT_EXISTS");
    products[productId] = Product(creator, price, pType, billingPeriod, contentHash, splitId, true);
    emit ProductCreated(productId, creator, price, pType, splitId);
  }

  function updateProduct(bytes32 productId, uint256 price, bool active) external onlyRole(CATALOG_ADMIN_ROLE) {
    require(products[productId].creator != address(0), "NO_PRODUCT");
    products[productId].price = price;
    products[productId].active = active;
    emit ProductUpdated(productId, price, active);
  }

  function requireActive(bytes32 productId) external view {
    require(products[productId].active, "PRODUCT_INACTIVE");
  }
}
```

## 13) Entitlements (enterprise access control)
You can do ERC721 (unique token per purchase) or ERC1155 (per product).  
For a paywall, **ERC1155 is better**.

## 14) `contracts/Entitlement1155.sol`
```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "@openzeppelin/contracts/token/ERC1155/ERC1155.sol";
import "@openzeppelin/contracts/access/AccessControl.sol";

contract Entitlement1155 is ERC1155, AccessControl {
  bytes32 public constant MINTER_ROLE = keccak256("MINTER_ROLE");

  constructor(address admin) ERC1155("") {
    _grantRole(DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE, admin);
  }

  function mint(address to, uint256 id, uint256 amount, bytes memory data) external onlyRole(MINTER_ROLE) {
    _mint(to, id, amount, data);
  }

  function burn(address from, uint256 id, uint256 amount) external onlyRole(MINTER_ROLE) {
    _burn(from, id, amount);
  }
}
```

# 15) Splits (immutable payout distribution)
Enterprise rule: splits must be **immutable per product version**.

## 16) `contracts/SplitWallet.sol`
```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "@openzeppelin/contracts/token/ERC20/utils/SafeERC20.sol";
import "@openzeppelin/contracts/security/ReentrancyGuard.sol";

contract SplitWallet is ReentrancyGuard {
  using SafeERC20 for IERC20;

  IERC20 public immutable mnee;

  address[] public payees;
  uint256[] public bps; // basis points, total = 10000

  event SplitPaid(address indexed payee, uint256 amount);

  constructor(address token, address[] memory _payees, uint256[] memory _bps) {
    require(_payees.length == _bps.length && _payees.length > 0, "BAD_SPLIT");
    uint256 total;
    for (uint i=0; i<_bps.length; i++) total += _bps[i];
    require(total == 10000, "BPS_NOT_10000");
    mnee = IERC20(token);
    payees = _payees;
    bps = _bps;
  }

  function distribute(uint256 amount) external nonReentrant {
    // amount already transferred to this wallet by router
    for (uint i=0; i<payees.length; i++) {
      uint256 share = (amount * bps[i]) / 10000;
      mnee.safeTransfer(payees[i], share);
      emit SplitPaid(payees[i], share);
    }
  }
}
```

> Enterprise optimization: for large payee counts, use pull-based claims.  
Hackathon version assumes small split size (creator + 1–5 collaborators + platform).

# 17) Checkout + Paywall Router (core of Product-4)
This is where we enforce:
- product active
- creator active
- payment transfer
- entitlement mint
- split settle
- refund logic
- freeze mode

## 18) `contracts/CheckoutRouter.sol`
```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "@openzeppelin/contracts/token/ERC20/utils/SafeERC20.sol";
import "@openzeppelin/contracts/access/AccessControl.sol";
import "@openzeppelin/contracts/security/ReentrancyGuard.sol";

import "./CreatorRegistry.sol";
import "./ProductCatalog.sol";
import "./Entitlement1155.sol";
import "./IncidentController.sol";

contract CheckoutRouter is AccessControl, ReentrancyGuard {
  using SafeERC20 for IERC20;

  bytes32 public constant OPS_ROLE = keccak256("OPS_ROLE");

  IERC20 public immutable mnee;
  CreatorRegistry public creators;
  ProductCatalog public catalog;
  Entitlement1155 public entitlement;
  IncidentController public incidents;

  struct Receipt {
    bytes32 receiptId;
    bytes32 productId;
    address buyer;
    uint256 amount;
    uint64 paidAt;
    bool refunded;
  }

  mapping(bytes32 => Receipt) public receipts;

  event Paid(bytes32 indexed receiptId, bytes32 indexed productId, address indexed buyer, uint256 amount);
  event EntitlementGranted(bytes32 indexed receiptId, address indexed buyer, uint256 entitlementId);
  event Refunded(bytes32 indexed receiptId, address indexed buyer, uint256 amount);

  constructor(
    address admin,
    address token,
    address creatorRegistry,
    address productCatalog,
    address entitlement1155,
    address incidentController
  ) {
    _grantRole(DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE, admin);
    _grantRole(OPS_ROLE, admin);

    mnee = IERC20(token);
    creators = CreatorRegistry(creatorRegistry);
    catalog = ProductCatalog(productCatalog);
    entitlement = Entitlement1155(entitlement1155);
    incidents = IncidentController(incidentController);
  }

  function pay(bytes32 receiptId, bytes32 productId) external nonReentrant {
    incidents.requireNotFrozen();
    require(receipts[receiptId].buyer == address(0), "RECEIPT_EXISTS");

    catalog.requireActive(productId);
    ProductCatalog.Product memory p = catalog.products(productId);
    creators.requireActive(p.creator);

    // Pull MNEE from buyer
    mnee.safeTransferFrom(msg.sender, address(this), p.price);

    receipts[receiptId] = Receipt(receiptId, productId, msg.sender, p.price, uint64(block.timestamp), false);
    emit Paid(receiptId, productId, msg.sender, p.price);

    // Grant entitlement (use productId hash -> uint256)
    uint256 entId = uint256(productId);
    entitlement.mint(msg.sender, entId, 1, "");
    emit EntitlementGranted(receiptId, msg.sender, entId);

    // Settlement:
    // For hackathon: direct transfer to SplitWallet (address resolved off-chain or via mapping in catalog extension).
    // If you want fully on-chain, we add SplitRegistry mapping splitId -> wallet address (next section).
  }

  function refund(bytes32 receiptId) external nonReentrant onlyRole(OPS_ROLE) {
    incidents.requireNotFrozen();
    Receipt storage r = receipts[receiptId];
    require(r.buyer != address(0), "NO_RECEIPT");
    require(!r.refunded, "ALREADY_REFUNDED");

    r.refunded = true;

    // burn entitlement
    uint256 entId = uint256(r.productId);
    entitlement.burn(r.buyer, entId, 1);

    // return funds (router must still hold funds or pull back from escrow if used)
    mnee.safeTransfer(r.buyer, r.amount);

    emit Refunded(receiptId, r.buyer, r.amount);
  }
}
```

✅ This is runnable, but **settlement is incomplete** until we add SplitRegistry or escrow mode. That’s next.

## 20) `contracts/SplitRegistry.sol`
```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "@openzeppelin/contracts/access/AccessControl.sol";

contract SplitRegistry is AccessControl {
  bytes32 public constant SPLIT_ADMIN_ROLE = keccak256("SPLIT_ADMIN_ROLE");

  mapping(bytes32 => address) public splitWallet;

  event SplitRegistered(bytes32 indexed splitId, address wallet);

  constructor(address admin) {
    _grantRole(DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE, admin);
    _grantRole(SPLIT_ADMIN_ROLE, admin);
  }

  function registerSplit(bytes32 splitId, address wallet) external onlyRole(SPLIT_ADMIN_ROLE) {
    require(wallet != address(0), "BAD_WALLET");
    require(splitWallet[splitId] == address(0), "SPLIT_EXISTS");
    splitWallet[splitId] = wallet;
    emit SplitRegistered(splitId, wallet);
  }
}
```

# 22) Dispute Escrow mode (optional, enterprise-grade)
When enabled per product, we do:
- buyer pays → funds held in escrow for X hours → then distributed
- refunds within window are simple + safe

## 23) `contracts/DisputeEscrow.sol` (minimal)
```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "@openzeppelin/contracts/token/ERC20/utils/SafeERC20.sol";
import "@openzeppelin/contracts/security/ReentrancyGuard.sol";

contract DisputeEscrow is ReentrancyGuard {
  using SafeERC20 for IERC20;

  IERC20 public immutable mnee;
  address public immutable router;
  address public immutable splitWallet;
  uint64 public immutable releaseAt;

  bytes32 public immutable receiptId;
  uint256 public immutable amount;

  bool public released;
  bool public refunded;

  event Released(bytes32 indexed receiptId, uint256 amount);
  event Refunded(bytes32 indexed receiptId, uint256 amount);

  constructor(address token, address _router, address _splitWallet, bytes32 _receiptId, uint256 _amount, uint64 _releaseAt) {
    mnee = IERC20(token);
    router = _router;
    splitWallet = _splitWallet;
    receiptId = _receiptId;
    amount = _amount;
    releaseAt = _releaseAt;
  }

  function fundFromRouter() external nonReentrant {
    require(msg.sender == router, "ONLY_ROUTER");
    // router transfers funds into escrow before calling this (or just direct transfer)
  }

  function release() external nonReentrant {
    require(block.timestamp >= releaseAt, "TOO_EARLY");
    require(!released && !refunded, "DONE");
    released = true;
    mnee.safeTransfer(splitWallet, amount);
    emit Released(receiptId, amount);
  }

  function refund(address buyer) external nonReentrant {
    require(msg.sender == router, "ONLY_ROUTER");
    require(!released && !refunded, "DONE");
    refunded = true;
    mnee.safeTransfer(buyer, amount);
    emit Refunded(receiptId, amount);
  }
}
```

# 23) Subscription model (enterprise reality)
On-chain “auto debit” is not possible without:
- allowance + off-chain agent “pulling”
or
- account abstraction / ERC-4337 paymaster patterns

Hackathon-safe approach:
- subscription requires buyer’s standing allowance
- a **Scheduler Agent** executes monthly `charge(subscriptionId)`  
This hits **AI/Agent Payments** track.

We’ll implement:
- `SubscriptionRegistry` storing nextChargeAt
- `charge()` callable by approved “billing agents”
- entitlement renewed on charge

# 25) 100% Manual testing (step-by-step)
### M4-01 Creator onboarding
1. Connect wallet  
2. Call `CreatorRegistry.register(payout, metadataHash)`  
3. Confirm `CreatorRegistered` event

### M4-02 Product create (admin flow)
1. Create split wallet (payees + bps)  
2. Register splitId → split wallet  
3. Create product in `ProductCatalog`

### M4-03 Checkout (buyer)
1. Buyer approves MNEE to router  
2. Call `CheckoutRouter.pay(receiptId, productId)`  
3. Verify:
   - receipt stored
   - entitlement minted
   - funds distributed to payees (SplitPaid events)

### M4-04 Refund
1. Ops calls `refund(receiptId)` inside window  
2. Verify:
   - entitlement burned
   - buyer receives MNEE back
   - receipt.refunded true

### M4-05 Freeze mode (incident)
1. Set IncidentController FREEZE  
2. Attempt pay → blocked  
3. Unfreeze → pay works

## 27) `test/p4_checkout_split_entitlement.spec.ts` (FULL)
```ts
import { expect } from "chai";
import { ethers } from "hardhat";

function b32(s: string) { return ethers.keccak256(ethers.toUtf8Bytes(s)); }

describe("Product-4 CPI: checkout + split + entitlement", function () {
  it("T4-01 pay distributes split + mints entitlement", async () => {
    const [admin, creator, buyer, editor, platform] = await ethers.getSigners();

    // token
    const ERC20Mock = await ethers.getContractFactory("ERC20Mock");
    const mnee = await ERC20Mock.deploy("MNEE","MNEE",admin.address, ethers.parseEther("1000000"));
    await mnee.waitForDeployment();
    await mnee.mint(buyer.address, ethers.parseEther("100"));

    // incident
    const IncidentController = await ethers.getContractFactory("IncidentController");
    const incidents = await IncidentController.deploy(admin.address);
    await incidents.waitForDeployment();

    // registry
    const CreatorRegistry = await ethers.getContractFactory("CreatorRegistry");
    const creators = await CreatorRegistry.deploy(admin.address);
    await creators.waitForDeployment();
    await creators.connect(creator).register(creator.address, b32("profile"));

    // entitlement
    const Entitlement1155 = await ethers.getContractFactory("Entitlement1155");
    const ent = await Entitlement1155.deploy(admin.address);
    await ent.waitForDeployment();

    // split wallet (70/20/10)
    const SplitWallet = await ethers.getContractFactory("SplitWallet");
    const split = await SplitWallet.deploy(await mnee.getAddress(), [creator.address, editor.address, platform.address], [7000,2000,1000]);
    await split.waitForDeployment();

    // split registry
    const SplitRegistry = await ethers.getContractFactory("SplitRegistry");
    const splits = await SplitRegistry.deploy(admin.address);
    await splits.waitForDeployment();
    const splitId = b32("SPLIT.V1");
    await splits.registerSplit(splitId, await split.getAddress());

    // catalog
    const ProductCatalog = await ethers.getContractFactory("ProductCatalog");
    const catalog = await ProductCatalog.deploy(admin.address);
    await catalog.waitForDeployment();

    const productId = b32("PROD.1");
    const price = ethers.parseEther("10");
    await catalog.createProduct(productId, creator.address, price, 0, 0, b32("content"), splitId);

    // router
    const CheckoutRouter = await ethers.getContractFactory("CheckoutRouter");
    const router = await CheckoutRouter.deploy(
      admin.address,
      await mnee.getAddress(),
      await creators.getAddress(),
      await catalog.getAddress(),
      await ent.getAddress(),
      await incidents.getAddress()
    );
    await router.waitForDeployment();

    // allow router to mint/burn entitlement
    const MINTER_ROLE = b32("MINTER_ROLE");
    await ent.grantRole(MINTER_ROLE, await router.getAddress());

    // PATCH NOTE: in your router, wire SplitRegistry + distribute (as described above).
    // For a runnable test, ensure CheckoutRouter uses splits + SplitWallet.distribute.

    // buyer approves
    await mnee.connect(buyer).approve(await router.getAddress(), price);

    // pre balances
    const c0 = await mnee.balanceOf(creator.address);
    const e0 = await mnee.balanceOf(editor.address);
    const p0 = await mnee.balanceOf(platform.address);

    const receiptId = b32("R1");
    await router.connect(buyer).pay(receiptId, productId);

    // entitlement minted
    expect(await ent.balanceOf(buyer.address, BigInt(productId))).to.equal(1n);

    // split paid: 7/2/1
    const c1 = await mnee.balanceOf(creator.address);
    const e1 = await mnee.balanceOf(editor.address);
    const p1 = await mnee.balanceOf(platform.address);

    expect(c1 - c0).to.equal(ethers.parseEther("7"));
    expect(e1 - e0).to.equal(ethers.parseEther("2"));
    expect(p1 - p0).to.equal(ethers.parseEther("1"));
  });
});
```

## 33) CheckoutRouterV2 — FULL CONTRACT

`contracts/CheckoutRouterV2.sol`
```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "@openzeppelin/contracts/token/ERC20/utils/SafeERC20.sol";
import "@openzeppelin/contracts/access/AccessControl.sol";
import "@openzeppelin/contracts/security/ReentrancyGuard.sol";

import "./CreatorRegistry.sol";
import "./ProductCatalog.sol";
import "./Entitlement1155.sol";
import "./SplitRegistry.sol";
import "./SplitWallet.sol";
import "./DisputeEscrow.sol";
import "./IncidentController.sol";

contract CheckoutRouterV2 is AccessControl, ReentrancyGuard {
  using SafeERC20 for IERC20;

  bytes32 public constant OPS_ROLE = keccak256("OPS_ROLE");

  IERC20 public immutable mnee;
  CreatorRegistry public creators;
  ProductCatalog public catalog;
  Entitlement1155 public entitlement;
  SplitRegistry public splits;
  IncidentController public incidents;

  struct Receipt {
    bytes32 receiptId;
    bytes32 productId;
    address buyer;
    uint256 amount;
    uint64 paidAt;
    bool refunded;
    address escrow; // zero if not used
  }

  mapping(bytes32 => Receipt) public receipts;

  event Paid(bytes32 indexed receiptId, bytes32 indexed productId, address buyer, uint256 amount);
  event EntitlementGranted(bytes32 indexed receiptId, address buyer, uint256 entitlementId);
  event Settled(bytes32 indexed receiptId, address splitWallet, uint256 amount);
  event Refunded(bytes32 indexed receiptId, address buyer, uint256 amount);

  constructor(
    address admin,
    address token,
    address creatorRegistry,
    address productCatalog,
    address entitlement1155,
    address splitRegistry,
    address incidentController
  ) {
    _grantRole(DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE, admin);
    _grantRole(OPS_ROLE, admin);

    mnee = IERC20(token);
    creators = CreatorRegistry(creatorRegistry);
    catalog = ProductCatalog(productCatalog);
    entitlement = Entitlement1155(entitlement1155);
    splits = SplitRegistry(splitRegistry);
    incidents = IncidentController(incidentController);
  }

  function pay(bytes32 receiptId, bytes32 productId) external nonReentrant {
    incidents.requireNotFrozen();
    require(receipts[receiptId].buyer == address(0), "RECEIPT_EXISTS");

    catalog.requireActive(productId);
    ProductCatalog.Product memory p = catalog.products(productId);
    creators.requireActive(p.creator);

    // pull funds
    mnee.safeTransferFrom(msg.sender, address(this), p.price);

    receipts[receiptId] = Receipt(
      receiptId,
      productId,
      msg.sender,
      p.price,
      uint64(block.timestamp),
      false,
      address(0)
    );

    emit Paid(receiptId, productId, msg.sender, p.price);

    // mint entitlement
    uint256 entId = uint256(productId);
    entitlement.mint(msg.sender, entId, 1, "");
    emit EntitlementGranted(receiptId, msg.sender, entId);

    if (p.escrowEnabled) {
      _createEscrow(receiptId, p);
    } else {
      _settleDirect(receiptId, p);
    }
  }

  function _settleDirect(bytes32 receiptId, ProductCatalog.Product memory p) internal {
    address wallet = splits.splitWallet(p.splitId);
    require(wallet != address(0), "NO_SPLIT_WALLET");

    mnee.safeTransfer(wallet, p.price);
    SplitWallet(wallet).distribute(p.price);

    emit Settled(receiptId, wallet, p.price);
  }

  function _createEscrow(bytes32 receiptId, ProductCatalog.Product memory p) internal {
    address wallet = splits.splitWallet(p.splitId);
    require(wallet != address(0), "NO_SPLIT_WALLET");

    uint64 releaseAt = uint64(block.timestamp + p.refundWindow);
    DisputeEscrow escrow = new DisputeEscrow(
      address(mnee),
      address(this),
      wallet,
      receiptId,
      p.price,
      releaseAt
    );

    receipts[receiptId].escrow = address(escrow);
    mnee.safeTransfer(address(escrow), p.price);
  }

  function releaseEscrow(bytes32 receiptId) external nonReentrant {
    incidents.requireNotFrozen();
    Receipt storage r = receipts[receiptId];
    require(r.escrow != address(0), "NO_ESCROW");
    DisputeEscrow(r.escrow).release();
    emit Settled(receiptId, DisputeEscrow(r.escrow).splitWallet(), r.amount);
  }

  function refund(bytes32 receiptId) external nonReentrant onlyRole(OPS_ROLE) {
    incidents.requireNotFrozen();
    Receipt storage r = receipts[receiptId];
    require(r.buyer != address(0), "NO_RECEIPT");
    require(!r.refunded, "ALREADY_REFUNDED");

    ProductCatalog.Product memory p = catalog.products(r.productId);
    require(block.timestamp <= r.paidAt + p.refundWindow, "REFUND_WINDOW_CLOSED");

    r.refunded = true;

    uint256 entId = uint256(r.productId);
    entitlement.burn(r.buyer, entId, 1);

    if (r.escrow != address(0)) {
      DisputeEscrow(r.escrow).refund(r.buyer);
    } else {
      mnee.safeTransfer(r.buyer, r.amount);
    }

    emit Refunded(receiptId, r.buyer, r.amount);
  }
}
```

## 34) SubscriptionRegistry

`contracts/SubscriptionRegistry.sol`
```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "@openzeppelin/contracts/access/AccessControl.sol";

contract SubscriptionRegistry is AccessControl {
  bytes32 public constant SUB_ADMIN_ROLE = keccak256("SUB_ADMIN_ROLE");

  struct Subscription {
    address buyer;
    bytes32 productId;
    uint64 nextChargeAt;
    bool active;
  }

  mapping(bytes32 => Subscription) public subs;

  event SubCreated(bytes32 indexed subId, address buyer, bytes32 productId, uint64 nextChargeAt);
  event SubCharged(bytes32 indexed subId, uint64 chargedAt);
  event SubCancelled(bytes32 indexed subId);

  constructor(address admin) {
    _grantRole(DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE, admin);
    _grantRole(SUB_ADMIN_ROLE, admin);
  }

  function create(bytes32 subId, address buyer, bytes32 productId, uint64 firstChargeAt)
    external onlyRole(SUB_ADMIN_ROLE)
  {
    subs[subId] = Subscription(buyer, productId, firstChargeAt, true);
    emit SubCreated(subId, buyer, productId, firstChargeAt);
  }

  function markCharged(bytes32 subId, uint64 nextChargeAt) external onlyRole(SUB_ADMIN_ROLE) {
    Subscription storage s = subs[subId];
    require(s.active, "SUB_INACTIVE");
    s.nextChargeAt = nextChargeAt;
    emit SubCharged(subId, block.timestamp);
  }

  function cancel(bytes32 subId) external onlyRole(SUB_ADMIN_ROLE) {
    subs[subId].active = false;
    emit SubCancelled(subId);
  }
}
```

---

## 35) BillingAgent (agent executes recurring charge)

`contracts/BillingAgent.sol`
```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "@openzeppelin/contracts/access/AccessControl.sol";
import "./SubscriptionRegistry.sol";
import "./CheckoutRouterV2.sol";
import "./ProductCatalog.sol";

contract BillingAgent is AccessControl {
  bytes32 public constant AGENT_ROLE = keccak256("AGENT_ROLE");

  SubscriptionRegistry public subs;
  CheckoutRouterV2 public router;
  ProductCatalog public catalog;

  constructor(address admin, address subReg, address checkout, address catalogAddr) {
    _grantRole(DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE, admin);
    _grantRole(AGENT_ROLE, admin);
    subs = SubscriptionRegistry(subReg);
    router = CheckoutRouterV2(checkout);
    catalog = ProductCatalog(catalogAddr);
  }

  function charge(bytes32 subId, bytes32 receiptId) external onlyRole(AGENT_ROLE) {
    SubscriptionRegistry.Subscription memory s = subs.subs(subId);
    require(s.active, "SUB_INACTIVE");
    require(block.timestamp >= s.nextChargeAt, "TOO_EARLY");

    ProductCatalog.Product memory p = catalog.products(s.productId);
    require(p.pType == ProductCatalog.ProductType.SUBSCRIPTION, "NOT_SUB");

    router.pay(receiptId, s.productId);

    subs.markCharged(subId, uint64(block.timestamp + p.billingPeriod));
  }
}
```

## 9) `AgentRegistry.sol`
```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "@openzeppelin/contracts/access/AccessControl.sol";

contract AgentRegistry is AccessControl {
  bytes32 public constant AGENT_ADMIN_ROLE = keccak256("AGENT_ADMIN_ROLE");

  struct Agent {
    address operator;
    bytes32 role;
    uint8 riskTier;
    bool active;
  }

  mapping(bytes32 => Agent) public agents;

  event AgentRegistered(bytes32 indexed agentId, address operator, bytes32 role, uint8 riskTier);
  event AgentState(bytes32 indexed agentId, bool active);

  constructor(address admin) {
    _grantRole(DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE, admin);
    _grantRole(AGENT_ADMIN_ROLE, admin);
  }

  function registerAgent(bytes32 agentId, address operator, bytes32 role, uint8 riskTier)
    external onlyRole(AGENT_ADMIN_ROLE)
  {
    agents[agentId] = Agent(operator, role, riskTier, true);
    emit AgentRegistered(agentId, operator, role, riskTier);
  }

  function setActive(bytes32 agentId, bool active)
    external onlyRole(AGENT_ADMIN_ROLE)
  {
    agents[agentId].active = active;
    emit AgentState(agentId, active);
  }

  function requireActive(bytes32 agentId) external view {
    require(agents[agentId].active, "AGENT_INACTIVE");
  }
}
```

## 12) `PolicyRouter.sol`
```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "@openzeppelin/contracts/access/AccessControl.sol";

contract PolicyRouter is AccessControl {
  bytes32 public constant POLICY_ADMIN_ROLE = keccak256("POLICY_ADMIN_ROLE");

  struct Policy {
    bool active;
    uint256 maxAmount;
    bool escrowRequired;
    bool allowAgent;
  }

  mapping(bytes32 => Policy) public policies;

  event PolicyActivated(bytes32 indexed policyId);
  event PolicyUpdated(bytes32 indexed policyId);

  constructor(address admin) {
    _grantRole(DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE, admin);
    _grantRole(POLICY_ADMIN_ROLE, admin);
  }

  function setPolicy(
    bytes32 policyId,
    uint256 maxAmount,
    bool escrowRequired,
    bool allowAgent
  ) external onlyRole(POLICY_ADMIN_ROLE) {
    policies[policyId] = Policy(true, maxAmount, escrowRequired, allowAgent);
    emit PolicyUpdated(policyId);
  }

  function requirePolicy(bytes32 policyId, uint256 amount) external view {
    Policy memory p = policies[policyId];
    require(p.active, "POLICY_INACTIVE");
    require(amount <= p.maxAmount, "POLICY_AMOUNT_EXCEEDED");
  }
}
```

## 15) `IntentHub.sol`
```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "@openzeppelin/contracts/access/AccessControl.sol";

contract IntentHub is AccessControl {
  bytes32 public constant INTENT_ADMIN_ROLE = keccak256("INTENT_ADMIN_ROLE");

  enum Status { NONE, CREATED, VALIDATED, APPROVED, EXECUTED, SETTLED }

  struct Intent {
    bytes32 agentId;
    bytes32 policyId;
    uint256 amount;
    Status status;
  }

  mapping(bytes32 => Intent) public intents;

  event IntentCreated(bytes32 indexed intentId, bytes32 agentId, uint256 amount);
  event IntentStatus(bytes32 indexed intentId, Status status);

  constructor(address admin) {
    _grantRole(DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE, admin);
    _grantRole(INTENT_ADMIN_ROLE, admin);
  }

  function createIntent(bytes32 intentId, bytes32 agentId, bytes32 policyId, uint256 amount)
    external onlyRole(INTENT_ADMIN_ROLE)
  {
    intents[intentId] = Intent(agentId, policyId, amount, Status.CREATED);
    emit IntentCreated(intentId, agentId, amount);
  }

  function setStatus(bytes32 intentId, Status s) external onlyRole(INTENT_ADMIN_ROLE) {
    intents[intentId].status = s;
    emit IntentStatus(intentId, s);
  }
}
```

## 18) `ExecutionRouter.sol`
```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "@openzeppelin/contracts/access/AccessControl.sol";
import "./IntentHub.sol";

contract ExecutionRouter is AccessControl {
  bytes32 public constant EXEC_ROLE = keccak256("EXEC_ROLE");

  IntentHub public intents;

  event Executed(bytes32 indexed intentId);

  constructor(address admin, address intentHub) {
    _grantRole(DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE, admin);
    _grantRole(EXEC_ROLE, admin);
    intents = IntentHub(intentHub);
  }

  function execute(bytes32 intentId) external onlyRole(EXEC_ROLE) {
    IntentHub.Intent memory i = intents.intents(intentId);
    require(i.status == IntentHub.Status.APPROVED, "NOT_APPROVED");
    intents.setStatus(intentId, IntentHub.Status.EXECUTED);
    emit Executed(intentId);
  }
}
```

- `PFTAAdapter` → emits ledger & period controller checks
- `A2AAdapter` → agent-to-agent commerce payments (Product-2)

We implement **interfaces + lightweight router**.

# 27) ACTF Contract Suite (enterprise minimal, runnable)

## 27.1 `contracts/actf/ACTFTypes.sol`
```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

library ACTFTypes {
  enum IntentType { NONE, CHECKOUT, SUB_CHARGE, TREASURY_SPEND, A2A_PAY, BATCH_SETTLE }
  enum Status { NONE, CREATED, VALIDATED, APPROVED, EXECUTED, SETTLED, REJECTED }

  struct Intent {
    bytes32 intentId;
    IntentType intentType;
    bytes32 agentId;
    bytes32 policyId;
    bytes32 refId;     // productId, spendRequestId, batchId, etc.
    uint256 amount;
    uint64 createdAt;
    Status status;
  }
}
```

---

## 27.2 `contracts/actf/EvidenceLog.sol`
Enterprise requirement: every decision emits evidence.

```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "@openzeppelin/contracts/access/AccessControl.sol";

contract EvidenceLog is AccessControl {
  bytes32 public constant WRITER_ROLE = keccak256("WRITER_ROLE");

  event Evidence(bytes32 indexed code, bytes32 indexed refId, address actor, uint256 amount, bytes32 meta);

  constructor(address admin) {
    _grantRole(DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE, admin);
    _grantRole(WRITER_ROLE, admin);
  }

  function emitEvidence(bytes32 code, bytes32 refId, uint256 amount, bytes32 meta)
    external onlyRole(WRITER_ROLE)
  {
    emit Evidence(code, refId, msg.sender, amount, meta);
  }
}
```

# 28) Core Fabric: `ACTFHub.sol` (THIS IS PRODUCT-5)

## 28.1 Responsibilities
- create intents
- validate by policy
- require approvals by ATG
- route execution by intent type
- write evidence

## 28.2 Contract
`contracts/actf/ACTFHub.sol`
```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "@openzeppelin/contracts/access/AccessControl.sol";
import "./ACTFTypes.sol";
import "../IncidentController.sol";
import "../AgentRegistry.sol";
import "../PolicyRouter.sol";
import "./EvidenceLog.sol";
import "./interfaces/ICPIAdapter.sol";
import "./interfaces/IPFTAAdapter.sol";
import "./interfaces/IATGAdapter.sol";
import "./interfaces/IA2AAdapter.sol";

contract ACTFHub is AccessControl {
  using ACTFTypes for ACTFTypes.Intent;

  bytes32 public constant OPS_ROLE = keccak256("OPS_ROLE");
  bytes32 public constant EXEC_ROLE = keccak256("EXEC_ROLE");

  IncidentController public incidents;
  AgentRegistry public agents;
  PolicyRouter public policies;
  EvidenceLog public evidence;

  ICPIAdapter public cpi;
  IPFTAAdapter public pfta;
  IATGAdapter public atg;
  IA2AAdapter public a2a;

  mapping(bytes32 => ACTFTypes.Intent) public intents;

  event IntentCreated(bytes32 indexed intentId, ACTFTypes.IntentType intentType, bytes32 agentId, bytes32 refId, uint256 amount);
  event IntentStatus(bytes32 indexed intentId, ACTFTypes.Status status);

  constructor(
    address admin,
    address incidentController,
    address agentRegistry,
    address policyRouter,
    address evidenceLog
  ) {
    _grantRole(DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE, admin);
    _grantRole(OPS_ROLE, admin);
    _grantRole(EXEC_ROLE, admin);

    incidents = IncidentController(incidentController);
    agents = AgentRegistry(agentRegistry);
    policies = PolicyRouter(policyRouter);
    evidence = EvidenceLog(evidenceLog);
  }

  function setAdapters(address cpiAddr, address pftaAddr, address atgAddr, address a2aAddr)
    external onlyRole(OPS_ROLE)
  {
    cpi = ICPIAdapter(cpiAddr);
    pfta = IPFTAAdapter(pftaAddr);
    atg = IATGAdapter(atgAddr);
    a2a = IA2AAdapter(a2aAddr);
  }

  function createIntent(
    bytes32 intentId,
    ACTFTypes.IntentType intentType,
    bytes32 agentId,
    bytes32 policyId,
    bytes32 refId,
    uint256 amount
  ) external onlyRole(OPS_ROLE) {
    incidents.requireNotFrozen();
    agents.requireActive(agentId);

    intents[intentId] = ACTFTypes.Intent(
      intentId,
      intentType,
      agentId,
      policyId,
      refId,
      amount,
      uint64(block.timestamp),
      ACTFTypes.Status.CREATED
    );

    emit IntentCreated(intentId, intentType, agentId, refId, amount);
    emit IntentStatus(intentId, ACTFTypes.Status.CREATED);

    evidence.emitEvidence(keccak256("ACTF.INTENT.CREATED"), intentId, amount, policyId);
  }

  function validateIntent(bytes32 intentId) external onlyRole(OPS_ROLE) {
    incidents.requireNotFrozen();
    ACTFTypes.Intent storage i = intents[intentId];
    require(i.status == ACTFTypes.Status.CREATED, "BAD_STATUS");

    policies.requirePolicy(i.policyId, i.amount);

    i.status = ACTFTypes.Status.VALIDATED;
    emit IntentStatus(intentId, ACTFTypes.Status.VALIDATED);
    evidence.emitEvidence(keccak256("ACTF.INTENT.VALIDATED"), intentId, i.amount, i.policyId);
  }

  function approveIntent(bytes32 intentId) external onlyRole(OPS_ROLE) {
    incidents.requireNotFrozen();
    ACTFTypes.Intent storage i = intents[intentId];
    require(i.status == ACTFTypes.Status.VALIDATED, "BAD_STATUS");

    // if you want strict tier mapping: store tier in AgentRegistry and require ATG approvals accordingly
    uint8 tier = agents.agents(i.agentId).riskTier;

    // ATG gate: ensures approvals exist if tier requires
    atg.requireApproved(intentId, tier);

    i.status = ACTFTypes.Status.APPROVED;
    emit IntentStatus(intentId, ACTFTypes.Status.APPROVED);
    evidence.emitEvidence(keccak256("ACTF.INTENT.APPROVED"), intentId, i.amount, bytes32(uint256(tier)));
  }

  function execute(bytes32 intentId, address buyerOrFrom, address to) external onlyRole(EXEC_ROLE) {
    incidents.requireNotFrozen();
    ACTFTypes.Intent storage i = intents[intentId];
    require(i.status == ACTFTypes.Status.APPROVED, "NOT_APPROVED");

    if (i.intentType == ACTFTypes.IntentType.CHECKOUT) {
      // refId = productId, amount = price
      cpi.checkout(intentId, i.refId, buyerOrFrom);
      pfta.postLedger(intentId, keccak256("EXPENSE.MERCH"), keccak256("ASSET.TREASURY"), i.amount, keccak256("CC.COMMERCE"));
    }
    else if (i.intentType == ACTFTypes.IntentType.SUB_CHARGE) {
      // refId = subId
      cpi.chargeSubscription(i.refId, intentId);
      pfta.postLedger(intentId, keccak256("EXPENSE.SUBS"), keccak256("ASSET.TREASURY"), i.amount, keccak256("CC.SUBS"));
    }
    else if (i.intentType == ACTFTypes.IntentType.A2A_PAY) {
      // from/to provided
      a2a.agentPay(intentId, buyerOrFrom, to, i.amount);
      pfta.postLedger(intentId, keccak256("EXPENSE.A2A"), keccak256("ASSET.TREASURY"), i.amount, keccak256("CC.A2A"));
    }
    else if (i.intentType == ACTFTypes.IntentType.TREASURY_SPEND) {
      // product-3 handles treasury spend; here just ledger evidence
      pfta.postLedger(intentId, keccak256("EXPENSE.TREASURY"), keccak256("ASSET.TREASURY"), i.amount, keccak256("CC.TREASURY"));
    }
    else if (i.intentType == ACTFTypes.IntentType.BATCH_SETTLE) {
      // refId=batchId, actual settlement actions off-chain or via CPI adapter loop (keep loops off-chain)
      evidence.emitEvidence(keccak256("ACTF.BATCH.SETTLE.EXEC"), i.refId, i.amount, keccak256("BATCH"));
    }
    else {
      revert("UNKNOWN_INTENT_TYPE");
    }

    i.status = ACTFTypes.Status.EXECUTED;
    emit IntentStatus(intentId, ACTFTypes.Status.EXECUTED);
    evidence.emitEvidence(keccak256("ACTF.INTENT.EXECUTED"), intentId, i.amount, i.refId);

    i.status = ACTFTypes.Status.SETTLED;
    emit IntentStatus(intentId, ACTFTypes.Status.SETTLED);
    evidence.emitEvidence(keccak256("ACTF.INTENT.SETTLED"), intentId, i.amount, i.refId);
  }
}
```

✅ This is the **enterprise fabric core**: consistent intent lifecycle + policy + approval + execution + ledger + evidence.

# 29) CPI Adapter (wired to Product-4)
We implement a minimal adapter that calls your `CheckoutRouterV2` and `BillingAgent`.

`contracts/actf/adapters/CPIAdapter.sol`
```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "../../CheckoutRouterV2.sol";
import "../../BillingAgent.sol";
import "../interfaces/ICPIAdapter.sol";

contract CPIAdapter is ICPIAdapter {
  CheckoutRouterV2 public router;
  BillingAgent public billing;

  constructor(address routerAddr, address billingAddr) {
    router = CheckoutRouterV2(routerAddr);
    billing = BillingAgent(billingAddr);
  }

  function checkout(bytes32 receiptId, bytes32 productId, address buyer) external {
    // buyer must call pay in a real system; for hackathon, this adapter is called by an EXEC_ROLE actor
    // If you want strict correctness: use ERC-4337 or call from buyer wallet.
    router.pay(receiptId, productId);
  }

  function chargeSubscription(bytes32 subId, bytes32 receiptId) external {
    billing.charge(subId, receiptId);
  }

  function releaseEscrow(bytes32 receiptId) external {
    router.releaseEscrow(receiptId);
  }
}
```

**Enterprise note:** In production, “checkout” must be triggered by buyer wallet; adapter is still correct as an orchestration boundary (execute role can be the buyer’s AA wallet/paymaster).

Minimal adapter that only emits ledger entries (or routes to Product-3 ledger contract).

`contracts/actf/adapters/PFTAAdapter.sol`
```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "../interfaces/IPFTAAdapter.sol";
import "../EvidenceLog.sol";
import "@openzeppelin/contracts/access/AccessControl.sol";

contract PFTAAdapter is IPFTAAdapter, AccessControl {
  bytes32 public constant LEDGER_WRITER_ROLE = keccak256("LEDGER_WRITER_ROLE");

  event LedgerEntry(bytes32 indexed refId, bytes32 debit, bytes32 credit, uint256 amount, bytes32 costCenter);

  constructor(address admin) {
    _grantRole(DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE, admin);
    _grantRole(LEDGER_WRITER_ROLE, admin);
  }

  function postLedger(bytes32 refId, bytes32 debit, bytes32 credit, uint256 amount, bytes32 costCenter)
    external onlyRole(LEDGER_WRITER_ROLE)
  {
    emit LedgerEntry(refId, debit, credit, amount, costCenter);
  }

  function requirePeriodOpen(uint64) external pure {}
}
```

(When you wire to Product-3, replace with real ledger + PeriodController.)

# 31) ATG Adapter (approval gate)
For hackathon runnable mode we implement a strict rule:
- Tier 1: auto
- Tier 2+: requires “approval recorded” mapping

`contracts/actf/adapters/ATGAdapter.sol`
```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "../interfaces/IATGAdapter.sol";
import "@openzeppelin/contracts/access/AccessControl.sol";

contract ATGAdapter is IATGAdapter, AccessControl {
  bytes32 public constant APPROVER_ROLE = keccak256("APPROVER_ROLE");

  mapping(bytes32 => uint8) public approvalCount;     // intentId -> count
  mapping(bytes32 => uint8) public requiredByTier;    // tier -> required approvals

  event Approved(bytes32 indexed intentId, address approver);

  constructor(address admin) {
    _grantRole(DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE, admin);
    _grantRole(APPROVER_ROLE, admin);
    requiredByTier[0] = 0;
    requiredByTier[1] = 0;
    requiredByTier[2] = 1;
    requiredByTier[3] = 2;
    requiredByTier[4] = 2;
  }

  function approve(bytes32 intentId) external onlyRole(APPROVER_ROLE) {
    approvalCount[intentId] += 1;
    emit Approved(intentId, msg.sender);
  }

  function requireApproved(bytes32 intentId, uint8 riskTier) external view {
    require(approvalCount[intentId] >= requiredByTier[riskTier], "ATG_NOT_APPROVED");
  }
}
```

# 33) ACTF “E2E runnable demo” test (Hardhat)

`test/p5_actf_e2e_intents.spec.ts`
```ts
import { expect } from "chai";
import { ethers } from "hardhat";

function b32(s: string) { return ethers.keccak256(ethers.toUtf8Bytes(s)); }

describe("Product-5 ACTF E2E: intent -> policy -> approvals -> execute", function () {
  it("T5-01 checkout intent executes when approved", async () => {
    const [admin, exec] = await ethers.getSigners();

    // controllers
    const IncidentController = await ethers.getContractFactory("IncidentController");
    const incidents = await IncidentController.deploy(admin.address);
    await incidents.waitForDeployment();

    const AgentRegistry = await ethers.getContractFactory("AgentRegistry");
    const agents = await AgentRegistry.deploy(admin.address);
    await agents.waitForDeployment();

    const PolicyRouter = await ethers.getContractFactory("PolicyRouter");
    const policies = await PolicyRouter.deploy(admin.address);
    await policies.waitForDeployment();

    const EvidenceLog = await ethers.getContractFactory("EvidenceLog");
    const evidence = await EvidenceLog.deploy(admin.address);
    await evidence.waitForDeployment();

    // adapters
    const ATGAdapter = await ethers.getContractFactory("ATGAdapter");
    const atg = await ATGAdapter.deploy(admin.address);
    await atg.waitForDeployment();

    const PFTAAdapter = await ethers.getContractFactory("PFTAAdapter");
    const pfta = await PFTAAdapter.deploy(admin.address);
    await pfta.waitForDeployment();

    const A2AAdapter = await ethers.getContractFactory("A2AAdapter");
    const a2a = await A2AAdapter.deploy();
    await a2a.waitForDeployment();

    // CPI adapter (for this runnable test, we will just use a mock)
    const CPIAdapterMock = await ethers.getContractFactory("CPIAdapterMock");
    const cpi = await CPIAdapterMock.deploy();
    await cpi.waitForDeployment();

    // ACTF hub
    const ACTFHub = await ethers.getContractFactory("ACTFHub");
    const hub = await ACTFHub.deploy(
      admin.address,
      await incidents.getAddress(),
      await agents.getAddress(),
      await policies.getAddress(),
      await evidence.getAddress()
    );
    await hub.waitForDeployment();

    await hub.setAdapters(await cpi.getAddress(), await pfta.getAddress(), await atg.getAddress(), await a2a.getAddress());

    // roles
    const OPS_ROLE = b32("OPS_ROLE");
    const EXEC_ROLE = b32("EXEC_ROLE");
    await hub.grantRole(EXEC_ROLE, exec.address);

    // agent + policy
    const agentId = b32("AGENT.1");
    await agents.registerAgent(agentId, exec.address, b32("ROLE.BUYER"), 2); // tier2 -> approvals required
    const policyId = b32("POLICY.LOW");
    await policies.setPolicy(policyId, ethers.parseEther("100"), false, true);

    // create intent (checkout)
    const intentId = b32("INTENT.1");
    const productId = b32("PROD.API");
    const amount = ethers.parseEther("10");

    await hub.createIntent(intentId, 1, agentId, policyId, productId, amount); // CHECKOUT=1
    await hub.validateIntent(intentId);

    // approve (tier2 needs 1)
    await atg.approve(intentId);

    await hub.approveIntent(intentId);

    // execute
    await hub.connect(exec).execute(intentId, exec.address, ethers.ZeroAddress);

    const i = await hub.intents(intentId);
    expect(i.status).to.equal(5); // SETTLED
  });
});
```

- Off-chain executor submits individual releases
- ACTF records evidence per action

This avoids gas bombs and keeps system enterprise-safe.

## 41) Why signed intents are mandatory (enterprise truth)
Without **agent-signed intents**, ACTF would still depend on:
- privileged operators
- centralized relayers
- unverifiable origin

**Signed intents give:**
- non-repudiation
- replay protection
- offline simulation
- regulator-grade evidence

Agents **author** value actions; ACTF **governs** them.

---

# PART A — EIP-712 SIGNED INTENTS (CORE)

## 42) Intent canonical schema (locked)
```text
Intent(
  intentId,
  agentId,
  intentType,
  policyId,
  refId,
  amount,
  validAfter,
  deadline,
  nonce
)
```

---

## 43) `IntentVerifier.sol` (EIP-712 authority)
```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "@openzeppelin/contracts/utils/cryptography/EIP712.sol";
import "@openzeppelin/contracts/utils/cryptography/ECDSA.sol";

contract IntentVerifier is EIP712 {
  using ECDSA for bytes32;

  bytes32 public constant INTENT_TYPEHASH = keccak256(
    "Intent(bytes32 intentId,bytes32 agentId,uint8 intentType,bytes32 policyId,bytes32 refId,uint256 amount,uint64 validAfter,uint64 deadline,uint256 nonce)"
  );

  mapping(address => mapping(uint256 => bool)) public usedNonce;

  constructor() EIP712("ACTF", "1") {}

  function hashIntent(
    bytes32 intentId,
    bytes32 agentId,
    uint8 intentType,
    bytes32 policyId,
    bytes32 refId,
    uint256 amount,
    uint64 validAfter,
    uint64 deadline,
    uint256 nonce
  ) public view returns (bytes32) {
    bytes32 structHash = keccak256(
      abi.encode(
        INTENT_TYPEHASH,
        intentId,
        agentId,
        intentType,
        policyId,
        refId,
        amount,
        validAfter,
        deadline,
        nonce
      )
    );
    return _hashTypedDataV4(structHash);
  }

  function verifyAndConsume(
    address signer,
    bytes32 digest,
    uint64 validAfter,
    uint64 deadline,
    uint256 nonce,
    bytes calldata signature
  ) external {
    require(block.timestamp >= validAfter, "INTENT_NOT_YET_VALID");
    require(block.timestamp <= deadline, "INTENT_EXPIRED");
    require(!usedNonce[signer][nonce], "NONCE_USED");

    address recovered = digest.recover(signature);
    require(recovered == signer, "BAD_SIGNATURE");

    usedNonce[signer][nonce] = true;
  }
}
```

---

## 44) ACTFHub patch — **signed create**
We add a **signed createIntent** entrypoint.

### 44.1 New storage
```solidity
IntentVerifier public verifier;
```

### 44.2 Constructor add
```solidity
verifier = IntentVerifier(intentVerifierAddr);
```

---

## 45) `createIntentSigned()` (NEW)
```solidity
function createIntentSigned(
  bytes32 intentId,
  ACTFTypes.IntentType intentType,
  bytes32 agentId,
  bytes32 policyId,
  bytes32 refId,
  uint256 amount,
  uint64 validAfter,
  uint64 deadline,
  uint256 nonce,
  bytes calldata signature
) external {
  incidents.requireNotFrozen();

  AgentRegistry.Agent memory a = agents.agents(agentId);
  require(a.active, "AGENT_INACTIVE");

  bytes32 digest = verifier.hashIntent(
    intentId,
    agentId,
    uint8(intentType),
    policyId,
    refId,
    amount,
    validAfter,
    deadline,
    nonce
  );

  verifier.verifyAndConsume(
    a.operator,
    digest,
    validAfter,
    deadline,
    nonce,
    signature
  );

  intents[intentId] = ACTFTypes.Intent(
    intentId,
    intentType,
    agentId,
    policyId,
    refId,
    amount,
    uint64(block.timestamp),
    ACTFTypes.Status.CREATED
  );

  emit IntentCreated(intentId, intentType, agentId, refId, amount);
  emit IntentStatus(intentId, ACTFTypes.Status.CREATED);

  evidence.emitEvidence(
    keccak256("ACTF.INTENT.SIGNED"),
    intentId,
    amount,
    policyId
  );
}
```

**Result:**  
Agents author intents; ACTF enforces everything else.

# PART B — SIMULATION / PREFLIGHT (WHY INTENT WOULD FAIL)

## 46) Why simulation matters
Enterprises require:
- “why was this blocked?”
- preflight risk checks
- UX clarity for humans + agents

---

## 47) `simulateIntent()` (pure view)
```solidity
function simulateIntent(
  bytes32 agentId,
  bytes32 policyId,
  uint256 amount
) external view returns (bool ok, bytes32 reason) {
  if (!agents.agents(agentId).active) return (false, keccak256("AGENT_INACTIVE"));
  PolicyRouter.Policy memory p = policies.policies(policyId);
  if (!p.active) return (false, keccak256("POLICY_INACTIVE"));
  if (amount > p.maxAmount) return (false, keccak256("AMOUNT_EXCEEDS_POLICY"));
  return (true, bytes32(0));
}
```

**This function alone** differentiates ACTF from “demo contracts”.

# PART J — BATCH INTENTS (THOUSANDS OF SETTLEMENTS SAFELY)

## 66) Why “batch” is dangerous on-chain
If you loop over N settlements in one tx, you risk:
- out-of-gas
- partial completion ambiguity
- DoS vectors
- unpredictable costs

**Enterprise rule:**  
✅ Batch is **declared** on-chain  
✅ Items are **executed individually** (or in small bounded chunks)  
✅ Evidence & accounting is recorded per item  
✅ Completion is tracked deterministically

---

## 67) Batch model (locked)
A batch is:
- a governance-approved plan
- containing a list of receiptIds or settlement items
- executed across multiple txs

**BatchIntent** is **an IntentType** with its own lifecycle.

---

## 68) `BatchRegistry.sol`
`contracts/actf/BatchRegistry.sol`
```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "@openzeppelin/contracts/access/AccessControl.sol";

contract BatchRegistry is AccessControl {
  bytes32 public constant BATCH_ADMIN_ROLE = keccak256("BATCH_ADMIN_ROLE");

  enum Status { NONE, CREATED, ACTIVE, COMPLETE, CANCELLED }

  struct Batch {
    bytes32 batchId;
    uint32 totalItems;
    uint32 completedItems;
    Status status;
    bytes32 meta; // hash for off-chain manifest (IPFS)
  }

  mapping(bytes32 => Batch) public batches;
  mapping(bytes32 => mapping(uint32 => bytes32)) public item; // batchId -> index -> itemId (receiptId/intentId)

  event BatchCreated(bytes32 indexed batchId, uint32 totalItems, bytes32 meta);
  event BatchActivated(bytes32 indexed batchId);
  event BatchItemCompleted(bytes32 indexed batchId, uint32 index, bytes32 itemId);
  event BatchCompleted(bytes32 indexed batchId);
  event BatchCancelled(bytes32 indexed batchId);

  constructor(address admin) {
    _grantRole(DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE, admin);
    _grantRole(BATCH_ADMIN_ROLE, admin);
  }

  function createBatch(bytes32 batchId, uint32 totalItems, bytes32 meta)
    external onlyRole(BATCH_ADMIN_ROLE)
  {
    require(batches[batchId].status == Status.NONE, "BATCH_EXISTS");
    batches[batchId] = Batch(batchId, totalItems, 0, Status.CREATED, meta);
    emit BatchCreated(batchId, totalItems, meta);
  }

  function setItem(bytes32 batchId, uint32 index, bytes32 itemId)
    external onlyRole(BATCH_ADMIN_ROLE)
  {
    require(batches[batchId].status == Status.CREATED, "NOT_CREATING");
    require(index < batches[batchId].totalItems, "OOB");
    item[batchId][index] = itemId;
  }

  function activate(bytes32 batchId) external onlyRole(BATCH_ADMIN_ROLE) {
    Batch storage b = batches[batchId];
    require(b.status == Status.CREATED, "BAD_STATUS");
    b.status = Status.ACTIVE;
    emit BatchActivated(batchId);
  }

  function markCompleted(bytes32 batchId, uint32 index) external onlyRole(BATCH_ADMIN_ROLE) {
    Batch storage b = batches[batchId];
    require(b.status == Status.ACTIVE, "NOT_ACTIVE");
    bytes32 it = item[batchId][index];
    require(it != bytes32(0), "NO_ITEM");
    b.completedItems += 1;
    emit BatchItemCompleted(batchId, index, it);
    if (b.completedItems == b.totalItems) {
      b.status = Status.COMPLETE;
      emit BatchCompleted(batchId);
    }
  }

  function cancel(bytes32 batchId) external onlyRole(BATCH_ADMIN_ROLE) {
    batches[batchId].status = Status.CANCELLED;
    emit BatchCancelled(batchId);
  }
}
```

---

## 69) How batch is executed (ACTF pattern)
Batch execution is done via:
- one Intent: `BATCH_SETTLE(batchId)`
- then N item executions: `releaseEscrow(receiptId)` or settlement calls

This yields a safe enterprise method.

---

# PART K — BATCH EXECUTOR (BOUNDED GAS)

## 70) Bounded “chunk execution” (optional)
Instead of single items, we can execute **chunks** of size K with strict limit.

**Enterprise safe default:** item-by-item  
**Optional:** chunked with max K=10 or 20.

We implement item-by-item here for determinism.

---

## 71) `BatchExecutor.sol` (ACTF integrated)
`contracts/actf/BatchExecutor.sol`
```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.20;

import "@openzeppelin/contracts/access/AccessControl.sol";
import "./BatchRegistry.sol";
import "../IncidentController.sol";
import "./EvidenceLog.sol";
import "./interfaces/ICPIAdapter.sol";

contract BatchExecutor is AccessControl {
  bytes32 public constant EXEC_ROLE = keccak256("EXEC_ROLE");
  bytes32 public constant OPS_ROLE  = keccak256("OPS_ROLE");

  BatchRegistry public batches;
  IncidentController public incidents;
  EvidenceLog public evidence;
  ICPIAdapter public cpi;

  constructor(address admin, address batchRegistry, address incidentController, address evidenceLog, address cpiAdapter) {
    _grantRole(DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE, admin);
    _grantRole(EXEC_ROLE, admin);
    _grantRole(OPS_ROLE, admin);
    batches = BatchRegistry(batchRegistry);
    incidents = IncidentController(incidentController);
    evidence = EvidenceLog(evidenceLog);
    cpi = ICPIAdapter(cpiAdapter);
  }

  function executeItem(bytes32 batchId, uint32 index) external onlyRole(EXEC_ROLE) {
    incidents.requireNotFrozen();

    BatchRegistry.Batch memory b = batches.batches(batchId);
    require(b.status == BatchRegistry.Status.ACTIVE, "BATCH_NOT_ACTIVE");

    bytes32 receiptId = batches.item(batchId, index);
    require(receiptId != bytes32(0), "NO_ITEM");

    // Execute settlement item (release escrow)
    cpi.releaseEscrow(receiptId);

    evidence.emitEvidence(keccak256("ACTF.BATCH.ITEM.EXECUTED"), receiptId, 0, batchId);

    // mark complete (ops role might be executor; simplest: grant BATCH_ADMIN_ROLE to this contract)
    // In hackathon: we let this contract be a BATCH_ADMIN_ROLE holder.
    batches.markCompleted(batchId, index);
  }
}
```

**Enterprise note:**  
Grant `BATCH_ADMIN_ROLE` to BatchExecutor so it can `markCompleted`.

# PART L — PERFORMANCE / GAS BUDGETS (ENTERPRISE)

## 72) Gas budget targets (realistic)
| Action | Target |
|---|---|
| create signed intent | < 150k |
| validate | < 80k |
| approve | < 80k |
| execute single checkout | depends on CPI path |
| release escrow | < 150k |
| batch item settlement | < 200k |

---

## 73) Storage budget rules
- store only hashes on-chain (`metaHash`, `manifestHash`)
- avoid arrays in storage for large sets (use mapping index)
- avoid loops over dynamic arrays in settlement logic

---

## 74) Deterministic completion
Batch completion is:
- `completedItems == totalItems`
- every completed item emits evidence
- auditors can reproduce completion state

---

# PART M — FORMAL VERIFICATION TARGETS (ENTERPRISE)

## 75) What to formally verify (top 6)
1) **Nonce replay protection** in `IntentVerifier`  
2) **Status transitions** in `ACTFHub` (no illegal jumps)  
3) **Freeze** blocks create/validate/approve/execute  
4) **Approval gating** by tier (tier mapping correct)  
5) **Batch completion** correctness (no overcount)  
6) **No settlement without ACTIVE batch**  

---

## 76) How to do it (tool-agnostic)
- Scribble invariants OR
- Echidna fuzz tests OR
- Foundry invariant tests (even if Hardhat used for main)

Hackathon-friendly: add Hardhat fuzz-ish tests with random nonces and replays.

---

# PART N — ACTF HARDENING: SECURITY CONTROLS

## 77) Threat model
| Threat | Control |
|---|---|
| Agent key compromised | disable agent + tier caps + freeze |
| Relayer malicious | signature prevents forgery |
| Replay attacks | nonce consumed |
| Policy tampering | AccessControl + audit events |
| DoS via batch | item-by-item execution |
| Oracle manipulation | avoid price oracles in v1 |

---

## 78) “Blast radius” math (explicit)
If an agent is Tier2 with maxAmount = £100 equivalent/day:
- worst-case = £100/day
- escrow requirement may delay settlement
- freeze stops further loss

This is the enterprise story.

# PART O — FULL AUTOMATION TESTS (ACTF + BATCH)

## 79) `test/p5_signed_intents_replay.spec.ts`
```ts
import { expect } from "chai";
import { ethers } from "hardhat";

function b32(s: string) { return ethers.keccak256(ethers.toUtf8Bytes(s)); }

describe("ACTF Signed intents replay protection", function () {
  it("T5-30 replay fails after nonce consumed", async () => {
    const [admin, agent] = await ethers.getSigners();

    const IntentVerifier = await ethers.getContractFactory("IntentVerifier");
    const verifier = await IntentVerifier.deploy();
    await verifier.waitForDeployment();

    const digest = await verifier.hashIntent(
      b32("I1"),
      b32("AG1"),
      1,
      b32("POL1"),
      b32("REF1"),
      ethers.parseEther("1"),
      0,
      9999999999,
      1
    );

    const chainId = (await ethers.provider.getNetwork()).chainId;
    const sig = await agent.signTypedData(
      { name: "ACTF", version: "1", chainId, verifyingContract: await verifier.getAddress() },
      {
        Intent: [
          { name:"intentId", type:"bytes32" },
          { name:"agentId", type:"bytes32" },
          { name:"intentType", type:"uint8" },
          { name:"policyId", type:"bytes32" },
          { name:"refId", type:"bytes32" },
          { name:"amount", type:"uint256" },
          { name:"validAfter", type:"uint64" },
          { name:"deadline", type:"uint64" },
          { name:"nonce", type:"uint256" },
        ]
      },
      {
        intentId: b32("I1"),
        agentId: b32("AG1"),
        intentType: 1,
        policyId: b32("POL1"),
        refId: b32("REF1"),
        amount: ethers.parseEther("1"),
        validAfter: 0,
        deadline: 9999999999,
        nonce: 1
      }
    );

    // consume once
    await verifier.verifyAndConsume(agent.address, digest, 0, 9999999999, 1, sig);

    // replay should fail
    await expect(verifier.verifyAndConsume(agent.address, digest, 0, 9999999999, 1, sig))
      .to.be.revertedWith("NONCE_USED");
  });
});
```

## 80) Batch lifecycle test (registry + executor)
`test/p5_batch_registry_executor.spec.ts`
```ts
import { expect } from "chai";
import { ethers } from "hardhat";

function b32(s: string) { return ethers.keccak256(ethers.toUtf8Bytes(s)); }

describe("ACTF Batch settlement", function () {
  it("T5-40 batch completes after all items executed", async () => {
    const [admin] = await ethers.getSigners();

    const BatchRegistry = await ethers.getContractFactory("BatchRegistry");
    const batches = await BatchRegistry.deploy(admin.address);
    await batches.waitForDeployment();

    // mock CPI adapter that just emits release
    const CPIAdapterMock = await ethers.getContractFactory("CPIAdapterMock");
    const cpi = await CPIAdapterMock.deploy();
    await cpi.waitForDeployment();

    const IncidentController = await ethers.getContractFactory("IncidentController");
    const incidents = await IncidentController.deploy(admin.address);
    await incidents.waitForDeployment();

    const EvidenceLog = await ethers.getContractFactory("EvidenceLog");
    const evidence = await EvidenceLog.deploy(admin.address);
    await evidence.waitForDeployment();

    const BatchExecutor = await ethers.getContractFactory("BatchExecutor");
    const exec = await BatchExecutor.deploy(
      admin.address,
      await batches.getAddress(),
      await incidents.getAddress(),
      await evidence.getAddress(),
      await cpi.getAddress()
    );
    await exec.waitForDeployment();

    // Grant BatchExecutor the BATCH_ADMIN_ROLE so it can markCompleted
    const BATCH_ADMIN_ROLE = b32("BATCH_ADMIN_ROLE");
    await batches.grantRole(BATCH_ADMIN_ROLE, await exec.getAddress());

    const batchId = b32("B1");
    await batches.createBatch(batchId, 2, b32("manifest"));
    await batches.setItem(batchId, 0, b32("R1"));
    await batches.setItem(batchId, 1, b32("R2"));
    await batches.activate(batchId);

    await exec.executeItem(batchId, 0);
    let b = await batches.batches(batchId);
    expect(b.completedItems).to.equal(1);

    await exec.executeItem(batchId, 1);
    b = await batches.batches(batchId);
    expect(b.completedItems).to.equal(2);
    expect(b.status).to.equal(3); // COMPLETE
  });
});
```

```ts
import { ethers } from "hardhat";

function b32(s: string) { return ethers.keccak256(ethers.toUtf8Bytes(s)); }

async function main() {
  const [admin] = await ethers.getSigners();

  // Replace with real MNEE address on Ethereum if needed
  const token = process.env.MNEE_TOKEN!;
  const incidents = process.env.INCIDENTS!;

  const CreatorRegistry = await ethers.getContractFactory("CreatorRegistry");
  const creators = await CreatorRegistry.deploy(admin.address);
  await creators.waitForDeployment();

  const Entitlement1155 = await ethers.getContractFactory("Entitlement1155");
  const ent = await Entitlement1155.deploy(admin.address);
  await ent.waitForDeployment();

  const SplitRegistry = await ethers.getContractFactory("SplitRegistry");
  const splits = await SplitRegistry.deploy(admin.address);
  await splits.waitForDeployment();

  const ProductCatalog = await ethers.getContractFactory("ProductCatalog");
  const catalog = await ProductCatalog.deploy(admin.address);
  await catalog.waitForDeployment();

  const CheckoutRouterV2 = await ethers.getContractFactory("CheckoutRouterV2");
  const router = await CheckoutRouterV2.deploy(
    admin.address,
    token,
    await creators.getAddress(),
    await catalog.getAddress(),
    await ent.getAddress(),
    await splits.getAddress(),
    incidents
  );
  await router.waitForDeployment();

  const MINTER_ROLE = b32("MINTER_ROLE");
  await ent.grantRole(MINTER_ROLE, await router.getAddress());

  const SubscriptionRegistry = await ethers.getContractFactory("SubscriptionRegistry");
  const subReg = await SubscriptionRegistry.deploy(admin.address);
  await subReg.waitForDeployment();

  const BillingAgent = await ethers.getContractFactory("BillingAgent");
  const billing = await BillingAgent.deploy(admin.address, await subReg.getAddress(), await router.getAddress(), await catalog.getAddress());
  await billing.waitForDeployment();

  console.log("CreatorRegistry:", await creators.getAddress());
  console.log("Entitlement1155:", await ent.getAddress());
  console.log("SplitRegistry:", await splits.getAddress());
  console.log("ProductCatalog:", await catalog.getAddress());
  console.log("CheckoutRouterV2:", await router.getAddress());
  console.log("SubscriptionRegistry:", await subReg.getAddress());
  console.log("BillingAgent:", await billing.getAddress());
}

main().catch((e) => { console.error(e); process.exit(1); });
```

## 239) `scripts/30_wire_adapters.ts` (REAL wiring)
```ts
import { ethers } from "hardhat";

async function main() {
  const [admin] = await ethers.getSigners();

  const hubAddr = process.env.ACTF_HUB!;
  const routerAddr = process.env.CPI_ROUTER!;
  const billingAddr = process.env.CPI_BILLING!;
  const atgAddr = process.env.ATG_ADAPTER!;   // or real Product-1 governor adapter
  const a2aAddr = process.env.A2A_ADAPTER!;
  const pftaAddr = process.env.PFTA_ADAPTER!;

  const CPIAdapter = await ethers.getContractFactory("CPIAdapter");
  const cpi = await CPIAdapter.deploy(routerAddr, billingAddr);
  await cpi.waitForDeployment();

  const ACTFHub = await ethers.getContractFactory("ACTFHub");
  const hub = ACTFHub.attach(hubAddr);

  await hub.setAdapters(await cpi.getAddress(), pftaAddr, atgAddr, a2aAddr);

  console.log("CPIAdapter:", await cpi.getAddress());
  console.log("Adapters wired into ACTFHub");
}

main().catch((e) => { console.error(e); process.exit(1); });
```

# PART U — ROLE GRANTS (ENTERPRISE ACCESS CONTROL)

## 240) Roles that must be explicitly granted
### CPI
- `OPS_ROLE` on CheckoutRouterV2 (refund authority)
- `MINTER_ROLE` on Entitlement1155 to CheckoutRouterV2
- `AGENT_ROLE` on BillingAgent (approved billing executors)

### ACTF
- `OPS_ROLE` on ACTFHub (create/validate/approve)
- `EXEC_ROLE` on ACTFHub (execute)
- `WRITER_ROLE` on EvidenceLog to ACTFHub and BatchExecutor
- `BATCH_ADMIN_ROLE` on BatchRegistry to BatchExecutor

## 242) Threat catalog (top 18)
| # | Threat | Likely? | Impact | Control |
|---|---|---:|---:|---|
| 1 | Agent key stolen | Med | High | Disable agent + tier caps + freeze |
| 2 | Relayer malicious | Med | Med | EIP-712 signatures |
| 3 | Replay intent | High | High | Nonce consume |
| 4 | Policy admin compromised | Low | High | Multi-sig admin + audit events |
| 5 | Router refund abuse | Med | Med | refund window + ops role |
| 6 | Split wallet replaced | Med | High | SplitRegistry immutable mapping |
| 7 | Batch DoS | High | Med | item-by-item execution |
| 8 | Escrow never released | Med | Med | executor runbook + automation |
| 9 | Overpayment | Med | Med | policy max + product price check |
|10 | Unauthorized mint entitlement | Low | High | MINTER_ROLE strict |
|11 | Reentrancy | Med | High | ReentrancyGuard + SafeERC20 |
|12 | ERC20 non-standard | Med | Med | SafeERC20 |
|13 | Timestamp manipulation | Low | Med | window tolerance + deadlines |
|14 | Event spoofing claims | Med | Med | on-chain state + events |
|15 | Chain congestion | High | Med | batch queue + retry logic |
|16 | Malicious creator metadata | Med | Low | hash only on-chain |
|17 | Approval bribery | Low | High | tier thresholds + audit |
|18 | Privileged insider | Med | High | role segmentation + logs |

---

# PART W — INCIDENT ESCALATION RUNBOOK (RISK TIERS)

## 243) Tiered response ladders
### Tier-1 (minor)
- disable affected agent
- notify ops
- keep system running

### Tier-2 (material)
- freeze system
- investigate last 50 intents
- resume with new policy

### Tier-3 (critical)
- freeze
- revoke relayers/exec roles
- rotate admin keys
- publish incident note (for hackathon: show transparency)

---

## 244) Unwinding rules (no chaos)
- If escrowed: refund window still works
- If settled: issue new compensating payment (ledger records reversal)

This is exactly how enterprises operate.

---

# PART X — GAS PROFILING CHECKLIST (HARDHAT)

## 245) Why you need it
Judges don’t require perfect gas, but they reward:
- awareness
- safeguards
- stable patterns

6. Measure `BatchExecutor.executeItem`

- move heavy loops off-chain

describe("ACTF freeze blocks operations", function () {

# P5-161) ACTF GOVERNANCE LAYER (ENTERPRISE REALITY)

ACTF is not only technical — it is **organizational governance encoded**.

Governance answers:
- *Who is allowed to let agents spend?*
- *Who can override automation?*
- *Who is accountable when money moves incorrectly?*

---

## P5-162) Governance roles (non-overlapping)

| Role | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| Treasury Admin | Budget ceilings, freezes |
| Policy Admin | Policy creation & updates |
| Ops Executor | Intent validation/execution |
| Approver | Human sign-off (ATG) |
| Auditor | Read-only evidence access |
| Emergency Admin | Kill-switch authority |

**Key rule:**  
❌ No single role can move funds end-to-end.

---

## P5-163) Governance enforcement (on-chain)

- `AccessControl` separation
- Explicit role grants
- Evidence emitted on **every privileged action**

Example:
```solidity
event GovernanceAction(bytes32 role, address actor, bytes32 action, bytes32 ref);
```

This is how auditors reconstruct **human accountability**.

---

# P5-164) COMPLIANCE ALIGNMENT (WHY ENTERPRISES CARE)

ACTF aligns with real frameworks:

| Framework | Mapping |
|---|---|
| SOX | Approval + audit trail |
| ISO 27001 | Access control + logging |
| SOC 2 | Change tracking + segregation |
| Internal Audit | EvidenceLog |

Judges **will recognize this language**.

---

# P5-165) EVIDENCE AS A FIRST-CLASS OBJECT

ACTF treats evidence like money.

Every step emits:
- deterministic code (`bytes32`)
- refId
- actor
- timestamp
- amount

This allows:
- forensic replay
- dispute resolution
- regulator review

---

## P5-166) Evidence taxonomy (final)

| Code | Meaning |
|---|---|
| ACTF.INTENT.SIGNED | Agent authored |
| ACTF.INTENT.VALIDATED | Policy passed |
| ACTF.INTENT.APPROVED | Human gate |
| ACTF.INTENT.EXECUTED | Funds moved |
| ACTF.INTENT.SETTLED | Final state |
| ACTF.BATCH.ITEM.EXECUTED | Batch unit |
| ACTF.INCIDENT.FREEZE | Kill switch |

---

# P5-167) OPERATIONAL MODES (ENTERPRISE)

ACTF supports **explicit modes**:

| Mode | Description |
|---|---|
| AUTO | Low-risk agents, auto execution |
| HYBRID | Agent + human approval |
| MANUAL | Humans only |
| FROZEN | No execution |

Mode is derived from:
- agent tier
- policy
- incident state

---

# P5-168) Mode resolution logic

```text
if incident == FROZEN → FROZEN
else if agent.tier >= 3 → HYBRID
else → AUTO
```

This logic is simple **by design** (audit-safe).

---

# P5-169) FAIL-SAFE PRINCIPLE

When anything is unclear:
> **System fails closed, not open**

Examples:
- Missing approval → block
- Expired intent → block
- Unknown adapter → block

---

# P5-170) DISPUTE & REVERSAL MODEL

Enterprise truth:
- Blockchain is immutable
- Business requires reversals

ACTF supports **compensating transactions**, not deletes.

---

## P5-171) Reversal pattern

1. Original intent remains immutable
2. New intent created: `REVERSAL`
3. Ledger records offset entry
4. Evidence links both intents

This is **how banks work**.

---

# P5-172) DISPUTE FLOW (Commerce)

1. Customer raises dispute
2. CPI escrow already holds funds
3. DisputeEscrow adjudicates
4. ACTF records outcome
5. Settlement or refund executed

ACTF never shortcuts this.

---

# P5-173) SLA & TIMEOUT ENFORCEMENT

ACTF enforces:
- approval deadlines
- escrow windows
- batch expiry

Expired intents are:
- marked `REJECTED`
- cannot be revived

---

# P5-174) AUTOMATION SAFETY BOUNDARIES

ACTF explicitly forbids:
- recursive intent creation
- agent self-approval
- agent policy mutation

These are **hard-coded guardrails**.

---

# P5-175) AGENT BEHAVIOR CONSTRAINTS

Agents:
- can request
- cannot approve
- cannot execute directly
- cannot bypass policy

They are **participants, not governors**.

---

# P5-176) WHY THIS MATTERS TO JUDGES

Most hackathon projects:
- show “AI pays”
- ignore control failures

ACTF shows:
> “AI pays **safely**, **accountably**, **recoverably**”

That is the differentiator.

---

# P5-177) PERFORMANCE UNDER LOAD (ENTERPRISE VIEW)

ACTF is designed for:
- thousands of intents/day
- hundreds of settlements/hour
- bounded gas per action

No hot loops. No unbounded arrays.

---

# P5-178) SCALING STRATEGY (L2 / OFF-CHAIN)

ACTF is chain-agnostic:
- deploy on L2 (Base / Arbitrum)
- keep settlement atomic
- keep evidence on-chain

---

# P5-179) WHAT IS *NOT* IN SCOPE (ON PURPOSE)

ACTF deliberately excludes:
- price oracles
- credit scoring
- ML decision logic

Why?
> Determinism beats “smart” for money.

# P5-185) `scripts/40_seed_demo_data.ts`
```ts
import { ethers } from "hardhat";

function b32(s: string) {
  return ethers.keccak256(ethers.toUtf8Bytes(s));
}

async function main() {
  const [admin, agentEOA, buyer] = await ethers.getSigners();

  // Attach contracts
  const ACTFHub = await ethers.getContractFactory("ACTFHub");
  const hub = ACTFHub.attach(process.env.ACTF_HUB!);

  const AgentRegistry = await ethers.getContractFactory("AgentRegistry");
  const agents = AgentRegistry.attach(process.env.AGENT_REGISTRY!);

  const PolicyRouter = await ethers.getContractFactory("PolicyRouter");
  const policies = PolicyRouter.attach(process.env.POLICY_ROUTER!);

  const CreatorRegistry = await ethers.getContractFactory("CreatorRegistry");
  const creators = CreatorRegistry.attach(process.env.CREATOR_REGISTRY!);

  const ProductCatalog = await ethers.getContractFactory("ProductCatalog");
  const catalog = ProductCatalog.attach(process.env.PRODUCT_CATALOG!);

  const SplitRegistry = await ethers.getContractFactory("SplitRegistry");
  const splits = SplitRegistry.attach(process.env.SPLIT_REGISTRY!);

  // 1) Creator
  const creatorId = b32("CREATOR.ALPHA");
  await creators.register(creatorId, admin.address);

  // 2) Split (90% creator / 10% platform)
  const splitId = b32("SPLIT.ALPHA");
  await splits.createSplit(
    splitId,
    [admin.address, buyer.address],
    [90, 10]
  );

  // 3) Product
  const productId = b32("PROD.API.ALPHA");
  await catalog.createProduct(
    productId,
    creatorId,
    ethers.parseEther("5"),
    splitId,
    true
  );

  // 4) Agent
  const agentId = b32("AGENT.BUYER");
  await agents.registerAgent(
    agentId,
    agentEOA.address,
    b32("ROLE.BUYER"),
    2 // Tier-2
  );

  // 5) Policy
  const policyId = b32("POLICY.SMALL");
  await policies.setPolicy(
    policyId,
    ethers.parseEther("10"),
    false,
    true
  );

  console.log("Demo data seeded");
}

main().catch((e) => {
  console.error(e);
  process.exit(1);
});
```

> ACTF is an enterprise-grade programmable money fabric built on MNEE that enables AI agents, commerce platforms, and treasuries to coordinate value flows safely.
>
> All economic actions are expressed as signed intents, validated against policies, gated by approvals, and executed through auditable adapters for commerce, treasury, and agent-to-agent payments.
>
> The system provides deterministic governance, blast-radius containment, batch settlement, and regulator-grade evidence — solving real coordination problems in autonomous finance.
