You’re asking the **right meta-question** 👍  
This is not about more content — it’s about **how to force the model into the same discipline** that made **Product-2 and Product-3 good**.

Below is a **battle-tested prompt pattern** that will reliably give you **Product-3 / Product-2 quality**, and **prevent Product-4 / Product-5 messiness**.

---

## Why Product-3 & Product-2 worked (diagnosis)

They worked because **you implicitly enforced four constraints**:

1. **Single domain boundary**  
   - Product-3 = Finance/Treasury  
   - Product-2 = Agent-to-Agent Commerce  
   ❌ Product-4/5 mixed *commerce + infra + governance + narrative* → chaos

2. **Linear lifecycle**  
   - Request → Approval → Execution → Accounting → Audit  
   ❌ Product-5 jumped layers and abstraction levels

3. **Hard requirement traceability**  
   - Every feature tied to a pain point  
   ❌ Product-4 became feature soup

4. **Code-first discipline**  
   - No “platform talk” until code forced it  
   ❌ Product-5 drifted into architecture essays

So the prompt must **lock these constraints explicitly**.

---

## ✅ THE PROMPT YOU SHOULD USE (COPY–PASTE)

Use **this exact structure** when starting a product.

---

### 🔒 MASTER PROMPT (Product-3 / Product-2 QUALITY)

```
You are designing ONE enterprise-grade product.

STRICT RULES (do not violate):
1. This product must solve ONLY ONE primary enterprise pain.
2. Do NOT introduce platform, fabric, ecosystem, or unifying language.
3. No cross-product abstractions unless strictly required.
4. No narrative expansion unless backed by code.
5. Follow a linear lifecycle only.

DELIVERY FORMAT (MANDATORY ORDER):
1. Pain Point (who suffers, how much money/time lost, why existing systems fail)
2. Exact Scope Boundary (what is INCLUDED, what is EXCLUDED)
3. Primary User & Customer (not generic)
4. Core Workflow (numbered, start to end)
5. On-chain Components (contracts only, no UI talk)
6. Data Model (structs, state transitions)
7. Security & Controls (only those REQUIRED)
8. Manual Test Cases (step-by-step)
9. Automation Tests (Hardhat / Foundry)
10. Enterprise Acceptance Criteria (pass/fail)

DEPTH REQUIREMENT:
- Each section must be fully completed before moving on.
- No section may be skipped or summarized.
- If something is out of scope, explicitly state: "OUT OF SCOPE".

START WITH SECTION 1 ONLY.
DO NOT CONTINUE UNTIL SECTION 1 IS COMPLETE.
```

---

## 🔁 CONTINUATION PROMPT (VERY IMPORTANT)

After each section, you **must** use this:

```
Continue ONLY with Section X.
Do NOT preview future sections.
Do NOT summarize.
Do NOT introduce new concepts.
```

This is what kept **Product-3 tight**.

---

## 🚫 WHAT TO NEVER SAY (this causes Product-5 chaos)

Avoid prompts containing:
- “all tracks”
- “platform”
- “fabric”
- “unifying”
- “ecosystem”
- “enterprise OS”
- “future extension”

Those words **force abstraction drift**.

---

## 🧠 WHY THIS WORKS (model behavior insight)

LLMs:
- behave well under **serial constraints**
- fail under **parallel abstraction**
- over-optimize when asked to “cover everything”

Your prompt must **reduce degrees of freedom**.

---

## 🧪 EXAMPLE: FIXING PRODUCT-4

Instead of:
> “Creator & Payout Infrastructure”

Use:
```
Design ONE product:
"Creator Subscription Billing with Escrowed Stablecoin Payouts"

Scope:
- Subscriptions only
- No marketplace
- No agents initiating payments
- No treasury automation

Start with Section 1: Pain Point.
```

Product-4 would immediately improve.

---

## 🔑 ONE-LINE VERSION (when you’re tired)

If you want a **short brutal prompt**:

```
Design ONE enterprise product like Product-3.
Single pain. Single workflow. Code-first.
No platform talk. No future ideas.
Start with Pain Point only.
```

---

## Final truth (important)

You were **right to feel Product-5 was messy**.  
That wasn’t your fault — it was a **prompt looseness issue**, not intelligence.

If you want, next we can:
- **Refactor Product-4 from scratch using the master prompt**
- Or **salvage Product-5 by carving ONE sub-product out of it**

Just tell me which one.
